## Flaws found in specs.md and in the code

Flaws 1 and 2, of `specs.md`, were real: each trace was replayed on the conformance build, and each had a black-box scenario in `flaws/` that failed (`make model-flaws`) until its remedy, the maintainer's to decide; the model's `Fixes` holds the one proposed for a flaw still open, so that the instances that expect `pass` look past it. Once a remedy is in `specs.md`, the model takes it whatever `Fixes` says, the flaw's name leaves `Fixes`, its instance expects `pass`, and its scenario moves to `../scenarios/`. Both are fixed now. Flaws 3 and 4 were of the code of Atoll's own view, which `specs.md` already said otherwise, and 5 to 7 of `specs.md`: the model took the code as it was unless `Fixes` named the remedy, and each had a test of the code behind the build tag `flaws` (Files, above), which failed until the remedy. The maintainer chose the remedies on 2026-11-22; with them in the code, the model's branch for the code went, the remedies' names left `Fixes`, the flaws' instances expect `pass`, and the tests lost the build tag `flaws`, regular tests of the writer and of `internal/jobs` now, with a black-box scenario for flaw 6. Flaws 8 to 12, found on 2026-11-22, each of `specs.md` and of the code as it said it (8 to 11 below, of the SSD cache's transfer and the link guard, and 12 in the section of `Copies.tla`), went the same way that day: each had a test of the code behind the build tag `flaws`, seen failing; the maintainer chose the remedies, now in `specs.md`, the code and the model, the model's branch for the code gone, and each test a regular one, seen failing before its remedy and passing after.

### Flaw 1: a write into a new replica before its row is written is recorded as verified

Fixed on 2026-11-19, with the remedy the maintainer approved (below): `specs.md` §14.2, `conformance/vectors/create_row.json`, `FlawReplicaEdited.cfg` expecting `pass`, and `../scenarios/replica-edited-before-commit.json`.

`specs.md` §14.2 commits a copy "with what a look at its new name finds", and its reprise, for a target `same` and a part `absent`, "finishes as above, with the hashes the job recorded". The look comes after the rename has put the copy in the library: whatever a user writes into it between the rename and the look is recorded as the copy's own state, `verified_ns` set against the hash of the version it was made from.

1. A file of version 1 on disk 1; the plan queues its replica on disk 2.
2. The operation `create` runs up to `source-synced`: the copy is at its name on disk 2, every sync done.
3. A user writes version 2 into it, in place (directly on disk 2, or through mergerfs, whose first branch may be disk 2).
4. The commit records the copy `ok`, verified, with the size and times of version 2, while the file's hash is version 1's (`ReplicaInSync` broken).
5. Every later look finds the copy as its row says: the edit is never propagated. A scrub then reads a mismatch with unchanged size and times, `corrupt`, and repairs the user's edit away from disk 1, the chunks it held in the trash (against G4); or a later change on disk 1 makes it stale and sends it to the trash.

A crash at `source-synced` makes the window as long as Atoll is down: the reprise records what is there when it runs. In the code: `internal/ops/create.go` (`PlacePart`, whose `movedFrom` looks after the syncs) and `internal/ops/recover.go` (`recoverCreate`, "what is there now is what gets recorded"), then `catalog.FinishCreate`. Reproduced by `FlawReplicaEdited.cfg` and `replica-edited-before-commit.json`, which failed until the remedy.

The remedy (`create-look`, approved by the maintainer on 2026-11-19): the row takes what the part was when it was verified, before the rename (inode, size, modification time), so that a write since reads as `edited` at the next look; the reprise likewise records the part's size and modification time from the job, never what it finds. As it landed: the job records with its whole-file hash what the part was then, its size, times, mode, owner and links (`part_look`), and the row takes it all, but for the change time, which the rename moved: that one comes from a look right after the rename, or in a reprise from its look, and only when the look found nothing else moved; otherwise the part's, which the next look finds moved, later than the row says, a modification and never a `conflict` (§15.4). In the model, whose gen stands for size, times and change time at once, and whose rename and the look after it are one step, the row takes gen 0. What it left: a write that keeps the size, the times, the mode and the owner moves only the change time, which a reprise could not tell from the rename's, and took; the model, where every edit moved the size, could not see it either.

**Its limit, closed** (the maintainer, 2026-11-20, "Les deux"; `specs.md` §14.2, `conformance/vectors/create_reprise.json`, `FlawReplicaRewritten.cfg`, and `../scenarios/replica-edited-keeping-dates-after-record.json` and `-before-record.json`). The model now counts apart what moves the change time alone (`cg`): the rename, the name in `tmp/` a reprise takes away, and a user's `rewrite`, a write that keeps the size and the dates. (b) Right after the rename and its look, the job records the change time that look found, in a commit of its own (`CopyRecord`, `placed`), and a reprise that finds another reads a write since: the row is the part's, and the next walk finds the copy `edited`. (a) A reprise that finds no record, the crash or the disk lost having come between the rename and that commit (`moved`), and a change time other than the part's when it was verified, records the copy unverified: the row's `doubt`, no column, which `ReplicaInSync` looks past and `DoubtUnverified` keeps unverified; its write is then for a read, which the model leaves out, to find a `conflict`. Each was taken out once to see TLC fail: without (b), a rename, its record, a rewrite, a crash and the reprise record the write as the copy (`ReplicaInSync`); without (a), the same with the disk lost before the record. The normal run's window stays: the look comes in the step of the rename, which here is the width of a `stat`, and on a disk without `RENAME_NOREPLACE` the look comes after the `unlink`, so that a rewrite between the link and that look is taken (What it leaves out).

### Flaw 2: a job running is dropped while its operation is still planned

Fixed on 2026-11-19, with the remedy the maintainer approved (below): `specs.md` §9, §13.4, §14.1 and §15.12, `conformance/vectors/plan.json` and `propagation.json` (`running`), `FlawJobDroppedGone.cfg` and `FlawJobDroppedPlan.cfg` expecting `pass`, and `../scenarios/replica-of-deleted-file.json` and `replica-of-old-version.json`.

`specs.md` §14.1 says that a job whose operation the reprise could not conclude "stays running, and does not run: what is at its target may be its own copy, and nothing is decided of it until that operation is". But two decisions drop such a job: the plan cancels a pending copy that no longer counts, "its row goes, with its job" (§13.4, §14.1), as one on a disk `missing` does; and the decision Gone deletes the file, with its jobs (§15.12, §9). Back, the disk's reprise finishes the operation with no job (§14.2), which `catalog.FinishCreate` records as an `ok` copy by Atoll, never verified, when the file still has a row, and not at all when it has none.

Through a deletion (`FlawJobDroppedGone.cfg`, `replica-of-deleted-file.json`):

1. A file of version 1 on disk 1; its replica's operation reaches `target-synced` on disk 2, which is then lost (`EIO`): the operation stays planned, the job running.
2. The user deletes the file's copy on disk 1.
3. A pass walks disk 1: gone. Disk 2 is missing, and its pending copy is no copy: the decision is Gone, and the file goes with its running job.
4. Disk 2 comes back. Its reprise finds the copy at its name and finishes the operation: no job, no file, nothing recorded.
5. Atoll's copy sits in the library unknown to the catalog (`Inv11_OwnOpsNotUsers` broken): the next walk records it as a user's new file, and the file the user deleted is back, with no trash.

Through the plan (`FlawJobDroppedPlan.cfg`, `replica-of-old-version.json`): the same lost disk; the plan cancels its pending copy and the running job; the user writes version 2 into the copy on disk 1, and the modification, which waits for a running job (§15.4), finds none and is decided; back, disk 2's reprise records its copy of version 1 as `ok`, by Atoll, at the file's level (`ReplicaInSync` broken): one real copy of version 2, where the catalog counts two, until a scrub reads it.

In the code: `internal/solver/plan.go` (`counts`: a pending copy on a disk `missing` is canceled), `catalog.cancelPending` (deletes the job whatever its state), the foreign key of `jobs` (`ON DELETE CASCADE`), and `internal/ops/recover.go` (`recoverCreate` with no job).

The remedy (`running-job`, approved by the maintainer on 2026-11-19): what §14.1 says, everywhere: a pending copy whose job is running is kept, and counts, as a copy on a disk not yet past `missing_grace` does; and a file with a job running waits for its decisions, as its modifications already do (§15.4), until the reprise concludes the operation. The other, a reprise that finds no job sending the copy to the trash, was not chosen. As it landed: the caller tells the plan which pending copies have their job running; such a copy counts as an `ok` copy on its disk would, so no longer past `missing_grace`, nor on a disk `draining`, `failed` or `retired`, holds no room, and is never canceled, by the level, the room, the file leaving or its disk; every decision of its file waits, Rename included, but not a file that only follows a folder renamed; and nothing else takes the file, the job or its pending copy away: a file whose last copy goes otherwise keeps that pending copy and its job (`catalog.dropFileIfEmpty`), as the model's `Emptied` does, and a modification keeps it too.

### Flaw 3: the view's reprise called a deletion done while its copy was still at its name

Fixed on 2026-11-22, with the remedy the maintainer chose, (a) below: `specs.md` §21.2, `ops.Disk.ResolveViewTrash` (the table of §17.2, the copy keeping its row for the pass), `writer.resolveTrash` for a deletion and a replacement, `TestTheRepriseOfALinkedDeletionRemovesTheCopysName` (`internal/view/writer/viewtrash_test.go`), which failed before, and `FlawViewRepriseLinked.cfg` expecting `pass`. What it was:

`specs.md` §21.2: "`atoll mount` resolves what it left `planned` at its start, before it mounts, by the table of §17.2 for what moves a name". The code (`writer.resolve`, `catalog.ViewUnlink`) calls a deletion done when the trash's name holds a file of the copy's inode (`w.trashed`), and undone otherwise, and does nothing on the disk: the source's name is never looked at nor removed, nothing is synced, and the inode alone decides, where §17.2 wants its size and modification time too.

1. A file at its level on disks 1 and 2, both without `RENAME_NOREPLACE`, as ntfs-3g and lowntfs-3g are; the view presents its copy on disk 1.
2. A user deletes it through the view: the operation `trash` and the row `unlink` journaled, the look, the `link` of the copy into the trash.
3. `atoll mount` is stopped there (kill -9, a crash, a reboot), between the `link` and the `unlink`.
4. At its next start, its reprise finds the trash's name holding the copy's inode: done, the row `unlink` and the operation `trash` with it. The copy is still at its name in the library, a second name of the same file now in the trash, which the table of §17.2 resolves (target `same`, source `same`) by removing the source's name.
5. From then on: the view presents nothing at the path, as it deleted the file, while the copy is at its name on its disk, where mergerfs would show it (break glass); a pass finds it there, unchanged, so the deletion never reaches the other copy; the catalog never presents what the view does, so the row `unlink` is never taken in, and holds back every later operation of the view at that path for good. The trash's entry is a hard link of a library file, which a purge later unlinks, the file staying.

The same happens on any disk when the view stops after the rename and before its syncs, then the power is cut once the reprise called it done: the rename was not durable, and the file is back at its name (§17.1, "An operation is never `done` on the strength of the move alone"). TLC's trace is the first: start, delete, look, link, stop, start, reprise; 158 distinct states. The test makes `renameat2` refuse its flag on the view's disk, stops the view at the crash point `linked`, starts it again and finds the copy at its name.

Remedies seen then: (a) what §21.2 says, the view's reprise of a row `unlink` (and of `replace`, which has the same shape in `resolveReplace`) resolved by `ops.Disk.ResolveNow` with its look in place of a row, as `ResolveTrashNow` does for a move's copy, names synced first and the table's actions taken (`view-reprise` in `Fixes`, which every instance that expects a pass takes); (b) the same, but leaving the row `planned` whenever the source still holds the copy, for a pass to see. (a) is what `specs.md` says already.

### Flaw 4: a deletion through the view gave up when the look after its move failed, and left a user's edit in the trash

Fixed on 2026-11-22, with the remedy the maintainer chose, (a) below: `specs.md` §21.2, `writer.afterFailedTrash`, called by a deletion and a replacement of the view as the daemon's session calls `ResolveNow`, `TestADeletionEditedAtTheMoveGoesBack`, which failed before, and `FlawViewEditedAtMove.cfg` expecting `pass`. What it was:

`specs.md` §17.1, step 4: "Afterwards the name at the target must hold the copy, compared as that look compares it, by its inode, its size and its modification time ... a file that took the source's name since the look ... or the copy edited then, is not the copy, and goes back, as §17.2 resolves it", and "Whatever stops a move before it is done ... is resolved at once by looking at both names, by the table of §17.2". The daemon does (`trash.Session.afterFailedMove`, `ResolveNow`). The view's deletion (`writer.toTrash`) does not: any error of `ops.Disk.ToTrash` but a disk lost goes to `finish`, which undoes the row and the operation, its trash row removed, and leaves both names as they are.

1. A file at its level on disks 1 and 2; the view presents its copy on disk 1.
2. A user deletes it through the view: journaled, then the look right before the move finds the copy as it was.
3. A user, or a program writing directly to disk 1, writes into the copy in place, in the moment between that look and the move.
4. The move takes the edited file into the trash; the look after it finds another size and time there, not the copy.
5. The view undoes the operation as it is: the edit, the latest version a user wrote, is in the trash, under an operation `undone`, with no trash row, so that `atoll trash` neither lists nor restores it, and nothing purges it; the library's path is empty, and the next pass finds the copy gone, as a deletion made directly on that disk (§15.5), which `deletes.direct = "propagate"` takes to the other copy. §17.2 (target `other`, source `absent`) puts the file back at its path, and the next pass finds it edited.

On a disk without `RENAME_NOREPLACE` the same comes of an edit between the `link` and the look at the source, when the source's name then goes by itself. TLC's trace: delete, look, a user's edit, move, the look after; 347 distinct states. The test edits the file from the fault hook of the move's `rename` and finds the edit in the trash, with no trash row, and nothing at the path.

Remedies: (a) what §17.1 says, `toTrash` and `replace` calling `ResolveNow` (with the look as the copy's row, `ResolveTrashNow`) after a failed move, as the daemon's session does, then finishing the row by what it concludes (`view-resolve` in `Fixes`); (b) the view's deletion leaving its row `planned` on such an error, for its reprise to resolve, once flaw 3's remedy is in. Either way the window is the width of a `rename`, and needs a user writing directly to the disk while the view deletes the file.

### Flaw 5: what the view left planned on a disk lost while it ran stayed planned until its next start

Fixed on 2026-11-22, with the remedy the maintainer chose, (a) below: `specs.md` §21.2, `writer.Recheck`, which `readonly.Serve` calls after its check every 30 s, the rows a part being written or closed and a move during its copy still hold left alone (`writer.hold`), `TestARecheckResolvesWhatADiskLostLeftPlanned` and `TestARecheckLeavesAPartBeingWritten`, and `ViewLiveLost.cfg`, the very instance that `FlawViewPlannedForGood.cfg` became once `Fixes` was emptied, which went. What it was:

1. No file at a path yet; a user makes one through the view on disk 2, a USB disk: the row `create` journaled.
2. Disk 2 is lost before the file is made there (a USB reset, `EIO`): the row stays `planned`, as §17.1 leaves what a disk that cannot be relied on stops.
3. Disk 2 comes back, as a USB disk does, the daemon remounting it (§10); `atoll mount` runs on, as it is made to, for months.
4. Nothing resolves the row: the view's reprise runs at its next start alone. Meanwhile every walk of disk 2 waits at that path, as at an operation left planned there (`viewPlannedOn`): what is at that path on disk 2, a copy Atoll makes there later included, is `unknown` to every pass, its edits and its deletion never seen; and no later operation of the view at that path is ever taken in, the one planned holding them back. A part dropped on a disk lost, or a deletion through the view stopped by one, stays the same way, its part in `tmp/` or its copy half moved.

Remedies: (a) the view's own mount check, once it finds a disk back, resolves what the view left planned there as its start would, under the writer's lock, but for a part it still writes or closes (`view-recheck` in `Fixes`, with which `ViewLiveLost.cfg` passes); (b) the daemon's reprise resolving the view's operations it finds planned while `atoll mount` does not run, which would need to know that it does not; (c) the view retrying a failed operation's resolution itself, as `dropPart` would retry its removal. (a) is the smallest, and matches what a start does.

### Flaw 8: the SSD cache's transfer purges its copy while the data disks no longer hold the file

Fixed on 2026-11-22, with the remedy the maintainer chose, (a) `xfer-look` below: `specs.md` §19, `trash.Session.Transfer`, which, the move done and before the purge, looks at each copy on a data disk that counts as §15.10 compares a known copy (`dataCopiesHold`), and when one is no longer as its row says, or cannot be read, purges nothing: the entry stays in the cache's trash, `surplus`, for its retention, the outcome `changed`, and the pass reads the file again at its next walk. Tests, each seen failing before: `TestATransferKeepsItsCopyWhenTheDataCopiesWentMeanwhile`, `…WhenADataCopyChangedMeanwhile` and `…WhenADataDiskFailsTheLook` (`internal/jobs/transferlook_test.go`), with `TestATransferKilledWhileItsDataCopiesGo`, kill -9 at each step of the transfer as the data copies go; `FlawTransferPurged.cfg` expects `pass`. What it was (495 states): §19 has a file leave the cache once "the copies on data disks that count reach its level, each `ok`, verified since it last changed": the catalog's word. The code plans the file alone from the catalog (`watch.CacheTransfers`), hands over its user part, then sends its copy on the cache to the trash, the move checked against that copy's row alone, and purges it at once (`jobs.transfer`, `trash.Session.Transfer`); nothing looked at the copies on the data disks.

1. A file on the cache disk 1 alone, a user copy, leaves the cache: the pass makes its copy on the data disk 2 (one replica wanted in the model; both data copies with two).
2. The same pass plans the file again: the copy on disk 2 is `ok` and verified, the transfer due; the user part is handed over and the move journaled.
3. A user deletes the copy on disk 2 directly (or writes into it in place), after the pass's walk and before the move.
4. The look right before the move finds the copy on the cache as its row says: it goes to the cache's trash, `surplus`, and is purged there.
5. The file is on no disk and in no trash, where a deletion made directly on one disk reaches the other copies only in their trash (invariant 10); or, for the edit, the version the catalog vouches for is gone (invariant 1), where the next pass would have sent the copy on the cache to its trash as `overwritten`, and kept it.

The window is the pass from its walk of the data disk to the purge, hours of copies at night, and needs a user or a program writing directly to a data disk: through Atoll's own view, the file is presented from the cache until the transfer's commit. TLC found the edit first, in `Cache.cfg`. `TestFlawATransferPurgesTheLastCopyOfAFileDeletedMeanwhile` and `TestFlawATransferPurgesTheVersionACopyOnADataDiskLostMeanwhile` (then `internal/jobs/flaws_test.go`, the deletion of both data copies from the fault hook of the cache's `rename`, and the edit of the one copy of a file of one replica) failed on the code; they are the first two above now.

Remedies, each checked against every invariant: (a) `xfer-look`: right before the purge, each copy on a data disk that counts is looked at as §15.10 compares a known copy, on a disk the run can read, and one that is not as its row says keeps the entry in the cache's trash, the purge undone, for its retention (`Cache.cfg` passes with it, 3,558,753 states, and the flaw's pool, 656); (b) `xfer-keep`: no purge at once, the entry staying in the cache's trash as `surplus`, the first that `trash.max_size` purges, which takes back the maintainer's choice of 2026-11-17 of the space back at once (3,336,142 states in `Cache.cfg`, 601 in the flaw's pool). (a) keeps that choice and costs a look at each data copy, on disks the pass has just written to.

### Flaw 9: the view presents a file from the cache while the transfer moves its copy there

Fixed on 2026-11-22, with the remedy the maintainer chose, (a) `view-fallback` below: `specs.md` §21.2, `view.Tree.Open`, which, when the copy it presents is not at its name, opens the next copy in sync in the order of §7, from the catalog alone (`nextCopies`); `TestTheViewOpensTheNextCopyWhileATransferMovesTheOriginal` (`internal/jobs/viewfallback_test.go`), seen failing before, and `FlawTransferUnseen.cfg` expecting `pass`. What it was (322 states, `ViewPresentsLatest`): the copy on the cache is a file's original (§19: "The copy on the cache is the older one"), which Atoll's own view presents and reads alone with `read.policy = "oldest"` (§7, `view.Tree.Open`).

1. A new file the view makes lands on the cache disk 1; a pass copies it to disk 2, and takes the view's operation in.
2. The next pass's transfer moves the copy on the cache to the trash, then syncs, then commits the removal of its row (§17.1).
3. Until that commit the catalog presents the file from disk 1, where nothing is at its path: an open through the view finds `ENOENT`, while disk 2 holds the file in sync (invariant 14).

Every file that leaves the cache goes through it, the width of a `rename` and three syncs, and as long as Atoll is down when a crash or a disk lost leaves the move planned. `TestFlawTheViewOpensNothingWhileATransferMovesTheOriginal` (then `internal/jobs/flaws_test.go`, an open through the view's tree from the crash point `moved` of the transfer) failed on the code.

Remedies: (a) `view-fallback`: the view's open, when the copy it presents is not at its name, opens the next copy that holds the file at its path in the order of §7, as `read.policy = "prefer-spinning"` already opens the first that opens; it serves `atoll prune` and any other move of a presented copy as well (`CacheView.cfg` passes with it, 174,150 states); (b) `xfer-hide`: the transaction that journals the transfer's move starts its copy's clock (`gone_ns`), so that the view presents the file from a data disk before the copy leaves its name, a move that does not happen stopping it again (180,435 states). Each passes the flaw's pool too, 383 states.

### Flaw 10: a write opened through the view during a transfer goes to the copy it purges

Fixed on 2026-11-22, with the remedy the maintainer chose, `view-held` below, all three parts: `specs.md` §9, §19, §21.2 ("Held open"); the state `open` of `view_ops`, a value added to its closed list by migration 0027, as format version 1 allows, with its catalog vectors; the transfer's move journaled only while no operation of the view no pass has taken in touches its path, read in the same transaction (`catalog.beginTrashTx`, `TrashStart.ViewFree`, `trash.OutcomeViewHolds`, the transfer then waiting a debounce); the view's open to write waiting, up to 10 s and then `EBUSY`, while a move of Atoll's own of the copy it presents is planned (`catalog.ErrMovePlanned`, checked in the transaction that journals the write or holds its row open again); and the write, or the new file, held `open` until its last handle closes, which no pass takes in, the view's start letting go of what a stopped view held (`writer.Reprise`). An open to write whose copy left its name since the kernel looked it up, as the transfer just done leaves it, opens what the view presents now, and one that cannot open its copy undoes the write it journaled (`TestAnOpenToWriteAfterTheTransferOpensWhatTheViewPresents`, which `TestAWriteOpenedThroughTheViewDuringATransferWaitsForIt` showed once in twenty runs when its open came late, and `TestAnOpenToWriteThatFailsUndoesItsWrite`); the model's open finds its copy at its name in the same step. Tests, each seen failing before: `TestAWriteOpenedThroughTheViewDuringATransferWaitsForIt`, `TestATransferWaitsForAWriteOpenedSinceThePassReadTheView` and `TestAFileTheViewHoldsOpenIsNeverTransferred` (`internal/jobs/viewheld_test.go`), and in the writer `TestAnOpenToWriteWaitsWhileAMoveOfItsCopyIsPlanned`, with `TestAFileIsHeldOpenUntilItsLastHandleCloses`, `TestAStoppedViewLetsGoAtItsStartOfWhatItHeldOpen` and `TestARowTheCatalogKeepsOpenIsLetGoOfAtTheNextStart` (`held_test.go`); `FlawTransferWritten.cfg` expects `pass`, with `ViewPresentsLatest` among its invariants again. What it was (2,534 states, `NoUserDataLost`; `ViewPresentsLatest`, which broke first, left out to show the loss): a pass read the view's operations once, before its transfers (`jobs.transfers`, `touchedByView`), and the view journaled a write when it opened a file, done at once, then wrote into the copy it presents (`writer.Open`, `journalWrite`).

1. As in flaw 9, a file on the cache disk 1, copied to disk 2.
2. A program opens it to write through the view after the pass read the view's operations, or kept it open since it made it, its operation taken in meanwhile, as one taken in while open is.
3. The look right before the move finds the copy on the cache as its row says, as nothing was written yet: it goes to the trash and is purged.
4. What the program writes then goes to a file no name holds, gone once it closes it (invariant 1), and the view presents the file from the cache, where nothing is, for good, its write never taken in (invariant 14).

`TestFlawAWriteOpenedThroughTheViewDuringATransferIsLost` (then `internal/jobs/flaws_test.go`, the open from the crash point `journaled` of the transfer) failed on the code: what the program wrote was on no disk.

Remedy (`view-held`, all three parts): the transfer reads the view's operations again in the transaction that journals its move; the view's open to write waits while a move of Atoll's own of the copy it presents is planned, and opens the copy the catalog presents once it is done; and an operation of the view that made or writes a file stays open, not taken in, while the view holds that file open to write, which the daemon learns from the catalog only, a state of `view_ops` that format version 1 lets be added. `CacheView.cfg` passes with it, and so does the flaw's pool (4,273 states).

### Flaw 11: the reprise of a link of the guard hides a write that kept the dates

Fixed on 2026-11-22, with the remedy the maintainer chose, (a) `link-keep` below: `specs.md` §17.2, `ops.recoverMakeLink` and `ops.recoverDropLink`, whose reprise ends the operation and leaves the copy's row as it recorded it; `TestAWriteKeepingTheDatesSurvivesTheRepriseOfALink`, seen failing before, and `TestTheRepriseOfALinkLeavesTheCopyInSync`, with nothing written, the next look reading a change of metadata alone (`internal/jobs/linkreprise_test.go`); `FlawLinkReprise.cfg` expects `pass`. What it was (1,832 states, `ReplicaInSync`): §17.2 said "`kind = link` finishes when the link is there with its inode, its file's names taking the change time they find now, as what it had before is gone with the crash" (`ops.recoverMakeLink`; `recoverDropLink` likewise for a link's removal).

1. A file replicated from disk 1 to disk 2, the link guard on; a pass journals the link of the copy on disk 2, with up to a thousand others (`catalog.BeginLinks`).
2. The link is made, which moves the copy's change time, and Atoll stops before the batch's commit (kill -9, a power cut once the folder is synced, or the disk lost at its sync).
3. Before the reprise, or between the journal and the link as TLC's trace has it, a program rewrites that copy keeping its size and its dates, as a tagger may: only its change time moves.
4. The reprise finds the link with its inode: done, and the copy's row takes the change time it finds, the write's with the link's: every look finds the copy as its row says, the write never reaches the other copy, and a scrub reads it as corruption and repairs it away with the copy on disk 1 (invariant 9, G4), as flaw 1 did of a copy's rename.

In a normal run the commit takes the change time only when the row recorded the one just before the link, so a write since reads as an edit. `TestFlawAWriteKeepingTheDatesIsHiddenByTheRepriseOfALink` (then `internal/jobs/flaws_test.go`, Atoll stopped at the crash point `linked`, the write while it is down) failed on the code; the same write after a link that committed reaches the other copy (a control, run once and not kept).

Remedies: (a) `link-keep`: the reprise leaves the rows' change time as they recorded it, so the next look finds it moved and reads the copy, a change of metadata alone when the content is the same, a read for each copy a stopped batch left so; such a copy changed together with another changed copy of its file before that read is a conflict of §15.4 rather than a modification (`GuardRewrite.cfg` passes with it, 451,650 states, and every instance of the guard takes it); (b) `link-doubt`: the reprise takes the change time it finds and records the copy unverified, as a copy's reprise with no record does (§14.2), so that a scrub reads a mismatch as a conflict, never a corruption (435,972 states).

## Modelling mistakes, found by TLC and fixed

TLC's first traces also showed where the model was wrong, not `specs.md`:

- a user deleting a fresh copy after Atoll synced it read as a commit before the syncs: the check now asks that a name still holding what Atoll put there be durable;
- a part in `tmp/` counted as a copy of the user's version, so a file deleted while its replica was being made read as lost by Atoll: only the library and the trash count (§17, Limits);
- Atoll kept writing to a disk lost and mounted again through the handles of the old mount, which answer `EIO` in reality (§10): a session now holds the mount it confirmed;
- a check of Atoll's own moves outlived a remount that put the copy back, then deleted by the user: it now looks where the file is;
- the liveness instance ran out of operation ids, after which nothing can be journaled: it has more.

On 2026-11-22, with the SSD cache's transfer and the link guard:

- what a user's action destroys was counted on durable names alone, so that a version the transfer had already moved to its trash, the source's name not yet synced, read as Atoll's to keep when the user then edited its last other copy: a version a user's action leaves only in an entry the transfer purges at once, or that the kernel no longer shows at another name, is now the user's to lose (`UserLost`), with the cache alone;
- a source copy a replica was verified against kept its doubt (`CreateFinish`): `DoubtUnverified` found it once a reprise of a link recorded copies in doubt (the remedy `link-doubt`, which the maintainer did not choose); being read against the file's hash takes the doubt off, which no earlier instance reached;
- the walk waited at the link guard's planned operations, which `jobs.plannedOn` leaves out, as they leave the root as it is;
- the liveness instances of the cache and of the guard ran out of operation ids, in one case on the churn of observation 2, below.

On 2026-11-23, with the cache and the guard together (`CacheGuard`, 2,258,996 states): `Inv2_LastUserCopyKept` asked that a file keep a user copy after a transfer even when it had none before it, as a stop right after a hand-over, then a user's deletion of the copy the hand-over had made the user's, Kept (the copy on the cache read as changed, a link of the guard having been undone there), leaves it; invariant 2 asks only that no user copy be taken, so the check now asks that the copy moved be no user's, and that a file that had a user copy keep one.

Two observations, which break no invariant, for the maintainer: (1) a pending copy whose part a stopped copy left is planned as if none of it were written, its part's bytes counted as used and its whole size as the room it needs: on a disk its parts nearly fill, the plan cancels it, its part goes at the next tidy, and it is copied again from nothing (`Copies.tla`, met while its tidy was not yet synced); (2) in a file in `conflict` whose copy on a disk was replaced, the link guard journals at each pass an operation `link` for that copy, which `ops.MakeLink` undoes, the copy's row holding the inode of the file replaced and the look calling it there (`catalog.LinkCopies`, `gone_ns` NULL): one undone operation a pass for each such copy until the conflict is resolved.

## How sensitive it is

Each of these known-bad designs, put into the model one at a time during its writing, was caught by the invariant it breaks: the walk's confirmation without the mount id (`Inv4_UnknownNeverAbsent`), the commit of a copy right after its rename (`Inv7_PipelineOrder`), a move to the trash without the look right before it (`Inv10_DeletionOnlyUnchangedToTrash`), the source's folder synced before the trash entry's (`NoUserDataLost`), the plan without the physical disks (`Inv13_OnePhysicalDisk`), a rename that replaces what took the target (`NoUserDataLost`), and a reprise of `trash` that finishes whatever both names hold (`Inv11_OwnOpsNotUsers`). Two were not: a deletion propagated to a copy found edited is undone by two later defences of `specs.md` (the modification of the same pass drops the trash job, and the look right before the move refuses an edited copy); and without the verdict `own`, nothing changes here, as the journal's planned operations make every copy Atoll is moving `unknown` to a walk, and `own` needs a catalog rebuilt, which the model leaves out.

The view's part, 2026-11-22, each put in on its own: a take-in that does not wait for the catalog to present what the view does (`ViewPresentsLatest`, in `ViewParallel`, 4,568 states: the view's file presented as nothing once its creation is taken in, before a pass records it); a part adopted while the program still writes it (`Inv7_PipelineOrder`, `ViewParallel`, 11,546 states: a write reaches the replica after its verification); and the daemon's reprise resolving the view's operations as its own (`Inv7_PipelineOrder` in `ViewDelete`, 1,883 states, and nothing in `ViewParallel`). Not caught by an invariant: a walk that takes what the view sent to the trash for Atoll's own move, which keeps the other copy where the deletion should reach it, a harm `EventuallyViewTaken` alone can see.

The SSD cache's transfer, 2026-11-22, each put in on its own: the transfer with no wait for the view's operations at its path (`ViewPresentsLatest`, in `CacheView`, 8,321 states: the view presents the file from the cache it left); the transfer with no hand-over of the user part (`Inv2_LastUserCopyKept`, `Cache`, 43,423); the move to the cache's trash with no look right before it (`NoUserDataLost`, `Cache`, 101,968: a user's edit of the copy on the cache purged); the purge committed before its folder's sync (`Inv7_PipelineOrder`, `Cache`, 91,386); and the transfer due with copies on data disks not verified (`NoUserDataLost` in `CacheRewrite`, 43,393, which was written for it: a write that moved only the change time of the new copy taken for the file; nothing in `Cache`, 3,738,360 states, where no write keeps the dates).

The link guard, 2026-11-22: a link removed though it is its data's last name (`NoUserDataLost`, in `GuardOff`, 41,895 states, and nothing in `Guard`); a link's commit giving the copy's row the change time it finds, whatever the row recorded (`ReplicaInSync`, `GuardRewrite`, 16,671). Not caught: the guard turned off removing the link of a copy whose deletion waits (`GuardOff`, 1,417,078 states), as the check of another name that `ops.DropLink` makes keeps a link that holds its data alone; and a link whose data is an entry of the trash sent to the trash again (`Guard`, 290,912), which makes a second entry of the same data, no invariant's harm.

The remedies of flaws 8 to 11, 2026-11-22, once each was what the model does, each part taken out on its own: the look at the data copies before the purge (`NoUserDataLost`, in `FlawTransferPurged`, 457 states); the view's open going to the next copy (`ViewPresentsLatest`, `FlawTransferUnseen`, 317); of `view-held`, the view's operations read again as the move is journaled (`ViewPresentsLatest` in `FlawTransferWritten`, 1,819, and in `CacheView`, 32,964), the open to write waiting while the move is planned (`ViewPresentsLatest`, 2,261 and 33,071), and what the view holds open never taken in (`NoUserDataLost` in `FlawTransferWritten`, 2,529, `ViewPresentsLatest` in `CacheView`, 43,331): each part is needed; and the reprise of a link keeping the row (`ReplicaInSync` in `FlawLinkReprise`, 1,820, and in `GuardRewrite`, 20,062).

## What it leaves out

Names and paths (one logical path a file), renames, folders and their operations (`Rename.tla`'s, below), cycles and exchanges; NTFS, collisions and refused names; the guards (a decision may always wait, which covers what they hold, not what they count); `deletes.direct = "restore"`; restores, purges but the transfer's, and `prune`; of the SSD cache, a second cache disk, `cache.mirror` and `cache.replicate_early`, the time of a residency, `replication.window` and `max_fill` (a transfer may always wait), and a cache disk without `RENAME_NOREPLACE`; of the link guard, disks without `RENAME_NOREPLACE` and the type `ntfs`, a link nothing names (a file whose row went with no `copy-gone` event, which the model never meets), batches of more than one link, a purge's links, and the look before a link or its removal and the act as one step; scrub and repair; of Atoll's own view, what moves or names (a rename, a replacement, a hard link, a folder made or removed, a symbolic link), extended attributes, changes of attributes alone, the move of a file whose write ran out of space (`ViewMove.tla`'s), the view read only, its checks of access, more than one part a file (`Level` 2), a part's room and the limit of parts at once, the size and time a take-in compares, which the model gives as the object and its change count, and two copies of one version compared by their versions; copies at once (`Copies.tla`'s, below); the catalog rebuilt; metadata-only changes and the operations `chmod`, `chown`, `utimens`, `xattr`; an edit that keeps size and modification time, which only the change time tells, everywhere but in `FlawReplicaRewritten`, where it is a user's only action: the reprise of a move to the trash (§17.2, `same`/`same`, and the trash's name alone) and the looks before the `unlink` and after the move of a move to the trash on a disk without `RENAME_NOREPLACE` compare size and modification time alone, as the `link` moved the change time, and a copy made on such a disk is looked at after its `unlink`, so that a rewrite between its link and that look is taken (§14.2); the time and its windows. A user acts durably, and with what came before on that disk.

An inode number taken again is left out too: every file a user makes is a new object, so the model never meets the case the maintainer decided on 2026-11-21 ("Inode + taille + mtime", `specs.md` §17.1, step 4, §17.2), a file made at a copy's name with the inode number of the copy just removed, which a move to the trash took for the copy by its inode alone. What it checks is what that decision changes for the copy itself: a look at the trash's name alone, in a reprise and right after the move, knows the copy by its object and its change count (gen), as Atoll's looks compare its inode, its size and its modification time, so that the copy edited through the name it shared with its link, then that name deleted, between the `link` and the look at the source, is not taken for the copy, and goes back to its path (`TrashLook`, `TrashUnlink`, `TrashResolve`, `TrashResolved`). That takes three user actions, one more than `Link.cfg` allows, whose states are the same with the rule or without it: `LinkEdited.cfg` reaches it, 249,072 distinct states, and 248,712 with the rule left out. No invariant of the model tells a user's version in the trash from one in the library, and none failed with the rule left out, in either instance: where such a file stays is the maintainer's choice, which the model takes as `specs.md` says it.

The window between a look and the move it guards is left out of the daemon's own moves, its trash among them: its look right before a move to the trash and the move are one step. The view's deletion takes them apart, which is how flaw 4 showed; the daemon resolves that case by the table already (`trash.Session.afterFailedMove`), and the model does not check it there.

The next models worth writing, by risk, since those of 2026-11-23 (folders renamed across disks, chains and exchanges in `Folders.tla`; the view's move of a file out of space in `ViewMove.tla`; the SSD cache with the link guard here; copies at once of one file to several disks with its refreshes in `Copies.tla`): through Atoll's own view, its rename, its replacement onto a name it presents and its folders renamed or removed on every disk at once (§21.2); and in `Folders.tla`, folders below folders and refused names.

## Renames and folders: Rename.tla

A model of its own, `Rename.tla` with `MCRename.tla` (2026-11-22), as renames need paths, which `Replication.tla` leaves out, one logical path a file: each of its instances names it, `\* module: MCRename.tla`. It holds two logical paths, A/x and B/x, each in a folder of its own below the root, A and B; on each of two disks the file at each path and each folder, by object (an inode with its creation time), as the kernel shows them and as a power cut leaves them, each folder with its mode, private or open, and who made it; the catalog's files and their copies, each where it sits (its file's path, or `old_lpath`), the folders' entries with `created_by`, and the journal (`rename`, `mkdir`, `rmdir`). A user, directly on a disk, renames the file either way (making the folder it goes to, with a mode), makes a file where the path is free, replaces one (an atomic save), deletes or edits one, makes a folder, changes its mode, or removes it empty. Atoll runs passes: the mount check and the reprise of each disk's planned operations (§17.2: a rename by the identity of §15.1; a folder made by what is there, which takes the mode its operation journaled while it holds nothing, as `ops.recoverMkdir` does; a folder removed by its inode), the walk of each disk and its confirmation, with the verdicts there, `moved`, `gone`, `replaced`, `unknown` at a name of an operation planned there, and what is new; the decision Rename (the file takes its target when no other file holds it, its copies left behind get `old_lpath`, a disk away included; a copy gone has its row go, as nothing goes to the trash here); then on each disk the rename of each copy behind (§15.12, "On each writable disk"): the look at both names, each missing parent an operation `mkdir` with the mode of the folder on the first disk whose copy is at the target, or on any disk, or 0755 with none, waiting for a disk that may hold it and was not looked at (`jobs.folderLike`), the folder made never replacing anything, then the operation `rename`, journaled, the look right before, the move without replacing anything (`RENAME_NOREPLACE`, or `link`, its sync, the look at the source and `unlink`), the look after, the syncs and the commit; and last, Atoll's own empty folders, each an operation `rmdir` (§17.5).

| Invariant | Checks |
|---|---|
| `NoUserDataLost` | every version a user wrote is still durably at a path of some disk, but for what a user's own action destroyed |
| `Inv4_UnknownNeverAbsent` | 4: nothing a walk of a disk gone found is recorded |
| `Inv7_RenameDurable` | 7: a rename is done only once its target holds the copy durably and its old name no longer does, as long as nobody changed them since: no row is ever said to be at a name it is not at (§17.2) |
| `Inv12_JournalFirst` | 12: every folder Atoll made is named by an operation `mkdir` of its disk |
| `FolderNeverOpened` | §15.12 step 3, §17.4: a folder Atoll makes for a rename is never open where the other disk holds it private |
| `UserFolderKept` | 2: Atoll gives its mode to, records as its own, or removes, only a folder it made |
| `NamesKept` | 15: Atoll's rename never leaves a file it did not mean to move at the name it gave it |
| `RenamesAreUsers` | §15.12, A disk away: a file takes only a path a user moved one of its copies to, so that a copy left behind, back, never reads as the user renaming the file back |
| `OneRowAnObject` | 13 on one disk: no object is the copy of two files |
| `EventuallyFollowed` | liveness, with fairness: every copy follows its file's rename, and no operation stays planned |

| Instance | Expects | What it explores | States | Time |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RenameFolder` | pass | a file renamed on one disk into a folder made for it, private or open, that the other disk lacks; Atoll killed once, a disk lost once | 18,421 | 5 s |
| `RenameUsers` | pass | two user actions of every kind on either disk, Atoll killed once, the power cut once, a disk lost once | 4,237,582 | 3 min 43 s |
| `RenameLink` | pass | two disks without `RENAME_NOREPLACE`, two user actions (renames, replacements, deletions, edits), Atoll killed once, a disk lost once | 563,088 | 35 s |
| `RenameLive` | pass | liveness: a file renamed on one disk into a folder the other lacks, Atoll killed once, a disk lost once | 18,673 | 9 s |
| `FlawRenameFolderTaken` | pass | flaw 6, fixed: its trace, with the remedy; a folder a user makes where an operation `mkdir` was journaled, Atoll killed in between | 5,468 | 4 s |
| `FlawRenameFileTaken` | pass | flaw 7, fixed: its trace, with the remedy; a file saved at a copy's old name between the look right before its rename and the rename | 1,604 | 4 s |

Flaws 6 and 7 are fixed, and `Fixes` is empty. `NDisks` is 2: `FolderNeverOpened` compares with the other disk, as the rule chooses the model among the others.

### Flaw 6: the reprise of a folder made took a user's folder for Atoll's own

Fixed on 2026-11-22, with the remedy the maintainer chose, (a) below, within format version 1 as `ops.file_key` holds the folder's inode: `specs.md` §9, §17.2, §17.4, `ops.Disk.MakeRootFolder`, which journals the inode right after the `mkdir` and before anything is given to the folder, and `ops.recoverMkdir`, which finishes only that folder, with the crash points `mkdir-journaled` and `mkdir-unkeyed`; `TestAFolderAUserMadeIsNotTakenByTheReprise` (`internal/jobs/mkdirkey_test.go`), which failed before, `TestAFolderMadeBeforeItsInodeIsJournaledIsLeftAsMade` for what the remedy leaves, `../scenarios/replicate-crash-folder-a-user-made.json`, and `FlawRenameFolderTaken.cfg` expecting `pass`. What it was:

1. A file at A/x on disks 1 and 2. A user renames it on disk 2 into B, which they make for it.
2. A pass takes the rename to disk 1, which lacks B: the operation `mkdir` of B journaled, with the mode of disk 2's B.
3. Atoll stops before the folder is made (kill -9, a reboot; or the disk fails with `EIO` at the `mkdir`, as in the test).
4. Meanwhile a user makes B on disk 1 by hand, with another mode: their own folder, private (0700) in the test.
5. The next reprise finds a folder at B: it finishes the operation, gives the user's folder the model's mode and owner, as it holds nothing yet (0755 in the test: a private folder opened), and records it `created_by = atoll`, which §17.5 then removes once Atoll leaves it empty.

The window is Atoll's whole time down between the journal and the `mkdir`, which a disk that fails there makes as long as it is away. Remedies: (a) the operation records the folder's inode right after the `mkdir`, in a commit of its own before it gives the mode, as a copy's part records its key (§14.2), and its reprise finishes only a folder of that inode, leaving any other as a user's, the operation `undone` (`mkdir-key` in `Fixes`); a folder Atoll made and stopped before recording it is then left as it was made, owned by Atoll's account and not tidied, the cost of keeping a user's; (b) the reprise takes the mode and owner only of a folder owned by the account Atoll runs as, which is what its own `mkdir` gives it, and records any other as a user's, which needs no new record.

### Flaw 7: a file saved at a copy's old name followed Atoll's rename to the new one

Fixed on 2026-11-22, with the remedy the maintainer chose, (a) below: `specs.md` §15.12, step 4, `ops.Disk.putBack`, called by `RenameFile` when the look after the move finds what arrived is not the copy, with the crash point `put-back`; `TestAFileSavedAtTheOldNameGoesBackAfterTheRename` (`internal/jobs/renameback_test.go`), which failed before, `TestAPassKilledOnceAFileIsPutBack`, and `FlawRenameFileTaken.cfg` expecting `pass`. What it leaves: a file a user saves at the new name in the width between the move and that look is put back at the old name too, as nothing tells the two apart. What it was:

1. A file at A/x on disks 1 and 2. A user renames it to B/x on disk 2; a pass takes the rename to disk 1: the operation `rename` journaled, and the look right before finds the copy at A/x and nothing at B/x.
2. In the moment between that look and `renameat2`, a program saves a new file at A/x on disk 1, an atomic save, as one that imports a new release at the old name does.
3. `rename(2)` moves what A/x holds then: the new file goes to B/x.
4. The look after finds at B/x a file that is not the copy: the table says target `other`, source `absent`, undone, and what is at the target stays. The user's file is at a name Atoll gave it, beside where the file of B/x is on the other disk, and the next walks take it for a copy of that file, of other content.

The window is the width of a `rename(2)` after a `stat`, and needs a program writing directly to the disk while a pass renames; on a disk without `RENAME_NOREPLACE` the look at the source after the `link` catches it, and the link goes. Remedies: (a) at once after the move, a file at the target that the move itself took from the old name, when the old name is free again, goes back there without replacing anything, synced, as §17.2 has a move to the trash put back what took its source's name (`rename-back` in `Fixes`); a reprise cannot tell such a file from one a user put at the target, and leaves it, as now; (b) nothing, as the window is that narrow, and the next walks see a conflict, every version kept.

### Modelling mistakes, found by TLC and fixed

- A rename's commit first had its names durable as the move left them, whatever a user did after the syncs: a rename back by the user right after them read as a commit before the syncs. As in `Replication.tla`, a name must be durable only while it still holds what Atoll put there (`NamesDurable`).
- On a disk without `RENAME_NOREPLACE`, a source that no longer held the copy at the look after the `link` left the link at the target when the source held another file: the code removes the link it made (`moveNoReplace`), and so does the model now, by what it linked.
- `RenamesAreUsers` first took a copy that caught up with its file's own path, as after that link, for a rename: §15.12 says it is none, a file's own path being free to it.

### How sensitive it is

Each put in on its own: the model of a missing parent chosen without waiting for a disk that may hold it and was not looked at (`FolderNeverOpened`, in `RenameFolder`, 5,454 states, and in `RenameUsers`); and a disk away given no `old_lpath` when its file renames, whose copy, back, reads as the user renaming the file back, so that the file returns to its old path on both disks, the user's rename undone: no invariant saw that until `RenamesAreUsers` was written for it (5,837 states in `RenameFolder`, 5,841 in `RenameLive`), as the liveness only asks that the copies agree.

### What it leaves out

Content beyond a version, edits seen as modifications and conflicts (`Replication.tla`'s); deletions propagated to the trash (a copy gone has its row go: Kept); folders renamed and their jobs, cycles and exchanges (`Folders.tla`'s); NTFS, refused names and collisions; the guards and the debounce; more than two disks; the time of folders; a rename onto a name a file holds, the overwriting move; and the verdicts `uncertain` and `own`, as no hard link and no trash is modelled.

## Copies at once: Copies.tla

A model of its own, `Copies.tla` with `MCCopies.tla` (2026-11-22), as `Replication.tla` runs one copy at a time, as the conformance build does (§29.4), and copies at once need the steps of several copies interleaved, and the room of a disk counted: each of its instances names it, `\* module: MCCopies.tla`. It holds files of a size each, their copies by object with the bytes each holds, as the kernel shows them and as a power cut leaves them; each disk's capacity and `min_free`, and the bytes a user's own files and the parts Atoll's own view writes in parallel take there; the catalog's copies (`ok`, `pending`), the replicate jobs (queued, running, the part each owns) and the journal (`create`). Atoll runs passes: the mount check and the reprise of each disk's planned operations (§14.2), the tidy of `tmp/`, synced, the plan (§13.4: the pending copies kept in the order of the files while each fits past `min_free` with those kept before it, one whose job is running holding no room, the others canceled; then each file below its level gets its copies, on the disk with the most room past `min_free` and what the plan holds there), then the run (§14, `internal/jobs/parallel.go`): a queued job starts as soon as the disk it reads and the disk it writes each run fewer copies than their limit, fewer than `Total` run in all, and no copy of its file runs, the per-path lock; each copy under way takes its steps, journaled, its part made, copied (`ENOSPC`, or a write that would take its disk past `min_free`, is `no-room`: its part goes, undone, queued again), synced and verified, renamed without replacing anything, synced, committed; and a disk one copy cannot rely on is lost at once for the others, each under way on it stopping at its next step, planned for the reprise on its target, undone with its part kept on its source. The world: Atoll killed, the power cut, a disk lost and mounted again, a user writing a file of their own to a disk, which takes room past `min_free` as they like, and the view writing a part to a disk, with the room past `min_free` it measured from the filesystem, less what the pending copies there still have to write.

| Invariant | Checks |
|---|---|
| `MinFreeKept` | §12.4, §13.4: Atoll's copies and the view's parts never leave a disk less free than `min_free`, but a disk where a user's own file took that room |
| `CapacityKept` | nothing is written past a disk's capacity |
| `OneCopyOfAFile` | the per-path lock: never two copies of one file under way at once |
| `LimitsKept` | each disk's limit of copies at once, read and written, and the limit in all |
| `Inv1_CopiesHold` | 1: a copy the catalog calls `ok` and verified holds its file |
| `Inv7_PipelineOrder` | 6, 7: no copy of Atoll's at a name of the library without all its bytes, durable, and no commit before the names are |
| `Inv11_OwnOpsNotUsers` | 11: no copy Atoll made in the library unknown to the catalog, unless its operation is planned |
| `Inv12_JournalFirst` | 12: every part and every entry of a trash, visible or durable, named by a committed operation of its disk |
| `LatestKept` | the last content a user wrote into a file is durably at its path or in a trash, on some disk |
| `ReplicaInSync` | a copy `ok`, verified, that nobody wrote into since its row, holds the content its row describes |
| `EventuallySettled` | liveness, with fairness: every file reaches its level, every copy holding its current content, and no operation stays planned |

**One file to several disks, with refreshes** (2026-11-23, `MaxEdit`): a user writes into a copy in place, a new content of the same size, at any moment. After the reprise, a pass looks at each disk of its session and decides the modification (§15.4): one content among the changed copies, read as it has the file's size, makes them current and verified, every other copy stale and unverified, with a trash job from the first changed copy by position, and drops every queued job of the file with its pending copy; several contents, or a stale copy changed, a conflict, never queued; a file with a job running waits. Each stale copy's trash job then runs as §17.1 sends a copy, journaled, compared with its row right before the move (one step with it), moved, synced, committed, its row a pending copy whose replicate job reads the copy that changed (§15.12, Trash jobs; §14.1), and the reprise of such a move finishes it or undoes it by §17.2. A copy starts only from a source `ok` and as its row says, its job waiting otherwise for its settle, the next pass here; it looks at its source again once read, and is dropped as changed when the source moved since it began or its part is not the file's hash (§14.2). With `MaxEdit = 0` none of it moves, and every earlier instance gives the very states it did.

| Instance | Expects | What it explores | States | Time |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ParallelRefresh` | pass | one file at three copies on three SSDs of two copies at once each; a user writes into a copy twice: the two others stale, sent to the trash and refreshed from it, two copies of one file at once, which the per-path lock runs one at a time; Atoll killed once, a disk lost once | 1,508,907 | 52 s |
| `ParallelSpread` | pass | one file on one SSD wanting two replicas on the two others, read from one source; a user writes into the source once; Atoll killed once, the power cut once, a disk lost once | 611,577 | 20 s |
| `ParallelRefreshLive` | pass | liveness: `ParallelRefresh`'s pool, one write, Atoll killed once: every copy holds the current content at last | 2,550 | 2 s |

TLC found no flaw there. A modelling mistake it showed, fixed: the commit recorded what the copy held then, where §14.2 records what the part was when verified (flaw 1's remedy), so that a user's write into the new copy between its sync and its commit read as the copy verified (`Inv1_CopiesHold`, 745,219 states, met in a sensitivity run). How sensitive it is, each taken out in a copy of the module not kept: the copy's look at its source again once read, and its check against the file's hash, each covers the other in these pools (`ParallelSpread` without the look, 469,855 states, and `ParallelRefresh` without the check, 1,508,907, the same states as with it, both pass); both taken out, a copy that read a source a user wrote into meanwhile is recorded `ok` and verified with a content the file does not have (`Inv1_CopiesHold`, `ParallelSpread`, 4,489 states).

The pool of every instance: disks 1 and 2 with a spindle, one copy at once each, each full past `min_free` with the file it holds; disk 3 an SSD, two copies at once, with room for both past `min_free` (`CapThree`) or for one (`CapTwo`); file 1 on disk 1, file 2 on disk 2, each wanting one replica, which only disk 3 can take: two copies at once to one disk, from two.

| Instance | Expects | What it explores | States | Time |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Parallel` | pass | both copies at once to the SSD, Atoll killed once, the power cut once, a disk lost once | 1,957,109 | 1 min 15 s |
| `ParallelTight` | pass | the SSD with room for one: the plan queues one copy, holding its room, the other waits | 3,497 | 2 s |
| `ParallelFill` | pass | `Parallel`, a user writing a file of their own to a disk, in place of the power cut | 973,464 | 43 s |
| `ParallelView` | pass | `Parallel`, the view writing a part to the SSD, in place of the power cut | 228,140 | 10 s |
| `ParallelLive` | pass | liveness: both copies at once, Atoll killed once | 5,081 | 7 s |
| `ParallelLiveLost` | pass | liveness: both copies at once, a disk lost once and mounted again in time | 10,715 | 14 s |
| `FlawParallelRoom` | pass | flaw 12, fixed: its pool, with both remedies; both copies at once, the view writing a part to the SSD | 235 | 2 s |

### Flaw 12: the room past min_free of a disk is shared by the daemon's copies and the view's parts

Fixed on 2026-11-22, with both remedies below, as the maintainer chose, model-checked together: `specs.md` §14, §14.1, §14.2 and §21.2; (a) `view-room` in `writer.openPart`, the room it measures on a disk less what the catalog's pending copies there still have to write, each its size less what its part holds (`catalog.PendingOn`, `ops.Disk.PartSize`, `writer.pendingLeft`); (b) `copy-room` in the copy (`replicator.spaceOf`, `room.keeps`), each write that grows its part checking `min_free` first, by the disk's free space then, or, when less, by what it was at the copy's first look less what the copy wrote since, and ending `no-room` past it, a free space that cannot be read letting the write through: in qemu, `test/loopdev`'s `TestACopyStopsAtMinFreeOnEachDriver` found that btrfs counts a write in its free space only once it is flushed, so that the disk's word alone let a copy write on past `min_free` there (`TestACopyCountsWhatItWroteWhereItsDiskDoesNotYet`). Tests, each seen failing before: `TestTheViewsPartsLeaveTheRoomAPendingCopyHolds` (`internal/view/writer/parallelroom_test.go`) and `TestACopyStopsBeforeItTakesItsDiskPastMinFree` (`internal/jobs/copyroom_test.go`), with `TestTheViewsPartsTakeTheRoomAPendingCopyNoLongerNeeds`, `TestACopyStoppedAsItDropsForMinFree`, kill -9 before and after its part goes, and `TestACopyGoesOnWhenItsDiskSpaceCannotBeRead`; `FlawParallelRoom.cfg` expects `pass`, 235 states, as `ParallelView`, 228,140, the same as with `view-room` alone: with it, no part takes the room a copy holds, and `copy-room` keeps `min_free` against any other writer Atoll has, which these pools do not hold. What it was (126 states): §14 said "Copies at once need no room of their own: what the plan queued on a disk fits there together, after `min_free`, each pending copy holding its room (§13.4)"; the copies checked nothing but `ENOSPC` as they wrote. §21.2: the view's parts in parallel take their targets from "each disk's free space as its filesystem says it now", and their room past `min_free` likewise (`watch.ParallelTargets`, `writer.openPart`): the room the catalog's pending copies hold was not counted.

1. A pass's plan queues two copies to the SSD disk 3, their pending copies holding all its room past `min_free`.
2. The copies run at once; meanwhile a new file written through Atoll's own view gets a part on disk 3, whose room is the disk's free space less `min_free`.
3. The part takes room the plan gave the copies, which then take the disk past `min_free`, not past its capacity: each writer keeps `min_free` alone, and both together do not.

`TestFlawTheViewsPartsTakeTheRoomAPendingCopyHolds` (then `internal/view/writer/flaws_test.go`) failed on the code: a part went to the disk whose room past `min_free` a pending copy held whole.

Remedies, each checked against every invariant: (a) `view-room`: the view's room past `min_free` on a disk takes off what the catalog's pending copies there still have to write, each its size less what its part holds (`ParallelView.cfg` passes with it, and the flaw's pool, 235 states); (b) `copy-room`: a copy checks `min_free` as it writes, as the view's parts do, and ends `no-room` past it, its part gone (497,078 states in `ParallelView`, 688 in the flaw's pool). (a) touches the view alone, which reads the catalog already; (b) keeps `min_free` against any writer Atoll has, at a look at the free space as a copy writes. A user's own file may still take the room, which `MinFreeKept` excuses: then the copies end `no-room` or `ENOSPC`, as `ParallelFill` checks.

### How sensitive it is

Each put in on its own: the plan placing a copy with no room past `min_free` (`MinFreeKept`, in `ParallelTight`, 358 states); the plan forgetting the room the copies it placed before hold (`MinFreeKept`, `ParallelTight`, 962); a copy going on through the handle of a mount it lost, as one beside it found the disk failing (`Inv7_PipelineOrder`, `Parallel`, 3,330); and the tidy of `tmp/` not synced (`MinFreeKept`, `Parallel`, met while the model was written: parts the tidy removed came back after a power cut beside a copy the plan made in their room). Not caught: each disk's limit of copies at once taken away (`Parallel`, 1,957,109 states, the same), as in these pools a disk with a spindle has one copy reading it and the SSD's limit is the number of copies there, a matter of speed, not safety; and the per-path lock, which `OneCopyOfAFile` checks by its definition alone, as a file here has one job at a time.

Flaw 12's remedies, 2026-11-22: both taken out, `MinFreeKept` in `FlawParallelRoom` (129 states) and in `ParallelView` (2,802); `view-room` alone taken out, nothing (688 and 497,078 states), `copy-room` keeping `min_free` by itself; `copy-room` alone taken out, nothing (235 and 228,140), as in these pools nothing but the view's parts and a user's own file, which `MinFreeKept` excuses, takes the room the plan counted on.

### What it leaves out

What a walk finds and the decisions beyond a copy changed in place (no deletion, replacement or rename), the debounce and settle (a source found changed waits for the next pass), the purge of the trash, folders, and the lock of a disk's names (each step that makes or removes a name is one step here, as the lock makes it in the code), more than one part a file, of the view's parts the room alone (their journal and adoption are `Replication.tla`'s), a chunk at a time (a copy writes its bytes in one step) and a filesystem that counts a write in its free space only once it is flushed, the window of 4,096 jobs, and time.

## Folders renamed across disks: Folders.tla

A model of its own, `Folders.tla` with `MCFolders.tla` (2026-11-23), for what `Rename.tla` leaves out: a folder renamed on one disk whose copies are on others, the job that carries it to each, a chain of renames on a disk, and a cycle that only an exchange of two names gets round. It keeps folder names at the root (D, E, F) and file names in a folder (x, y), each instance naming how many; on each of two disks the folders at the root and the files in each folder, by object, as the kernel shows them and as a power cut leaves them, each folder's names made durable by its own sync, an exchange's two folders by one; the catalog's files and copies, each where it sits and with the kind of clock its last look started (`gone_kind`), the entries of the folders, the folder jobs of each disk (`kind = rename`, `file_id` NULL) and the journal (a rename of a file or of a folder, and `mkdir`, here in one step: `Rename.tla` has its steps). A user, directly on a disk: renames a folder, swaps two folders' names, moves a file (its folder made), swaps two files' names, makes, replaces or deletes a file, makes or removes a folder. Atoll, one pass after another: the mount check and the reprise (§17.2: a file by the identity of §15.1, a folder by its inode, and a folder's commit as `FinishFolderRename(op, 0)` gives it in a reprise); the walk of each disk, its confirmation, its verdicts and the folders found renamed (`watch.moveFolders`: a folder row whose folder is not at its name, found at a name with no row, its old name holding no file at home; its row stays, and its new name and what is below it get none until decided), a place holding another row's copy read as gone, as the code reads it; the decisions about the files, Rename by its greatest fixed point, a job above a copy whose `old_lpath` a rename back clears dropped (`catalog.dropJobsAbove`); then the folders found renamed, in the order of their jobs, each waiting while its disk still holds copies below its old name, but for those no pass can take from there, one no copy came with only its entries and the jobs it moves on (`catalog.moveFolderJobs`), and otherwise the files below it that only other disks hold following it, its entries, the jobs moved on and a job for each other disk with a copy below it (`jobs.decideFolder`); then on each disk by position its folder jobs, in the order of `jobs.folderOrder`, the first of a cycle dropped, each run when ready (`folderReady`, `landsBadly`) as one operation, journaled, the look right before by the folder's inode, `RENAME_NOREPLACE`, the look after, the sync and the commit, anything else resolved at once by the table (`trash.Session.AfterFailedMove`), the job dropped when not ready, its copies then following one by one; then the renames by old path, round after round until one moves nothing, each as `Rename.tla`'s with the put-back of flaw 7; and once a round moves nothing, the copies left path-taken whose targets lead round a cycle, the first by place and the copy at its target exchanged (`jobs.exchange`, `ops.Disk.ExchangeFiles`): both journaled, the look right before at both names, the exchange of what both hold then, the look after at both, the sync of both folders and the two commits.

| Invariant | Checks |
|---|---|
| `NoUserDataLost` | every version a user wrote is durably at a path of some disk, but for what a user's own action destroyed |
| `Inv4_UnknownNeverAbsent` | 4: nothing a walk of a disk gone found is recorded |
| `Inv7_RenameDurable` | 7: a rename of a file or of a folder, or an exchange, is done only once its names are durable as it left them |
| `NamesKept` | 15: Atoll never leaves a file or a folder it did not mean to move at a name it gave it; a folder's operation carries what is below it, by design (§15.12) |
| `RenamesAreUsers` | §15.12: a file takes only a path a user moved one of its copies to, or the latest name a user's folder renames gave its folder, as a file new on a disk that a folder's job carries is read (§15.12, "as a user's rename would be"), never a name a user took back |
| `OneRowAnObject` | 13 on one disk: no object is the copy of two files |
| `EventuallyFollowed` | liveness, with fairness: every copy follows its file, no folder job is left, nothing stays planned |

Measured on 2026-11-23 with `test/tla/run.sh`, 2 workers, other agents' containers on the machine:

| Instance | Expects | What it explores | States | Time |

### Flaw 13: a file saved at one of two names right before an exchange follows it to the other

Fixed on 2026-11-23 with the remedy the maintainer chose, (a) `exchange-back` below: `specs.md` §15.12, A cycle; `ops.Disk.ExchangeFiles`, whose look after, finding a name that does not hold the other's copy, exchanges the two back (`exchangeBack`, the crash point `put-back`), synced, the folders' times restored, and says `ErrExchangedBack`, on which `jobs.exchange` undoes both renames, an `op-undone` event each; what stops the exchange back leaves the names as they are, for the table. `FlawExchangeSaved.cfg` expects `pass` now, a regression instance (1,211 states before the remedy, `NamesKept`). Tests, each failing first: `TestAFileSavedBeforeAnExchangeStaysAtItsName` (`internal/jobs`, which was `TestFlawAFileSavedBeforeAnExchangeFollowsIt` behind the build tag `flaws`) and `TestAnExchangeBackStoppedOrFailing` (kill -9 at `put-back`, then the reprise; its sync failing with `EIO`; the exchange back failing with `EIO`, the names then the table's, nothing lost); on loop devices, `TestAnExchangeBackOnEachDriver` (ext4, XFS, btrfs).

§15.12, A cycle: "the first of them by place and the copy at its target exchange their names in one step, `renameat2` with `RENAME_EXCHANGE` ... a look right before finds each name holding its copy as its row says, then the exchange, a look at each name, which must hold the other's copy by the identity of §15.1, as after a rename". `RENAME_EXCHANGE` swaps whatever the two names hold when it runs, as `rename(2)` moves whatever the old name holds (flaw 7).

1. Files a at D/x and b at D/y on disks 1 and 2. A user swaps their names on disk 1; a pass renames both, and on disk 2 each copy is path-taken at the other's name: the exchange, both journaled, the look right before finds each copy at its name.
2. In the moment between that look and `renameat2`, a program saves a file at D/y on disk 2 (an atomic save; TLC's trace has the user swap the two names there instead, which does the same).
3. The exchange swaps what the names hold: the saved file goes to D/x, a's copy to D/y.
4. The look after finds D/y holding a's copy and D/x not b's: the table of §17.2 finishes a's rename and undoes b's, and what is at b's target, the file saved, stays there, at D/x, a name Atoll gave it (invariant 15); in TLC's trace a user's own swap there is undone by Atoll. Nothing is lost: the next walk finds b's copy gone, a file of its own at D/x, and records the saved file as a copy of b, in conflict with b's other copy, every version kept.

The window is the width of a `stat` and a `renameat2`, as flaw 7's, and needs a program writing directly to the disk while a pass gets round a cycle, which only a user's swap of names makes. The test saves the file from the fault hook of the exchange and finds it at the other name. The remedies proposed then, each checked against every invariant: (a) `exchange-back`: when the look after finds that a name does not hold the copy it should, and both names still hold what the exchange gave them, exchange them back at once, synced, and undo both operations, as flaw 7's remedy puts back what a rename took (`Exchange`, `SwapDirs`, `ExchangeLive` passed with it, and so did `FlawExchangeSaved`, 2,076 states); (b) nothing: the window is that narrow, and every version is kept.

### Flaw 14: a folder made at a folder's name right before that folder's job renames it is carried to the new name

Fixed on 2026-11-23 with the remedy the maintainer chose, (a) `folder-back` below: `specs.md` §15.12, Folders; `ops.Disk.RenameFolder`, whose look after, finding at the new name another folder than the one journaled, puts what came back at the old name when it is free (`putBackFolder`, the crash point `put-back`), synced, both parents' times restored; the table then undoes the operation and the job goes. A stop between the put back and the job's drop keeps the job, and the folder at its old name is then the new one: `jobs.folderReady` takes only the folder that disk's entry records there by its inode (`catalog.EntryKeyAt`), else the job goes, its copies following one by one; the model's `keep` and `FJPick` say the same. `FlawFolderTaken.cfg` expects `pass` now, a regression instance (14,498 states before the remedy, `NamesKept`). Tests, each failing first: `TestAFolderMadeBeforeAFolderJobStaysAtItsName` (`internal/jobs`, which was `TestFlawAFolderMadeBeforeAFolderJobFollowsIt` behind the build tag `flaws`) and `TestAFolderPutBackStoppedOrFailing` (kill -9 at `put-back`, which showed the job kept and carrying the new folder until the job took only its entry's folder; its sync failing with `EIO`; the put back failing with `EIO`, the folder then at E, the table's, nothing lost); the scenario `daemon-crash-folder-journaled-rotated.json`, a stop at `journaled` and the folder rotated by the runner, which fails without the entry's check; on loop devices, `TestAFolderPutBackOnEachDriver` (every driver, the plain rename of ntfs-3g and lowntfs-3g included).

§15.12, Folders: the job "runs when D is a folder on that disk, E is not below D ... E is absent ...: one operation `kind = rename`, `path` D, `path2` E, `file_key` the folder's inode. Everything below moves with it, whatever it is". `ops.Disk.RenameFolder` looks at D by the folder's inode, then `renameat2` moves whatever D holds then; the look after at E, by the inode, fails, `trash.Session.AfterFailedMove` resolves the operation by the table of §17.2, target `other`, source `absent`: undone, and what is at the target stays, as for a rename. `putBack` puts back only a file.

1. A folder D on disks 1 and 2, D/x on both. A user renames D to E on disk 1; a pass decides the folder renamed and queues a job D to E for disk 2.
2. The job's look right before finds D on disk 2 by its inode.
3. A program renames D away on disk 2, to F, and makes a new D there, with a file in it (a tool that rotates a folder), between that look and `renameat2`.
4. The rename moves the new D, with all it holds, to E. The look after finds there another folder: undone, and the new folder stays at E, a name Atoll gave it (invariant 15), its files at paths no one gave them; D is gone from disk 2.

Nothing is lost: the next walks find the files where they are. The window is the width of a `stat` and a `renameat2`, and needs two operations in it, a rename and a `mkdir`. The test makes them from the fault hook of the folder's rename and finds the new folder's file at E. The remedies proposed then, each checked against every invariant: (a) `folder-back`: at once after the move, a folder at the new name that is not the one journaled, which the move took from the old name, goes back there when that is free, without replacing anything, synced, as a file's rename puts back what it took (flaw 7), the operation then undone and the job dropped, its copies following one by one (`FlawFolderTaken` passed with it, 68,450 states); (b) nothing, as the window is narrow.

### Flaw 16: a folder's job left planned on a disk away carries a file a rename back has cleared

Fixed on 2026-11-23 with the remedy the maintainer chose, (a) `folder-wait` below: `specs.md` §15.12, A disk away; `jobs.belowAPlannedRename`, which gives a file with a copy, not pending, below the old or new name of a rename left planned on that copy's disk `Running`, as a file with a job running, so the solver decides nothing of it and a folder found renamed above it waits with it. `FlawFolderBack.cfg` expects `pass` now, a regression instance (30,355 states before the remedy, `RenamesAreUsers`).
