Tests, each failing first: `TestAFolderJobLeftPlannedHoldsTheFilesBelowItUntilItsReprise` (`internal/jobs`, which was `TestFlawAFolderJobLeftPlannedMovesAFileARenameBackClears` behind the build tag `flaws`); the scenario `daemon-crash-folder-then-renamed-back-alone.json`, the folder's job stopped at `moved`, its disk unmounted and the folder renamed back, D/y ending at D/y, and `daemon-crash-folder-then-renamed-back.json` updated: nothing decided while the disk is away, then the folder taken back whole; both fail without the remedy.

§15.12, A disk away: "A rename back meanwhile clears `old_lpath` with no disk operation", and "So does a job above a copy whose `old_lpath` a decision clears, as it was moved back and the job would carry it away again" (`catalog.dropJobsAbove`). §17.2: "A folder gives each copy behind below it `old_lpath` as above, and likewise a copy at its own file's path below it with another copy there, which a rename back left so". The decision does not ask whether the folder's operation is left planned on that disk, and the reprise moves only a copy with another copy elsewhere at its own path.

1. A folder D on disks 1 and 2, D/x on both and D/y on disk 2 alone. A user renames D to E on disk 1; a pass decides it, D/y following, and queues a job D to E for disk 2.
2. The job's rename of D goes through on disk 2, and its sync fails (a USB disk dropping): the operation stays planned, disk 2 lost.
3. The user renames the folder back to D on disk 1 (TLC's trace has them do it right after the walk of disk 1, before its decisions: the job then runs on a rename the walk no longer holds; the test does it after the job, with disk 2 away: the same).
4. The next pass, disk 2 away, decides the rename back: disk 2's rows get `old_lpath` NULL, as if its copies were still below D, and the job goes.
5. Disk 2 back, its reprise finds the folder at E, by its inode: done. Of its copies, D/x moves to E/x in the catalog, as disk 1 holds x at its own path; D/y, which disk 2 alone holds, keeps its row at D/y while it is at E/y.
6. The next walk finds y at E/y: a rename, which the decision takes for the user's: y's path becomes E/y, where the user had taken the folder's name back (`RenamesAreUsers`); x goes back to D/x on disk 2. Nothing is lost; a file the user kept in D is now in a folder E on disk 2, and is a file of E for every view.

The window is a disk lost at the sync of a folder's rename, and a rename back decided while it is away. The test fails the sync of the folder at its new name on disk 2, makes disk 2 missing, renames the folder back on disk 1, then brings disk 2 back: y ends at E/y on disk 2, at E/y in the catalog. The remedies proposed then, each checked against every invariant: (a) `folder-wait`: a file with a copy below a folder's operation left planned on its disk, at the old name or the new, is decided nothing of, and follows no folder, until that disk's reprise concludes it, as a file with a job running waits (§15.12, Due; flaw 2's remedy); a folder found renamed waits with it (`FlawFolderBack` passed with it, 37,359 states, `FolderJob` and `FolderLive` too); (b) the reprise of a folder's operation moves every copy of that disk at its own file's path below the old name, as a rename back left it, not only one with another copy at its own path elsewhere (a copy new on that disk would then follow too, which the rule keeps from it).

### Modelling mistakes, found by TLC and fixed

- A folder job ran above a copy whose rename back the same pass decided, and carried it away again, which the next walk read as a user's rename (`RenamesAreUsers`, `FolderJob`, 1,269,744 states): the code drops such a job in the decision's own transaction (`catalog.dropJobsAbove`, §15.12 "So does a job above a copy whose `old_lpath` a decision clears"), and so does the model now.
- A copy whose place holds another row's copy, as a half-resolved exchange leaves two rows at one place, read as `replaced` by that copy: the code reads it as gone, a name the file had left, and the decision is Kept (observation 3, below), and so does the model now.
- `RenamesAreUsers` read as Atoll's a file a user saved into a folder on a disk whose job then carried it to the folder's new name (1,434,452 states), which §15.12 reads "as a user's rename would be": it now takes the latest name a user gave the file's folder as a user's move, and so still finds flaw 16, whose user took that name back.
- An invariant of two rows at one place, once nothing is planned, which the first run of `Exchange` broke (231,441 states) on a trace that the next walk resolves as a user's own swap would: dropped, observation 3 below.

### Observation 3: an exchange resolved half way leaves two rows at one place

Not a flaw, for the maintainer: an exchange stopped once made and before its two commits (Atoll killed, or a disk lost), then resolved one operation at a time by the table of §17.2, where a user has deleted, saved or swapped one of the two names meanwhile, finishes one rename and undoes the other, whose row stays at the place the first one's copy now holds; or with only the first operation journaled and a user making the same swap by hand, the first finishes. §17.2 says "No row is ever said to be at a name it is not at". The next walk reads that row's copy gone with its `old_lpath`, a name the file had left: Kept, the file copied back to that disk, or with a file saved at its name a `conflict`, every version kept, as a user's own swap of the two names would read. A temporary test of the code (not kept) showed it: two rows of a disk at one place after the reprise, then the deletion Kept and the save a conflict. A remedy, if one is wanted: a rename whose source holds the copy of the other operation of its exchange, which only the exchange put there, finishes too, its row at its target.

### How sensitive it is

Each taken out on its own, in a copy of the module not kept: the put-back of flaw 7's remedy in a rename's look after (`NamesKept`, a pool of D/x on two disks, a user's move and save, 771 states); the drop of a folder's job above a copy a rename back clears (`RenamesAreUsers`, `FolderJob`, 1,269,744 states, the modelling mistake above); and, once the remedies of flaws 13, 14 and 16 were what every instance does, each part taken out on its own: the exchange back (`NamesKept` in `FlawExchangeSaved`, 1,214 states), the folder's put back (`NamesKept` in `FlawFolderTaken`, 14,452), the job taking only the folder its entry records: nothing, in `FlawFolderTaken` (68,644 states) nor with three operations (73,550), as the model's decisions are all due in the pass that walks (no debounce), so the walk that finds the folder renamed away moves the kept job before it runs, where the code's decision waits for its debounce and the job runs first: the scenario `daemon-crash-folder-journaled-rotated.json` shows it, failing without the check, and the files below a folder's operation left planned waiting (`RenamesAreUsers` in `FlawFolderBack`, 30,428). `LandsBadly`, the job's refusal to carry a copy at its own path onto a name another file holds, was not taken out.

### What it leaves out

The content beyond a version (edits, modifications and conflicts are `Replication.tla`'s); the trash (a copy gone has its row go: Kept); refused names and NTFS but the exchange (a disk outside `ExDisks` only lacks it); folders below folders, and so a folder renamed inside another and a folder's operation landing below its own old name (refused names and folders below folders are `Nested.tla`'s, below); a folder's mode and Atoll's own empty folders (`Rename.tla`'s); `mkdir`'s steps; the guards and the debounce (everything is due); the link variant of a move; a copy moved to two targets by two disks; more than two disks; the time of folders.

## The view's move of a file out of space: ViewMove.tla

A model of its own, `ViewMove.tla` with `MCViewMove.tla` (2026-11-23): `view.move_on_enospc` (§21.2, "A write that runs out of space"), which removes a user copy as the SSD cache's transfer does once the same bytes are on another disk. Two disks, the full one and the target; on each the file's path, the move's part in `tmp/` and its entry in the trash, as the kernel shows them and as a power cut leaves them; objects with their content, what size and modification time say of it and what moves the change time alone; the program's handle, the view's row of the move (its look at the full disk's copy when it began, and the operation of `ops` it runs now) and the journal (`create` of the part, `trash` of the copy left, `unlink` of its purge, or of its removal at its path). The program writes through the view; a write the full disk answers with `ENOSPC` starts the move when the file may move (`mayMove`); the move's steps, the program's writes held back meanwhile: the move and its `create` journaled, the part made, the copy read into it and looked at again, synced, read again; under the writer's lock the checks again, the rename without replacing to the path on the target, its sync; the `trash` journaled, the `create` done; the handle the part's; the copy left sent to its trash against what was copied, or left at its path beside the target's when changed, or, the trash with no room for it, its removal at its path journaled and made against what was copied; the purge; the move done; then the write made on the target. A user writes, rewrites keeping the dates, or deletes directly on either disk at any moment; `atoll mount` stopped short, the power cut, a disk lost and mounted again between any two steps; the view's start resolves a move left planned by the operation it names (`repriseMove`, `repriseMoveCreate`, `trashLeft`), and so does its check every 30 s for a disk back. The daemon's passes are left out: they wait at the path of a move planned, and the catalog holds no file at its path all through, as the move asks.

| Invariant | Checks |
|---|---|
| `NoUserDataLost` | every version a user or the program wrote is durably at the path or in the trash of some disk, but for what a write in place or a deletion of a user's took |
| `Inv2_MovedFirst` | 2, §21.2: the copy on the full disk leaves its path, or its trash, only once the target holds the same content durably at its path |
| `ViewPresentsLatest` | 14, §21.2: while the view runs and both disks are there, the path presents what the program last wrote, as long as no user wrote to a disk directly since: never nothing between the two disks |
| `Inv12_JournalFirst` | 12: every part and every entry of the trash is named by an operation of that disk |
| `EventuallyResolved` | liveness, with fairness: no move stays planned |

| Instance | Expects | What it explores | States | Time |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ViewMove` | pass | the move, the trash with room or without; a user once, on either disk; `atoll mount` stopped once, the power cut once, a disk lost once | 30,524 | 2 s |
| `ViewMoveLive` | pass | liveness: the move, a user once, `atoll mount` stopped once, a disk lost once and mounted again in time | 7,138 | 2 s |
| `FlawMoveRecheckOpen` | pass | flaw 15, fixed: the move, the target lost once, no user, with the remedy | 612 | 1 s |
| `FlawMoveTargetGone` | pass | flaw 17, fixed: the move, a user's deletion once, on either disk, with the remedy | 71 | 1 s |

### Flaw 17: the move purges the copy it left while the target no longer holds the file

Fixed on 2026-11-23 with the remedy the maintainer chose, (a) `move-look` below: `specs.md` §21.2, step 8 and What a failure leaves; `writer.targetHolds`, called by `purgeLeft` and `removeHere` right after the unlink is journaled, which compares the target's path with the copy as `placeMove` placed it (`PlaceUserPart`'s look right after the rename: inode, size, modification time, change time, `sameAs`), the program's writes held back until then; another, none, or a look that fails undoes the unlink with an `op-undone` event, the copy left kept, in the trash or at its path, the move done; the new crash point `move-target-looked` comes after a look that found it. It is flaw 8 of the SSD cache's transfer, in the view's move. `FlawMoveTargetGone.cfg` expects `pass` now, a regression instance (58 states before the remedy, `NoUserDataLost`). Tests, each failing first: `TestAMoveKeepsTheCopyLeftWhenTheTargetNoLongerHoldsItsCopy` (deleted, written, rewritten keeping its size and times, each in the trash and with the trash with no room; `internal/view/writer`, after `TestFlawAMoveOutOfSpacePurgesTheLastCopyOfAFileDeletedMeanwhile` behind the build tag `flaws`) and `TestAMoveWhoseTargetCannotBeLookedAtKeepsTheCopyLeft` (`EIO`); `TestAMoveStoppedAtEachStep` and `TestARemovalAtItsPathStoppedAtEachStep` stop at the new point too; on loop devices, `TestViewMoveKeepsTheCopyLeftWhenTheTargetGoes` (ext4, both ways the copy left goes) and `TestViewMovesAFileThatRunsOutOfSpace`, where the look finds the moved copy as placed.

§21.2, step 8: "the copy on the full disk sent to its trash, as §17.1 sends one, against what was copied ... then purged from there at once, a journaled `unlink`; or, when that trash has no room for it, ... removed at its path". `writer.leave` compares the copy left with what was copied, and looks at nothing on the target before the purge or the removal.

1. A new file the view made on disk 1, its write out of space: the move copies it to disk 2, places it at its path there, synced, journals the trash of the copy left, and the handle becomes the part's (steps 1 to 7).
2. A user, or a program, deletes the file at its path on disk 2 directly, replaces it, or writes into it, as the view now presents it from there.
3. Step 8 finds the copy on disk 1 as it was copied: it goes to disk 1's trash and is purged at once, or, the trash with no room, is removed at its path.
4. The file's content is on no disk and in no trash, where a deletion made directly on one disk reaches the other copies only in their trash, kept for the retention (invariant 10); for a replacement or a write, the content the copy left held, the file's version before it, is gone the same way, where a modification sends the other copies to the trash as `overwritten` (§15.4).

The window is the width of steps 7 and 8, the program's writes held back meanwhile, and needs a deletion made directly on the target, or through mergerfs: through Atoll's own view the deletion would wait for the writer's lock. The test deletes the moved copy from the fault hook of the trash's rename on disk 1: nothing is left anywhere. The remedies proposed then, each checked against every invariant: (a) `move-look`: right before the purge, and before the removal at its path, the target's path is looked at, on a disk the view can read, as flaw 8's `xfer-look` looks at the data copies: one that no longer holds the copy the move placed as it placed it, its inode, size, times and change time, keeps the copy left, in the trash unpurged for its retention, or at its path (`ViewMove`, `ViewMoveLive` passed with it, and so did the flaw's pool, 71 states); (b) no purge at once, the copy left staying in the trash as `surplus`, the first that `trash.max_size` purges, as flaw 8's (b). As flaw 8's look, the look and the purge are one step in the model; in the code a deletion in the width of one `unlink` after the look still reaches the copy left, a limit.

### Flaw 15: the view's check resolves a move whose file the program still holds open

Fixed on 2026-11-23 with the remedy the maintainer chose, (a) `move-held` below: `specs.md` §21.2, The view's journal and What a failure leaves; `writer.letGo`, which, once a move returns left planned (`moving.planned`: its part not removed, the target lost at step 5, the catalog at step 6, or step 8 on a disk lost), keeps the writer's hold on its row (flaw 10's `held`, which `Recheck` skips) for the file, `File.moving`, until `File.Close` lets go of it, as the file's own row of `view_ops` stays `open` until then; a stop of the view lets go of everything, and its start resolves it. `FlawMoveRecheckOpen.cfg` expects `pass` now, a regression instance (298 states before the remedy, `ViewPresentsLatest`). Tests, each failing first: `TestARecheckWaitsForAMovedFileToBeClosed` (`internal/view/writer`, after `TestFlawARecheckMovesAFileTheProgramHoldsOpen` behind the build tag `flaws`: a check while open leaves the move planned and the program's write in place presented; closed, the next check resolves it, the view presenting that write) and `TestARecheckWaitsForAMovedFileToBeClosedAfterItsSwap` (a move left planned at step 8).

§21.2, What a failure leaves: "the same on a target that cannot be relied on → `ENOSPC` → the move `planned`, presenting the full disk's copy, for the view's reprise", the file still open on its disk; and "Its check every 30 s does the same while it runs, under the writer's lock, for what a disk that could not be relied on left planned, once that disk is there again, but for a part it still writes or closes and a move during its copy". A move that stopped is not during its copy any more: `writer.Recheck` holds back only rows `isHeld`, which `move` holds while it runs.

1. A new file the view made on disk 1, written, still open; a write disk 1 answers with `ENOSPC` starts the move to disk 2.
2. At step 5 the part is renamed to the path on disk 2, and the sync after it fails, as a USB disk dropping answers it (`EIO`): the move stays planned, its `create` planned, the program gets `ENOSPC` and the file stays open on disk 1, presented from there.
3. Disk 2 comes back on a mount of its own and confirms; the view's check resolves the move as a start does (`repriseMoveCreate`): disk 2's path holds the part, by inode, and disk 1's copy has its size and time, as the program could not write: disk 1's copy goes to its trash, unpurged, the move done, and the view presents disk 2's copy.
4. The program, which still holds the file open on disk 1, writes in place, as a muxer writes its header last, which a full disk takes as it needs no new block: the write goes to the copy in disk 1's trash. The view presents the file without it, the pass takes in disk 2's copy, and what the program wrote last is in a trash entry, purged with its retention (invariant 14; invariant 1 once purged).

The window is the time between that check and the program's close, once a disk failed at the very sync of step 5 and came back while the program held the file: rare, and what it loses is the last writes of one file. The test fails the sync of the file's new name on disk 2 through the fault layer, then runs the check and a write in place: the view presents disk 2's copy, without it, the write in disk 1's trash. The remedies proposed then, each checked against every invariant: (a) `move-held`: a move whose file the program still holds open through the handle that made it is held, as a part being written is, and the check resolves it only once the file is closed, or the next start does (`ViewMove`, `ViewMoveLive` passed with it, and so did the flaw's pool, 597 states); (b) the check undoes such a move instead, both copies staying and the full disk's presented, as when the program wrote since; (c) the check gives the handle the target's copy, as step 7 does, which needs the writer to swap a handle under the program while it may be writing.

Modelling mistakes, fixed: the program wrote only to a file at its path, where a handle writes its inode wherever it went (found reading the model against `Replication.tla`'s `VData`, before TLC ran it, and the way to flaw 15); a write into a file a user deleted under the program's handle was counted lost, which is the user's to lose (`NoUserDataLost`, 84 states); the check that the target holds what the move placed compared contents, where a user may write into the target's copy since (`Inv2_MovedFirst`, `ViewMoveLive`, 2,289 states): it compares the copy placed, by object; the size and time a write gives a file counted per object, so that a user's write on the target and the program's on the full disk gave both copies one stamp, which real times never do (`ViewPresentsLatest`, `ViewMove`, 11,202 states): each write's stamp is its own now; and the handle after step 7 looked the target's path up, where it is the part's own, opened at step 1.

How sensitive it is, each taken out on its own in a copy not kept: the comparison of the copy left with what was copied, right before its move to the trash (`NoUserDataLost` in `ViewMove`, 12,998 states: a user's write into the copy left purged with it); the sync of the copy at its name on the target before the trash is journaled (`ViewPresentsLatest`, `ViewMove`, 2,972 states: a power cut once the copy left is in its trash takes the target's name back, and the view presents nothing); and, once the remedies of flaws 15 and 17 were what every instance does, each taken out on its own: the check holding a move whose file is open (`ViewPresentsLatest` in `FlawMoveRecheckOpen`, 298 states) and the look at the target before the purge or the removal (`NoUserDataLost` in `FlawMoveTargetGone`, 58).

What it leaves out: the daemon's passes and the take-in; the choice of the target and its room; parts written in parallel (dropped first); a hard link, folders, extended attributes; the program's writes made durable as they come, as a user's are in every model, so that a power cut takes nothing the program wrote; the look right before a removal and the removal as one step. And of the view, its rename, its replacement onto a name it presents and its folders renamed or removed on every disk at once (below, Atoll's own view, its renames and folders).

## Atoll's own view, its renames and folders: ViewNames.tla

A model of its own, `ViewNames.tla` with `MCViewNames.tla` (2026-11-23), for what `Replication.tla`'s view and `ViewMove.tla` leave out of §21.2: a file renamed through the view, a rename onto a name it presents, which replaces the file there, and a folder renamed or removed on every disk that holds it at once (`internal/view/writer`: `Rename`, `renameFile`, `replace`, `renameFolder`, `Rmdir`, and `reprise.go`). Two disks; folder names at the root (D, E) and file names in a folder (x, y); on each disk its folders and the files in each, by object (an inode, with the version it holds), and its trash; the catalog's files, their copies (`ok`, a deletion, a copy to make again or to refresh; where each sits, its `old_lpath`), the entries of the folders and the folder jobs of each disk, each with what the view had done when it was decided; the view's journal, `view_ops` (kind, disk, paths, the inode a file's operation names or a folder's its holder's folder, a replacement's copy sent to the trash and its operation `trash`, state, taken in), its process and its handles. The view, one operation at a time, each journaled before it runs: a file renamed on the disk it presents it from, without replacing; a replacement, its look at the copy presented at the new name, that copy to the trash against the look, then the rename; a folder renamed or removed on each holder in turn, a disk with no handle or no folder skipped, one whose name is taken failed, one that cannot be relied on leaving the operation planned when it moved nothing, the journal naming, before each holder's rename until one went through, that holder and its folder by inode (flaw 19's remedy); its start resolving what it left planned, and its check every 30 s what a disk lost left planned once it is back on a mount of its own. What it presents, as `view.Resolve` lays its operations over the catalog, newest first, a folder renamed presenting below its new name the catalog's rows below the old one. A user, directly on either disk, once: moves, makes, deletes, saves atomically or writes in place a file; makes, removes or renames a folder. `atoll mount` stopped short, a disk lost and mounted again, between any two steps. A pass, disk after disk: its walk, which waits at the names of the view's operations planned on that disk (`jobs.viewPlannedOn`), finds each copy where it is, moved, replaced or gone, and the folders found renamed, each decided once a later walk of that disk finds it still (the debounce, `pass.isDue`), and only when no copy of the file on another disk waits at its name or shows a change not yet due (`solver.Decide`, `folderWaits`), nor against the last folder's rename of the view at its names that stands on its disk, its disk having missed it (flaw 23's remedy); a folder found renamed keeps its entry until then; a folder decided gives each other disk with its entry a job, which the walks move on (`catalog.moveFolderJobs`); then a folder's rename of the view no walk finds, decided as a folder renamed on the disk its journal names (observation 5's remedy); then on each disk its walk found, on the mount it found, its folder jobs, each run as one operation when the folder its entry records is at the old name and the new is free, and no operation of the view not taken in is at its names but the same rename and those before it that it undoes, dropped otherwise, the last folder's rename of the view at its names that stands on its disk, which that disk missed, made at once, never queued (flaw 23's remedy), and what follows, a copy at a time, a copy's rename waiting for an operation of the view at its names planned when the pass began or journaled since (flaw 22's remedy), or for the last folder's rename of the view at its names that stands on its disk (observation 5's), and a folder of Atoll's own that its rename leaves empty removed; then the take-in of the view's operations the pass read when it began, done then (`view.TakenIn`), an operation a user undid directly taken in as it is (flaws 20 and 21's), a folder's rename waiting for every disk that holds it and for a disk that missed it (observation 5's), and while the catalog holds a file below its old name (flaw 23's), and a copy a replacement sent to the trash the view's, as a deletion's is (flaw 18's).

| Invariant | Checks |
|---|---|
| `NoUserDataLost` | every version a user wrote is at a path or in the trash of some disk, but for what a user's own write, save or deletion destroyed |
| `ViewJournalTrue` | §21.2, The view's journal: an operation is done only once what it names happened on a disk, and undone only when it did not, a folder's on any of its holders ("done once it reached one"); a verdict on what the view did not make itself, as a reprise's, must be so, and undone is wrong only of a move the view made, while no user acted directly |
| `ViewNamesKept` | invariant 15, §15.12: no folder job a pass decided before an operation of the view takes a folder from the name that operation gave it |
| `ViewNamesSettled` | §21.2, §15.12: once all is still, nothing of the view planned or not taken in, no pass under way, every disk there, no job, no change a walk would find or found and has not decided, nothing to follow, and as long as no user acted directly, the catalog holds no file below the old name of the last folder's rename the view made (observation 5) |
| `EventuallySettled` | liveness, with fairness: no operation of the view stays planned, and each done is taken in at last |

The module also holds `ViewReadable`, that a file the view presents can be opened there once nothing of it is planned, which no instance checks (observation 4, below), and `BackKeepsUser`, that the rename a pass makes on a disk that missed a folder's rename of the view never takes a folder back from the name a user gave it directly there, which no instance checks either, as flaw 23's remedy does not keep it (below).

Measured on 2026-11-24 with `test/tla/run.sh`, 2 workers, other agents' containers on the machine, in the run of the whole set (above), once the remedies of flaws 18 to 23 and observation 5's were what every instance does, `Fixes` empty; the flaws' instances are regression instances now, the states before their remedies in their sections below. `ViewNamesSettled` is checked by every instance with folders renamed, `ViewFolders` too since flaw 23's remedy.

| Instance | Expects | What it explores | States | Time |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ViewNames` | pass | D/x and D/y on both disks: the view renames them or replaces one by the other, twice at most; a user acting once directly on either disk; `atoll mount` stopped once, a disk lost once | 4,302,691 | 114 s |
| `ViewNamesRestore` | pass | `ViewNames` with `deletes.direct = "restore"`, one operation of the view | 668,886 | 22 s |
| `ViewFolders` | pass | D/x on both disks, D/y on disk 2 alone: a file renamed or replaced, the folder renamed on every disk, twice at most; `atoll mount` stopped once, a disk lost once | 271,377 | 15 s |
| `ViewFoldersUser` | pass | `ViewFolders`' pool, the folders renamed or removed through the view, twice at most, a user acting once directly on either disk (a folder made, renamed or removed, a file moved or deleted); `atoll mount` stopped once | 360,961 | 21 s |
| `ViewNamesLive` | pass | liveness: `ViewNames` with `deletes.direct = "restore"` and no user | 16,412 | 7 s |
| `ViewFoldersLive` | pass | liveness: the folder renamed or removed on every disk, `atoll mount` stopped once, a disk lost once and mounted again in time | 19,359 | 7 s |
| `ViewFoldersUserLive` | pass | liveness: D/x on both disks, the folder renamed through the view, a user making or renaming a folder directly once, `atoll mount` stopped once | 8,986 | 4 s |
| `FlawViewReplaceRestore` | pass | flaw 18, fixed: a replacement through the view, `deletes.direct = "restore"`, with the remedy | 175 | 2 s |
| `FlawViewFolderReprise` | pass | flaw 19, fixed: a folder's rename stopped before any disk renamed it, a user making the new name directly, with the remedy | 3,186 | 2 s |
| `FlawViewRenameUndone` | pass | flaw 20, fixed: a file renamed through the view, moved back directly, with the remedy | 604 | 2 s |
| `FlawViewFolderTaken` | pass | flaw 21, fixed: a folder renamed through the view onto a name a user made directly on one disk, with the remedy | 652 | 1 s |
| `FlawViewFolderJobBack` | pass | flaw 22, fixed: a folder renamed through the view and back, a disk lost meanwhile, with the remedy | 44,624 | 4 s |
| `FlawViewFolderRenameBack` | pass | observation 5, fixed: D/x on both disks, the folder renamed through the view and back, `atoll mount` stopped once, a disk lost once, with the remedy | 175,892 | 8 s |
| `FlawViewFolderRenameBackLive` | pass | observation 5, fixed, liveness: the same pool, the disk lost mounted again in time | 175,892 | 26 s |
| `FlawViewFolderBackLost` | pass | flaw 23, fixed: D/x on both disks, D/y on disk 2 alone, the folder renamed through the view and back, a disk lost once, with the remedy | 62,178 | 5 s |
| `FlawViewFolderBackLostLive` | pass | flaw 23, fixed, liveness: the same pool, the disk lost mounted again in time | 62,178 | 15 s |

Each flaw had a test of the code behind the build tag `flaws`, in `internal/jobs/flaws_test.go`, seen failing (`make model-flaws`), the runner having no FUSE to drive the view; each remedy proposed was checked against every invariant, alone, in the flaw's pool, and with the others in every instance above. The maintainer chose them on 2026-11-24, the first proposed every time but for flaw 23, whose restricted variant he put first did not hold (its section, below) (DONE.md); with them in the code and specs.md, the tests are regular tests of `internal/jobs`, each seen failing first on the code before its remedy, with cases of faults and kill -9 at the steps around it, and loop-device tests run them through the view served over FUSE on every driver (`test/loopdev/viewnames_test.go`). No black-box scenario can reach them, as the runner mounts no view. Two remedies are in the code as the model then found the code could do them, each checked again against every invariant: flaw 19's journal names the holder of each rename until one went through, and flaw 22's folder job goes for any operation of the view not taken in at its names (their sections, below).

### Flaw 18: a replacement through the view is kept on the other disk under `deletes.direct = "restore"`

Fixed on 2026-11-24 with the remedy the maintainer chose, (a) `replace-gone` below: `specs.md` §21.2, A rename and Taken in; `jobs.sentToTrash`, which gives the copy a replacement sent to the trash, its operation `trash`'s `file_key` (`catalog.ViewOp.OpFileKey`), as the view's, so that its deletion propagates whatever `deletes.direct` says, and `deletedThroughView` its file's trash job the same. `FlawViewReplaceRestore.cfg` expects `pass` now, a regression instance (86 states before the remedy, liveness). Tests, each failing first: `TestAReplacementThroughTheViewReachesTheOtherDisk` (which was `TestFlawAReplacementThroughTheViewIsKeptOnTheOtherDisk` behind the build tag `flaws`), under `propagate` and `restore`; `TestAReplacementThroughTheViewWithAFaultOnTheOtherDisk` (the other disk's move to its trash failing once, `EIO` or out of room); `TestAReplacementThroughTheViewWithAKillOnTheOtherDisk` (kill -9 at each point of the trash job and the rename there); on loop devices, `TestAReplacementThroughTheViewOnEachDriver`.

§21.2, A rename: "A rename onto a name the view presents replaces it ...: the copy presented there goes to the trash, `overwritten`, the rename is made on its source's disk"; and Taken in: "A copy the view sent to the trash ... is gone by the user ... and its deletion propagates whatever `deletes.direct` says". `jobs.readViewOps` marks as the view's only a deletion's copy (`view_ops.kind = unlink`), not the copy a replacement sent to the trash.

1. Files a at D/x and b at D/y on disks 1 and 2, `deletes.direct = "restore"`. Through the view, x is renamed onto y: on disk 1, the copy of b at D/y goes to the trash, then a's copy is renamed to D/y.
2. The next walk of disk 1 finds a's copy moved to D/y and b's gone, a name another row's copy now holds: gone directly, as no deletion of the view names it, and under `restore` the decision is Kept, b's copy to make again on disk 1, onto a name a's copy holds.
3. a's rename never reaches disk 2, whose D/y still holds b's copy; on disk 2, D/x still holds a's. The catalog never presents at D/x and D/y what the view does, and the replacement is never taken in, as long as the view runs (`EventuallySettled`). Nothing is lost: b's version is on disk 2 and in disk 1's trash.

The test, `TestFlawAReplacementThroughTheViewIsKeptOnTheOtherDisk`, replaces through the view and runs six passes: disk 2 keeps b's version at `Films/b.mkv` and a's at `Films/a.mkv`, and the operation stays open. The remedies proposed, each checked against every invariant: (a) `replace-gone`: the copy a replacement sent to the trash is the view's, as a deletion's is (its operation `trash` names it), and its file's deletion propagates whatever `deletes.direct` says, as §21.2 says of "a copy the view sent to the trash" (`FlawViewReplaceRestore` passed with it, 104 states); (b) §21.2 changed to read a replacement as §15.4's modification of the file replaced, the copy at its name `replaced`, its other copies `stale`, and the file renamed's other copies deleted, which needs the walk to tell a's copy at D/y from a user's own: not modelled.

### Flaw 19: the reprise of a folder's rename calls it done by any folder at the new name

Fixed on 2026-11-24 with the remedy the maintainer chose, (a) `folder-key` below, in the form the code can hold: `specs.md` §9 and §21.2; `writer.renameFolder`, whose journal names, in a commit of its own before each holder's rename until one went through, that holder and its folder's inode there (`catalog.NameViewFolder`, `view_ops.disk_id` and `file_key`, the crash point `folder-named`), and `writer.resolve`, which calls a folder's rename done only when the disk its journal names holds at the new name the folder of that inode, undone otherwise, one that names none having renamed nothing. The proposal's other holders, a holder holding a folder at the new name and none at the old, went: the code records one folder, and TLC found that part taking for the rename a user's own rename of the folder directly on another holder, and a holder the first rename never reached (`ViewJournalTrue`, `ViewFoldersUserLive` 437 states, `ViewFolders` 3,493), where naming the holder of each rename until one went through needs it not. `FlawViewFolderReprise.cfg` expects `pass` now, a regression instance (196 states before the remedy, `ViewJournalTrue`). Tests, each failing first: `TestTheRepriseOfAFolderRenameKnowsItsFolder` (which was `TestFlawTheRepriseOfAFolderRenameTakesAUsersFolderForIt` behind the build tag `flaws`): every disk failing the rename, then a user's folder at the new name; stopped at `folder-named`, then the same; stopped once the first holder renamed it; the first holder's new name taken, the second renamed, then stopped; `TestTheRepriseOfAFolderRenameWhoseLookFails` (`EIO` at the look, the rename left planned, then resolved); on loop devices, `TestAFolderRenameThroughTheViewRepriseOnEachDriver`.

§21.2, The view's journal: `atoll mount` "resolves what it left `planned` at its start ... by the table of §17.2 for what moves a name". `writer.renameFolder` journals a folder's rename with no inode, `Disk` its first holder; `reprise.go` calls it done when that disk has a folder at the new name, any.

1. D, with D/x, on disks 1 and 2. The view renames D to E: journaled, then every disk fails the rename as one that cannot be relied on (or atoll mount is stopped short before the first), and the operation stays planned, nothing moved.
2. A user makes a folder E directly on disk 1.
3. The view's start, or its check, resolves the operation: disk 1 holds a folder at E, so done, though no disk renamed D (`ViewJournalTrue`). The view presents E with D's files, from the catalog's rows below D, and nothing at D, where every disk holds the folder, until a pass takes the operation in, which it never can while D is there: the user's files are presented at no name they are at.

The test, `TestFlawTheRepriseOfAFolderRenameTakesAUsersFolderForIt`, fails the renames with `EIO`, makes E on the first holder, runs `Reprise`: the operation is done, and the view presents nothing at `Shows/D/e1.mkv`. The remedy proposed, checked against every invariant: (a) `folder-key`: the operation records its first holder's folder inode, as a file's does (`file_key`), and the reprise calls it done when that disk holds that folder at the new name, or another holder holds a folder at the new name and none at the old (`FlawViewFolderReprise` passed with it, 1,772 states); the other holders count, as a rename that missed its first holder, one with no handle, is done all the same.

### Flaw 20: a rename through the view that a user moves back directly is never taken in

Fixed on 2026-11-24 with the remedy the maintainer chose, (a) `view-moot` below: `specs.md` §21.2, Taken in; `jobs.mootViewOps`, which gives `view.TakenIn` the operations a user undid directly, taken in as they are, as the model has them: a file's rename or replacement whose new name its disk, looked at by this pass, no longer holds the inode of; a folder's rename that no disk whose entry holds the folder at either name holds at the new name any more, the disk its journal names by the folder of its inode, another by any folder; and one whose old name a disk holds a folder at again that no job of that disk will move, on the disk its journal names another than the folder of its inode, elsewhere one the catalog holds nothing of that disk below (`catalog.MadeBelow`). `FlawViewRenameUndone.cfg` expects `pass` now, a regression instance (371 states before the remedy, liveness). Tests, each failing first: `TestARenameThroughTheViewUndoneDirectlyIsTakenIn` (which was `TestFlawARenameThroughTheViewMovedBackIsNeverTakenIn` behind the build tag `flaws`): moved back, and moved on to another name; `TestARenameUndoneDirectlyWhoseLookFails` (the look at the new name failing with `EIO`, the operation not taken in on it, then taken in); on loop devices, `TestARenameThroughTheViewUndoneDirectlyOnEachDriver`.

§21.2, Taken in: each operation done "is taken in ... once the catalog alone presents at each path it touched what the view presents with it". What the view presents at a file renamed's new name is "what its disk holds there, as a look finds it", and at its old name nothing.

1. D/x on disks 1 and 2. The view renames D/x to D/y on disk 1: done.
2. Before a pass takes it in, a user moves D/y back to D/x directly on disk 1.
3. The passes find disk 1's copy where its row says, D/x: nothing to decide. The view presents D/y from disk 1, which holds nothing there, and nothing at D/x; the catalog presents the file at D/x. They never agree: the operation is never taken in (`EventuallySettled`), and the view presents the file at neither name, for as long as it runs. The same holds of a folder's rename moved back directly (`ViewFoldersUserLive` without the second part of the remedy, below).

The test, `TestFlawARenameThroughTheViewMovedBackIsNeverTakenIn`, renames through the view, moves the file back on its disk, and runs six passes: the operation stays open, the view presents nothing at either name. The remedy proposed, checked against every invariant: (a) `view-moot`: a done rename or replacement whose new name its disk, there, no longer holds the inode it names, once a pass walked that disk, is taken in as it is, the catalog then presenting what the disks hold; likewise a folder's rename none of whose holders holds the folder it named at the new name any more, and one whose old name a disk that is there holds a folder at again, with no job of that disk to move it (flaw 21) (`FlawViewRenameUndone` passed with it, 361 states).

### Flaw 21: a folder renamed through the view onto a name a user made directly is never taken in

Fixed on 2026-11-24 with the remedy the maintainer chose, (a) `view-moot` below, flaw 20's, without the view's refusal: the old folder left empty on the disk whose new name a user's folder took, with no job to move it, makes the rename one a user undid directly. `FlawViewFolderTaken.cfg` expects `pass` now, a regression instance (373 states before the remedy, liveness). Tests, each failing first: `TestAFolderRenamedThroughTheViewOntoAUsersFolderIsTakenIn` (which was `TestFlawAFolderRenamedThroughTheViewOntoAUsersFolderIsNeverTakenIn` behind the build tag `flaws`), and the same with the daemon killed at each point of the renames of the copies that follow one by one; on loop devices, `TestAFolderRenamedThroughTheViewOntoAUsersFolderOnEachDriver`.

§21.2, A rename: "a folder is renamed on every disk that holds it, and the pass records each as a folder renamed there"; Taken in: "A folder renamed or removed on several disks is one operation, of the first disk it reached, done once it reached one; a pass takes it to a disk it missed as a folder renamed directly on the others (§15.12)." The view looks only at what it presents before a rename (`ErrExists`), not at what a disk holds there.

1. D, with D/x, on disks 1 and 2. A user makes E directly on disk 1; no pass has recorded it, so the view presents nothing at E.
2. The view renames D to E: refused on disk 1, E being there, made on disk 2: done, as it reached one.
3. The pass records disk 2's rename and queues disk 1's job, which can never run there, E being taken; it goes, and disk 1's copies follow one by one, into the user's E. D stays on disk 1, empty, its entry with it.
4. The view presents nothing at D, renamed from; the catalog presents the folder D of disk 1. The operation is never taken in (`EventuallySettled`), and the empty D stays on disk 1, where the view never shows it.

The test, `TestFlawAFolderRenamedThroughTheViewOntoAUsersFolderIsNeverTakenIn`, makes `Shows/E` on t1, renames `Shows/D` to `Shows/E` through the view and runs six passes: the operation stays open. The remedies proposed, each checked against every invariant: (a) `view-moot`, flaw 20's, whose part for a folder whose old name holds a folder again on a disk with no job to move it takes the operation in, the catalog then presenting the empty D, which a user can remove (`FlawViewFolderTaken` passed with it, 397 states); (b) the view refusing the rename, `EEXIST`, when a disk that holds the folder has anything at the new name, which it would not present: tried on an earlier version of the model, it was not enough alone (`FlawViewFolderTaken` still broke `EventuallySettled`), as a user may make E right after that look; with (a), not needed.

### Flaw 22: a folder's job decided before a rename back through the view carries the folder from the name the view gave it

Fixed on 2026-11-24 with the remedy the maintainer chose, (a) `job-view-held` below, in the form the code can hold: `specs.md` §15.12 and §21.2; `catalog.BeginHeldRename`, which reads in the transaction that would journal a rename of a pass the view's operations not taken in at, below or above either name, but that very rename (`ViewHold`, `ErrViewHolds`): a folder's job goes for any of them, as a job keeps no mark of when it was decided, a later pass deciding anew from what the disks hold, the copies following one by one meanwhile (`jobs.runFolderJob`); a copy's rename waits where it is, with no outcome, for one of an id at or past the first planned when the pass began, or the next to come (`pass.viewSince`, `catalog.NextViewOp`). TLC checked the hold of every operation not taken in against every invariant, in every instance and in flaw 22's pool. `FlawViewFolderJobBack.cfg` expects `pass` now, a regression instance (6,894 states before the remedy, `ViewNamesKept`). Tests, each failing first: `TestAFolderJobGoesForARenameBackThroughTheView` (which was `TestFlawAFolderJobCarriesAFolderFromTheNameTheViewGaveItBack` behind the build tag `flaws`), atoll mount stopped short between the two disks of the rename back, t1 lost, the job queued and going once t1 is back, the rename back then done by its reprise, by the folder's inode, which fails too with the copy's wait taken out; `TestACycleOfRenamesWaitsForTheView`, an operation of the view journaled while a pass runs holding the two renames of an exchange. A copy's rename reads the hold before it makes the folders missing on the way to its target, and again in its transaction; an exchange of two copies' names (§15.12, A cycle), which the model leaves out, journals its two renames in one transaction now, each waiting so.

Observation 5, for the maintainer, found by that test, which no invariant of the model stated then: the rename back lost all the same, once its reprise called it done, by t1's copy following the first rename; fixed since (below, Observation 5).

§15.12, Folders: a folder renamed on one disk gives each other disk that holds it a job, which "runs when D is a folder on that disk, the one its last walk found there by its inode"; §21.2: "A pass waits at the paths of the view's operations under way". A folder's operation is journaled on its first holder alone, so a walk of another holder does not wait for it (`jobs.viewPlannedOn`), and `jobs.folderReady` asks nothing of the view.

1. D, with D/x, on disks 1 and 2. The view renames D to E on both: done.
2. The view renames E back to D: journaled on disk 1, its first holder, and made there; atoll mount is stopped short before disk 2, and disk 1 is lost. The rename back stays planned, on disk 1 alone.
3. The passes walk disk 2, which nothing of the view holds, and find the first rename there, D to E, twice: decided, a job D to E queued for disk 1, away, from its entry of D.
4. Disk 1 comes back; the next pass runs the job before anything resolves the rename back: disk 1's D, the folder its entry records, goes to E, the name the view had taken it from (`ViewNamesKept`). The view's check then finds no folder at D on disk 1 and calls the rename back undone; the folder is at E on both disks, and the user's rename back is lost, as after a stop it may be, but by Atoll's own job, not by the stop.

TLC's trace is the same with the view slow rather than stopped. The test, `TestFlawAFolderJobCarriesAFolderFromTheNameTheViewGaveItBack`, stops the rename back between the disks (a panic at t2's first call, standing for kill -9), makes t1 missing for three passes, then brings it back: its folder is at `Shows/E`. The remedy proposed, checked against every invariant: (a) `job-view-held`: a folder's job decided before an operation of the view at, below or above its names, planned when the pass decided it or journaled since, done or not and taken in or not, goes when it would run, read in the transaction that would journal its rename, for a later pass to decide anew from what the disks hold; and a copy's rename by a pass waits for one planned when the pass began or journaled since, one done before, or that is the same rename, being what the job carries out (`FlawViewFolderJobBack` passed with it, 22,928 states).

### Observation 5: a folder's rename back through the view made on one disk alone is lost

Fixed on 2026-11-24 with the fix the maintainer chose, the remedy sketched below, in the fuller form the code and TLC showed it needs, the parts beyond the sketch to confirm (DONE.md): `specs.md` §15.12 (a copy's rename, a folder's job, Folders) and §21.2 (Taken in); `jobs.viewFolderMoves`, `viewKeeps` and `missedViewOps`, and `catalog.viewHoldsRename`. The invariant `ViewNamesSettled` states what it breaks: once all is still, and as long as no user acted directly, the catalog holds no file below the old name of the last folder's rename the view made. `FlawViewFolderRenameBack.cfg` and its liveness instance `FlawViewFolderRenameBackLive.cfg` expect `pass`, regression instances (70,317 and 70,318 states before the remedy, `ViewNamesSettled` in both).

The sketch was: "a pass that finds a folder's rename of the view done on a disk where the catalog's entry already holds the folder at the new name takes it to the disks it missed, as a folder renamed there". The form in the end, five parts, each needed (How sensitive it is, below):

- (a) `view-back`, the sketch: a folder's rename of the view done, not taken in, the last operation of the view at its names, whose disk this pass walked and found holding the folder its journal names, by its inode, at the new name, where that disk's entry already holds it and none holds one at the old name, and the walk found no folder renamed there, or at neither name, at, below or above either name, is a folder renamed on that disk, once the catalog holds a file below the old name, or another disk a copy or an entry below it with no job of that rename. It is decided from the rows as the walks left them, a real rename whatever copies came with it, as it is the folder the view presents: the files below the old name follow it, a copy of that disk its row puts at the new path already following with its file, and each other disk gets its job. One a decision of the same pass makes so, as when that pass decided the first rename from the other disk, waits for the next pass.
- (b) `view-kept`: a copy's rename by a pass waits while it would take the copy from at or below the new name of that last folder's rename of the view, done and not taken in, to at or below its old name, as long as the disk the rename names holds there the folder it names, as this pass's walk of that disk found: the rename stands on that disk, and the catalog comes to it.
- (c) `view-missed`: that rename is not taken in while another disk holds at the old name, and nothing at the new, the folder its entry records at either name: that disk missed it, and the catalog may present what the view does before any walk of it found so.
- (d) `view-away`: a folder's rename of the view is taken in once every disk that holds the folder, by the catalog's entries at either name, a disk failed but, was looked at, not only every disk the pass could read: one away may have missed it.
- (e) `view-same`: a rename of a pass that carries out an operation of the view not taken in, as the job of a rename back does, is held back by the later operations at its names alone, not by the earlier ones it undoes (`catalog.viewHoldsRename`, a folder's job's and a copy's).

The traces, each found by TLC on the way and each a test of the code, seen failing first: (1) observation 5's own, below, `TestARenameBackThroughTheViewReachesTheDiskItMissed`, with the job's rename on t2 failing once with `EIO` and the daemon killed at `decided`, `journaled`, `moved`, `target-synced` and `source-synced`; (2) no disk lost, a pass while the rename back is planned finding the first rename on t2, and the pass after the reprise deciding it, which carried t1's copy in that very pass (b), `TestARenameBackThroughTheViewWhileAPassDecidesTheFirst`; (3) no pass between the renames and the rename back's end, the rename back failing on t2 with `EIO` or `atoll mount` stopped short: the catalog presented what the view does, both were taken in at the first pass, and the next read t2's folder at E as a user's rename (c), `TestARenameBackThroughTheViewNoPassRecorded`; (4) t2 lost from the rename back's failure for two passes, which took both in (d), `TestARenameBackThroughTheViewWithT2Away`; (5) a pass between the rename and the rename back that found the first and could not take it in, whose rename back's job for t2 it then held back for good (e), `TestARenameBackThroughTheViewAfterAPassFoundTheFirst`. And `TestARenameBackThroughTheViewMadeWhereTheFolderIsEmpty`, the folder empty on t1, which no copy came with (a's real rename), and `TestARenameBackThroughTheViewWaitsForACopyGone`, a copy on t2 below the old name deleted directly under `deletes.direct = "restore"`, the rename back waiting for its debounce, t1's copies kept where the view put them (b). No loop-device test: every trace needs the rename back to stop short, or fail, between the disks, a kill -9 or a fault the loop-device tests do not put into the view served over FUSE.

Observation 5, as recorded: once the rename back's reprise calls it done, t1's copy, behind at D since the passes decided the first rename from t2, follows it to E, as no operation planned holds it then, and the folder ends at E on both disks, the rename back lost all the same. The catalog never recorded t1's folder at E, which the first rename and the rename back made and unmade there while no pass looked, and so no walk of t1 finds the rename back, and none takes it to t2, which it missed. It needs `atoll mount` stopped short within a folder's rename back and its first holder lost at that moment; the traces above need less.

1. D, with D/x, on disks 1 and 2. The view renames D to E on both: done.
2. The view renames E back to D: made on disk 1, then `atoll mount` is stopped short before disk 2, and disk 1 is lost.
3. The passes find the first rename on disk 2 and decide it: the file at E/x, disk 1's copy behind at D/x, its job D to E going when disk 1 is back (flaw 22's remedy).
4. The view starts again, and its reprise calls the rename back done, disk 1 holding the folder it names at D. The next pass renames disk 1's copy from D/x to E/x, the file's path; the rename back is taken in as one a user undid, the folder at E on both disks (`ViewNamesSettled`).

### Flaw 23: a folder's rename back through the view missed by a disk lost is not kept

Found on 2026-11-24 by `ViewNamesSettled` in `ViewFolders`' pool once observation 5's remedy was in the model; `FlawViewFolderBackLost.cfg` (57,620 states, `ViewNamesSettled`; 45,974 before the two modelling mistakes the code's experiment showed were fixed, below). Fixed on 2026-11-24 with the maintainer's remedy, (c) and (d) below, his restricted variant of (c), put first, not holding (The remedy, below).

§21.2, Taken in: "a pass takes it to a disk it missed as a folder renamed directly on the others (§15.12)"; §15.12, Folders: a folder found renamed is a user's rename, which every disk that holds it follows.

1. D/x on disks 1 and 2, D/y on disk 2 alone. The view renames D to E on both: done. It renames E back to D: journaled, then disk 1 is lost before its turn, which it misses.
2. Meanwhile the passes find the first rename on disk 2 and decide it, x and y at E, disk 1's copy of x behind at D, its job queued; then the view renames disk 2's folder back to D: the rename back done.
3. Disk 1 is back, its folder at E, which its entry records at D. Its walk finds D renamed to E, the first rename, and decides it as a user's rename: y, which disk 1 holds no copy of, follows it to E, disk 2's copy of y behind at D/y. Disk 2's walk finds the rename back and decides it: x follows back to D, and disk 1 gets its job, which runs; y, whose copy on disk 2 decided nothing, stays at E.
4. The rename back is taken in, no disk holding the folder at E; disk 2's copy of y is then renamed to E/y, the file's path: y at E, x at D, though no user undid the rename back (`ViewNamesSettled`).

The test, `TestFlawARenameBackMissedByALostDiskIsNotKept` (build tag `flaws`, then `internal/jobs/flaws_test.go`, now `TestARenameBackMissedByALostDiskIsKept`), runs it in the code with the rename back's failure on t1 an `EIO`: the code ends otherwise, as its passes decide every walk before any folder, but no better: e1 as two files, `Shows/D/e1.mkv` and `Shows/E/e1.mkv`, t1 holding `Shows/E`, two operations of the view never taken in; on `beta`'s code before observation 5's remedy, the same, with one. Remedies proposed, each in the model as an entry of `Fixes` and checked by TLC on 2026-11-24 against every invariant, alone in the flaw's pool and with the others in every instance of the module, `ViewFolders` checking `ViewNamesSettled` too and the flaw's pool also with fairness and `EventuallySettled`; the counts are the model's with its two mistakes fixed (Modelling mistakes, below), each of (a) and (b) giving what it gave before them:

- (a) `back-wait`: a folder found renamed on a disk, and a copy found moved there, from at or below the new name of the last folder's rename of the view, done and not taken in and standing on its disk, to at or below its old name, waits, as observation 5's (b) has a copy's rename wait, that disk having missed the rename; and that disk gets a job from the old name to the new, which runs on a folder there that no entry records at the old name, as `jobs.folderReady` does. It does not hold: alone, `ViewNamesSettled` in the flaw's pool (68,473 states) and in `FlawViewFolderJobBack` (49,620), `ViewNamesKept` in `FlawViewFolderRenameBack` (72,180) and `ViewFolders` (71,009); before the two mistakes were fixed, the same, in `FlawViewFolderRenameBackLive` too, alone or with (b). Its job is flaw 22's again: disk 2 misses the first rename, D to E, lost at its turn, and gets its job; the view renames E back to D on disk 1, while disk 2 never left D; a pass takes both renames in, the catalog presenting D as the view does; then the job, no operation of the view left at its names to hold it back, carries disk 2's folder to E, where the walks take the files. A job carrying out an operation of the view would have to go once that operation stands no longer, which needs the job to name it (`jobs.payload`, a contract of §29.3), or the take-in of an operation to wait for such a job, and the later one that undoes it with it; neither is modelled.
- (b) `back-follow`: a folder found renamed below which a file has a copy on that disk at the file's new path already, its row behind there, takes that file with it, the copy no longer behind, as observation 5's (a) has it for a rename no walk finds. It holds in the model: alone, the flaw's pool passes, 57,377 states, and with fairness and `EventuallySettled` too; `ViewFolders` checking `ViewNamesSettled` too, 239,937; every other instance passes, `ViewFoldersUser` 343,543, the others the very states they have without it (before the two mistakes were fixed: 45,729, 187,310, 278,246). Its sensitivity: its one part is the remedy, without which the flaw comes back (45,974 states); its two guards, the file's new path held by no other file and no name of the view's planned there, never fire in these pools, nothing changing without either, as in the code `solver.Decide` (`Taken`) and the walk's waits hold them. In the code, it is a condition of `jobs.followers`, a few lines, and not enough (below).

The code's experiment, on 2026-11-24, a patch not kept (`bin/flaw23-experiment.patch` in the worktree that made it, `bin/` being ignored by git): (b) alone, `jobs.followers` taking a copy at its file's new path with its file for any folder found renamed, not only observation 5's, does not end the code's test, nor does it with (d) below. t1, back, holds its folder at `Shows/E`, its row of e1 behind at `Shows/D/e1.mkv` since the pass that followed the first rename while t1 was away, the file at `Shows/E/e1.mkv`: its copy is found moved, to its file's own path, never there, and (b) does not fire. t2's copy, found moved to `Shows/D/e1.mkv` by the rename back, is decided in the same pass: two targets, the file's own path first, as t1 comes first, and t2's copy a file of its own (`solver.Decide`, `ActOtherTarget`): e1 as two files, both renames never taken in. The model decides a disk at a time, clearing disk 1's row first, its copy back at its file's path, then following disk 2's move: it never reaches the split (What it leaves out, below), which is why (b) holds there. What ends the code's test is two more parts, each in the model as an entry of `Fixes`:

- (c) `back-pass`: (a)'s waits, a folder found renamed and a copy found moved, on a disk, from at or below the new name of the last folder's rename of the view that stands on its disk to at or below its old name, that disk having missed the rename; and that disk's rename made by the pass that finds it, once that disk's folder jobs ran, on the folder at the old name that its entry records at either name, the new name free and the rename standing still: never queued as a job, which could outlive the rename it carries out, as (a)'s does. In the code, the copy's wait in `pass.propFile` (a copy found moved against `viewKeeps` not yet due), the folder's in `decideFolder` (waited, unless the view renamed it there), and the rename `backRenames`, a folder's job run on the spot (`runFolderJob`) in `workOn` after the disk's folder jobs, for each rename of the view observation 5's remedy kept. Alone it does not hold: `ViewNamesSettled` in the flaw's pool (64,088 states), with fairness too (63,830), and in `ViewFolders` (278,518): the rename back is taken in once both disks hold the folder at D, the take-in comparing the folder's names alone, while the catalog still holds y at E/y, its one copy behind at D/y, which nothing moves; disk 2's copy of y is then renamed to E/y. Every other instance passes.
- (d) `back-files`: the take-in of a folder's rename of the view waits while the catalog holds a file below its old name, no user having undone the rename; observation 5's remedy then takes the rename to the catalog, the files following. In the code, a hold in `jobs.missedViewOps` (`filesBelow`: `catalog.FilesBelow`, `folderUndone`). Alone it holds every invariant in every instance, the flaw's pool 54,814 states, `ViewFolders` with `ViewNamesSettled` 229,321, but not liveness in the flaw's pool (54,814): the rename back is never taken in, y held at E.

With (c) and (d), every instance passes: the flaw's pool 62,178 states, with fairness too; `ViewFolders` checking `ViewNamesSettled` too, 274,112; `FlawViewFolderJobBack` 44,624, `FlawViewFolderRenameBack` and its liveness instance 177,886, `ViewFoldersUser` 354,250, `ViewFoldersLive` 19,611, `ViewFoldersUserLive` 8,542, `FlawViewFolderReprise` 3,186; the others the very states they have without them. With (b) as well, the same (the flaw's pool 62,178, `ViewFolders` 272,890). With (b) and (d), without (c), every instance passes too (54,499; 228,581), in the model, not in the code. Each part of (c) taken out on its own, with (d), in a copy of the module not kept: the folder's wait, `ViewNamesSettled` in `FlawViewFolderRenameBack` (179,191), its liveness instance (179,068) and `ViewFolders` (274,903); the copy's wait, nothing, every instance passing (the flaw's pool 62,523), where the code needs it, as it decides two copies found moved to two names together (above); the rename, `ViewNamesSettled` in the flaw's pool (54,718, with fairness too), `ViewFolders` (231,296) and `ViewFoldersUser` (332,918), liveness in `FlawViewFolderRenameBackLive` (148,362) and `ViewFoldersUserLive` (7,982); (d) out, (c) alone, above. In the code, the flaw's test fails without the copy's wait, without the rename and without (d); none fails without the folder's wait, which no test of the code reaches.

In the code, (c) and (d) are 81 lines in `internal/jobs`: `pass.go` 12 (a field and the two waits), `viewops.go` 66 (`backRenames`, `filesBelow` and the hold in `missedViewOps`), `work.go` 3 (the call); no contract change (no schema, payload, configuration key, `--json` output or exit code); specs.md would say it in §15.12, step 4 and Folders, and §21.2, Taken in. With them, flaw 23's test passes, observation 5's too, and `go test -tags flaws ./internal/jobs/` and `go test -race ./internal/jobs/` pass. For a user: a folder's rename back through the view that a disk lost at its turn missed is kept: when that disk is back, the pass that finds it renames its folder to the name the view gave it, every file ending below that name, none split in two, no operation of the view left open. What it costs: while a folder's rename of the view stands, not taken in (until the next pass, or longer while a disk that holds the folder is away), a user's direct rename, on another disk, of that folder from the view's new name back to the old is not followed but undone, the pass renaming it to the view's name again (`ViewFoldersUser` explores it; no invariant says which should win, `ViewNamesSettled` holding only while no user acted directly); and the take-in of a folder's rename waits while the catalog holds a file below its old name, a pass or so in the pools explored. Narrowing (c) to a disk whose folder an earlier rename of the view, the one the standing rename undoes, put at the old name would leave such a user's rename alone; it is not checked.

**The remedy** (the maintainer, 2026-11-24, DONE.md). He chose first a restricted variant of (c): `back-pass` acting only on a disk whose folder the view's earlier rename put at the old name, so that a user's own rename of that folder back on another disk is never undone, to be made if it held in the model and in the code, and otherwise (c) and (d) as the experiment made them. It does not hold, and (c) and (d) are the remedy.

A check, `BackKeepsUser` in `ViewNames.tla`, catches a user's rename undone: the rename a pass makes on a disk that missed a folder's rename of the view never takes a folder back from the name a user gave it directly on that disk, nothing having renamed it since (a ghost of the last rename a user made of a folder on each disk, which any other rename of it clears). With (c) and (d) it fails, in `ViewFoldersUser` (17,598 states) and `ViewFoldersUserLive` (3,548): D/x on both disks, the view renames D to E on both; a pass walks both disks; a user renames disk 2's folder back to D directly; the pass renames it to E again, as disk 2 then holds at the old name the folder its entry records at D, and nothing at E, which is just what a disk that missed the rename holds. The restricted variant, `back-own` in `Fixes` for the check: a disk whose folder at the old name an earlier rename of the view, the one the standing rename undoes, not taken in, put there, by the journal of that rename naming that disk and that folder by its inode, as the code can read it (`view_ops.disk_id`, `file_key`). With (d), it holds every invariant and `BackKeepsUser` in every instance (`ViewFoldersUser` 336,601 states, `ViewFoldersUserLive` 8,368), and the flaw's pool's safety (56,657), but not its liveness (56,657): the view's journal names only the first disk each rename reached (flaw 19's remedy), and when the disk lost at the rename back is another holder that the first rename reached, the pass does not act there, and the rename back is never taken in, a folder's job made and dropped each pass. In the code the same: `TestARenameBackMissedByALostDiskIsKept` with t2 lost, which the first rename's journal does not name, leaves e1 as two files, as the flaw did; with t1 lost, which it names, it passes. Widened to a disk whose entry still records, at the standing rename's new name, the folder now at its old name (`back-entry`), the flaw's pool passes with fairness too (57,257), and `BackKeepsUser` fails in `ViewFoldersUser` (63,307): the view renames D to E and back to D on both disks, a user renames disk 2's folder to E directly before any walk of it saw either rename, and the pass puts it back at D. Nothing on the disks tells a disk that missed the rename back from a user who renamed it back there, when the journal does not name that disk: the restriction that keeps every user's rename lets the flaw stand on one holder in two, and the one that ends the flaw puts some back. The checks of the two restricted forms ran on the model before its fix below (the holder's walk), the code's test being the one that settles it. A journal of the view recording each holder a folder's rename reached or missed, not only the first that went through, would let the pass act only on a disk that missed it and keep a user's rename back: a schema change (§29.3), the maintainer's (TODO.md).

So (c) and (d) are in specs.md (§15.12, Folders; §21.2, Taken in), in the code (`jobs.viewKeeps` in `propFile` and `decideFolder`, `backRenames` and `backRenamesOn` in `workOn` and in a dry run's list, the hold in `missedViewOps`), and in the model, what every instance does, `Fixes` empty, `BackKeepsUser` stated and checked by no instance, as it fails. For a user: a folder's rename back through the view that a disk lost at its turn missed is kept, the pass that finds that disk back renaming its folder to the name the view gave it, every file there, none split in two; a user's direct rename back of such a folder on another disk, made while the view's rename stands, until the next pass in the usual case, is put back. `TestARenameBackMissedByALostDiskIsKept` (`internal/jobs/viewfolders_test.go`), with either disk lost, the pass's rename failing once with `EIO`, and the daemon killed at the decision and at each point of that rename (`journaled`, `moved`, `target-synced`, `source-synced`), failed on the code before the remedy in each of its 14 cases, and passes, and so did `TestADryRunListsTheRenameOfADiskThatMissedIt`, a dry run listing that rename and doing nothing, with either disk lost; `TestARenameBackThroughTheViewWhileAPassDecidesTheFirstWithT1Lost`, TLC's trace of the folder's wait, passes on the code before and after; `TestARenameBackMissedByADiskUnmountedOnEachDriver` (`test/loopdev/viewnames_test.go`) runs it through the view served over FUSE on every driver, the disk unmounted rather than failing. `FlawViewFolderBackLost` expects `pass` (62,178 states), and `FlawViewFolderBackLostLive` checks its liveness (62,178).

### Observation 4: what the view presents may not open until the next pass

Not a flaw, for the maintainer: `ViewReadable`, that a file the view presents can be opened at its path on the disk it presents it from, or, of a file it presents from the catalog, on a disk there holding a copy at its path, once nothing of the view is planned and no user acted directly, fails in two ways the next pass ends: a folder renamed through the view while a holder had no handle presents its files at the new name from the catalog's rows below the old one, which that disk holds at the old name until the pass takes the rename there (§21.2, "a pass takes it to a disk it missed"); and an operation done whose take-in a later planned one holds back. Each is the window of one pass. No instance checks `ViewReadable`.

### Modelling mistakes, found by TLC and fixed

- A folder's rename counted done only when its first holder held it at the new name: §21.2 says "done once it reached one", and the journal's truth is now any holder's (`ViewJournalTrue`).
- A replacement whose source is gone once its copy replaced is in the trash read as a journal that lies: the code leaves that to finish (`resolveReplace`, "what was replaced is in the trash, which is all there is to finish"), and so does the model now.
- `ViewJournalTrue` flagged a verdict of `undone` on a move a user made directly, or after a user changed a name the view's verdict reads by names: it now asks the truth of `done` of what the view did not make, and of `undone` only of what it made while no user acted directly.
- What the view presents below a folder renamed took the catalog's rows at the old name alone: `view.Tree` reads them below the new name too, once a pass recorded the rename, and so does the model.
- The holders of a folder were those of its last name only: `writer.folderHolders` reads every path it resolves to.
- The take-in compared a file presented from another copy than the one the view wrote: §21.2 takes it in once the catalog holds that copy in sync (`view.inSyncOn`).
- A folder's job was modelled as copies following one by one: the code runs it as one operation when ready, its copies following one by one only when it goes (`jobs.runFolderJob`), and the walks move a job on when its target is renamed (`catalog.moveFolderJobs`).
- Every change a walk found was decided at once, by the walk of that disk alone: the code decides a copy's change only once its clock is a debounce old, after every walk of the pass, waiting while another copy of the file is at a name its disk's walk waited at or shows a change not yet due (`pass.isDue`, `solver.Decide`, `folderWaits`), and a folder found renamed keeps its entry until then. Without that, TLC found a job decided from a walk older than the view's rename (`ViewNamesKept`), which the code's test showed no pass can make (2026-11-23): the debounce, the waits and the walk's waits recorded for the pass (`pc.w`) are in the model now.
- A folder's job ran on whatever folder was at its old name: since flaw 14's remedy it runs only on the one its disk's entry records there (`jobs.folderReady`, `catalog.EntryKeyAt`), and so does the model.
- A pass worked on a disk missing at its walk, or lost since and back on another mount: the code's session keeps such a disk missing for the rest of the pass (`trash.Session`, `MarkMissing`, `Lose`), and so does the model (`Workable`, 2026-11-24, found with observation 5's remedy, a copy carried on a disk its pass never walked).
- A folder's job left its disk's entry at the old name, and a copy behind below it at its old place when its file's path is elsewhere below the new name: its transaction moves the entries and gives each copy behind its place below the new name (`catalog.FinishFolderRename`: `rewriteEntries`, `folderRows`), and so does the model (2026-11-24, found by `ViewNamesSettled`, the disk's later walk reading the job's own rename as a user's).
- No folder Atoll made was ever removed: a folder Atoll made for its own rename (`ops.makeParents`), which its own rename leaves empty, it removes at the end of the pass (`trash.Session.RemoveEmptied`, `catalog.OwnFolder`), and so does the model, for a copy's rename (2026-11-24, found by `FlawViewFolderRenameBackLive`, such a folder left at a rename's old name holding its take-in back for good).
- The take-in read the view's operations as they stood when it ran: the code reads them when the pass begins (`jobs.readViewOps`), and takes in only those done then, one journaled or done since waiting for the next pass, and so does the model (2026-11-24, found with flaw 23's (c), a rename back journaled while a pass ran taken in by that pass).
- A folder's job ran only on the folder its disk's entry records at the old name: `jobs.folderReady` runs it too on a folder there where that disk has no entry (`catalog.EntryKeyAt` giving none to compare), as the model did for (a) alone, and so does it now (2026-11-24, found by flaw 23's (c) and (d) with fairness, the flaw's pool waiting for good on a job the code runs).
- A folder's rename of the view stood while its disk was up: the code takes as standing only one whose disk this pass walked (`jobs.viewFolderMoves`, `p.walked`), and so does the model (`HolderSeen`; 2026-11-24, found reading the code against the model while making flaw 23's remedy; no instance changed its verdict either way).

### How sensitive it is

Each part of a remedy taken out on its own, in a copy of the module not kept, once the remedies were what every instance does: `replace-gone` (liveness in `FlawViewReplaceRestore`, 86 states, and `ViewNamesLive`, 4,954); `folder-key`'s look by the folder's inode, taking any folder at the new name (`ViewJournalTrue` in `FlawViewFolderReprise`, 220, `ViewFolders`, 3,517, `ViewFoldersUser`, 574); its journal naming the holder of each rename until one went through, the first holder's folder alone named (`ViewJournalTrue` in `ViewFolders`, 1,419); of `view-moot`, the part for a file (liveness in `FlawViewRenameUndone`, 401), the part for a folder's rename no holder holds at the new name any more (liveness in `ViewFoldersUserLive`, 5,326), and the part for a folder whose old name holds a folder again (liveness in `FlawViewFolderTaken`, 372, and `ViewFoldersUserLive`, 5,186); of `job-view-held`, the folder's job going (`ViewNamesKept` in `FlawViewFolderJobBack`, 6,898, `ViewFolders`, 23,714, `ViewFoldersUser`, 51,526), and the copy's rename waiting: nothing broke without it, in any instance above, once the folder's job goes for every operation not taken in; in the code, flaw 22's test fails without it, t1's copy following the first rename to E, the folder made there for it, before the rename back is resolved; why no instance of the model needs it is not known yet. Before the remedies, on the model as the flaws were recorded: the part of `view-moot` for a folder's rename no holder holds (`ViewFoldersUserLive`, 5,292) and for a folder whose old name holds one again (5,132, and flaw 21's pool, 373); `job-view-held`'s folder part (`ViewFolders`, 24,214, `ViewFoldersUser`, 50,488, flaw 22's pool, 6,860) and its copy's part (`ViewJournalTrue` in `ViewFolders`, 62,650); counting the operations a pass took in already: nothing broke without it.

Observation 5's remedy, each part taken out on its own, in a copy of the module not kept, in its two instances: (a) `view-back`, liveness in `FlawViewFolderRenameBackLive` (115,337 states), the safety instance passing (115,337), as the rename back then waits for good; (b) `view-kept`, liveness (120,844; 120,844 passing); (c) `view-missed`, `ViewNamesSettled` in both (68,185, 68,325); (d) `view-away`, `ViewNamesSettled` in both (70,450, 70,253); (e) `view-same`, liveness (116,461; 116,461 passing). All of it out, `ViewNamesSettled` in both (70,317, 70,318). In the code, each part's tests fail without it: (a) every test of observation 5, (a)'s real rename `TestARenameBackThroughTheViewMadeWhereTheFolderIsEmpty` alone, and its copy at the new path following with its file `TestARenameBackThroughTheViewWaitsForACopyGone` alone; (b) the traces 2 to 5 and the copy gone; (c) the traces 3 and 4; (d) the trace 4; (e) the traces 1 and 5 and the copy gone, (d) keeping the first rename open while t1 is away.
