| `NoLastCopyLost` | §18, invariant 1: the version a user wrote last is kept by a disk of the pool, in service or draining, at the path or in its trash, on a device not dead, but for what the user gave up by declaring its last disk failed, and what a disk dead for good took the last of while the pool held it nowhere else: `disk remove`, `disk replace` and `disk fail` never take it out of the pool |
| `OneIdOneDisk` | invariant 8, §10: never two disks of the configuration with one identifier |
| `OnePhysicalDisk` | invariant 13, §10: never two pool filesystems on one physical disk, as `disk add` checks it |
| `EventuallySettled` | liveness: once the world is done, every disk of the pool mounted again read-write from a device that answers is `online` with nothing planned on it; the file is at its level on such disks, as far as they go, unless in conflict or with no copy left to read; and a draining disk ends its drain once enough disks are in service |
| `FailedLeaves` | liveness: a failed disk the user takes out leaves the pool, whatever is planned on it |

The ghost is the version a user wrote last; the versions a user gave up, declaring failed the last disk of the pool that held them; those a disk dead for good took the last of while the pool held them nowhere else, the world's loss and not Atoll's; and those a user wrote on the disk a removal is taking out, or a disk's death took, between its look and its edit of the configuration: the user's own race with the command.

Measured on 2026-11-26 with `test/tla/run.sh`, 2 workers, once flaws 29 and 30's remedies were what every instance does; before them, the flaws' instances found them in 30, 34 and 251 states. Disk 4 is the spare where an instance has one.

| Instance | Expects | What it explores | States | Time |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `DisksWorld` | pass | what the world does to a disk, without a command: disk 1 unplugged, failing, unmounted by systemd or by hand, given another filesystem or a clone, mounted read-only, held, mounted again by the daemon after a reset; ntfs-3g's dead superblock; a user editing the file on disk 2; Atoll killed once | 173,250 | 5 s |
| `DisksRemove` | pass | disk remove, its drain and disk fail: disk 1 drained, failed or taken out, a user editing the file on it, disk 2 unplugged or dead, or Atoll killed, two times in all | 12,922 | 2 s |
| `DisksRemoveGone` | pass | disk remove against a disk gone: the file x1 on two disks, one surplus once a pass ran, either disk dead or unplugged once, either taken out; Atoll killed once | 1,383 | 1 s |
| `DisksReplace` | pass | disk replace and disk add: the spare brought in for disk 1, or beside it, then disk 1 taken out; a user editing the file on disk 1 once; disk 1 unplugged once | 2,819 | 1 s |
| `DisksClone` | pass | disk add and disk replace of a spare whose filesystem is a clone of disk 1: refused | 9 | 1 s |
| `DisksShared` | pass | disk add and disk replace of a spare on disk 1's physical disk: refused | 9 | 1 s |
| `DisksFail` | pass | disk fail and disk remove: a file replicated from disk 1, disk 2 unplugged or dead, a user editing the file on disk 1 or 2, Atoll killed, three times in all; disk 1 or 2 failed, drained or taken out | 113,950 | 4 s |
| `DisksMixed` | pass | the commands while the world acts: the spare; a file on disks 1 and 2; disk 1 or 2 drained, failed, replaced or taken out; unplugged, unmounted by systemd or by hand, dead, or mounted read-only, and mounted again by the daemon, once; a user editing the file on disk 1 or 3, there or away; Atoll killed once | 889,151 | 16 s |
| `DisksFailLive` | pass | liveness: a file replicated from disk 1, disk 2 unplugged once, then failed and taken out; Atoll killed once | 3,561 | 3 s |
| `DisksDrainLive` | pass | liveness: disk 1 replaced by the spare, drained, then taken out; a user editing the file on disk 1 once | 151 | 1 s |
| `DisksBackLive` | pass | liveness: disk 1 unplugged, unmounted by systemd, or failing, held by the view, mounted again by the daemon: ntfs-3g's dead superblock, or dead for good; the disk comes back, or its copy is made again past the grace | 1,348 | 2 s |
| `FlawDiskRemoveEdited` | pass | flaw 29, fixed on 2026-11-26 (remove-looks): a user edits the file on disk 1 directly, or through mergerfs, and disk remove looks at disk 1 before a pass takes the edit in | 86 | 1 s |
| `FlawDiskRemoveGone` | pass | flaw 29 through a disk gone, fixed on 2026-11-26 (remove-looks): the file x1 on two disks, disk 2 dead for good, and disk remove of disk 1 while the catalog still has disk 2 online | 94 | 0 s |
| `FlawDiskFailedPlanned` | pass | flaw 30, fixed on 2026-11-26 (failed-remove-drops): a file replicated from disk 1 to disk 2, disk 2 unplugged once while its copy is planned, then declared failed and taken out: what is planned there is dropped | 398 | 2 s |

`DisksFailLive`, `DisksDrainLive` and `DisksBackLive` check `EventuallySettled`, and `FlawDiskFailedPlanned` `FailedLeaves`, under `LiveSpec`: each step of a pass weakly fair, as are the drop and the edit of a removal that started, a user's removal of a failed disk strongly fair, the daemon's check of a disk missing and the grace strongly fair, as they wait for the pool's lock between two passes, and a device coming back, what holds a mount letting go, and the administrator putting back what he took, each weakly fair.

### Flaw 29: disk remove takes out a disk the file depends on, as the catalog does not know it yet

Found on 2026-11-26 by `NoLastCopyLost`, and fixed that day with the remedy the maintainer chose, `remove-looks` (below, The remedy of flaws 29 and 30).

§18: "once nothing depends on it, `atoll disk remove u2 --yes` takes it out of the configuration"; §25: "A file depends on a disk when it has a copy there, `ok` or `unavailable`, and fewer copies `ok` on the disks `online`, in `standby` or `readonly` than its level". `disk remove` reads that from the catalog alone (`catalog.ReadDiskHolds`), as the last pass or command left it: it checks no disk's mount before, and looks at nothing on the disk it takes out.

- `FlawDiskRemoveEdited` (30 states): the file x2 on three disks, each copy `ok`. A user edits the copy on disk 1, directly or through mergerfs, whose branch of a draining disk takes a write to a file it holds; `disk remove 1 --yes` comes before a pass takes the edit in: the catalog has the copies of disks 2 and 3 in sync, nothing depends on disk 1, and it goes, with the only copy of the edit. The pool keeps the version before. A drained disk goes the same way: its copies made elsewhere, an edit made there then goes out with it.
- `FlawDiskRemoveGone` (34 states): the file x1 on two disks. Disk 2 dies, or only its mount goes, with no pass since: the catalog still has it `online`, `disk remove 1 --yes` counts its copy, and the file is left with no copy the pool can read. The same through a copy `surplus`: a surplus copy makes no file depend on its disk, whatever holds the copy kept, so the disk holding it goes while the kept one is on a disk gone since the plan that kept it, which ranks a copy on a missing disk last only once it knows the disk missing.

In the code (build tag `flaws`, then `cmd/atoll/flaws_test.go`, now `TestAnEditOnADrainedDiskStaysInThePool` and `TestASurplusCopyStaysWhileTheKeptOneIsGone` of `cmd/atoll/diskremove_test.go`), each seen failing on 2026-11-26: `TestFlawAnEditOnADrainedDiskStaysInThePool`, t1 drained to t2 and t3, then edited directly, `disk remove t1 --yes` exits 0, and t2 and t3 keep the version before; `TestFlawASurplusCopyTakenOutWhileTheKeptOneIsGone`, the file x1 on t1 and t2, t2's the surplus copy as the plan keeps the first disk's, t1's mount gone with no pass since, `disk remove t2 --yes` exits 0. Each passes once the flaw is fixed, whatever the remedy: the disk stays, or the edit is in the pool.

A remedy proposed, an entry of `Fixes` then, checked by TLC on 2026-11-26 against every invariant, in every instance of the module (the flaw's instances in copies not kept; every other instance that runs a command took it); the maintainer chose it that day:

- `remove-looks`: under the pool's lock, `disk remove` checks every disk's mount and records the states it found, as it records them after its change now, before it counts what depends on the disk, a `surplus` copy counted as an `ok` one, there and elsewhere, as the plan that marked it may have counted a disk gone since; and it looks at the disk it takes out, but a failed one, once its confirmation passes, as a walk would (§15.10), refusing it when a copy there is not what its row says or a file there has none, or when it cannot be looked at: a disk `missing` is refused unless failed, so that `disk fail`, which gives up what only that disk holds, comes first. It holds in every instance (`FlawDiskRemoveEdited` 86 states, `FlawDiskRemoveGone` 94, and the rows above). Each of its parts is needed: without the look at the disk, `NoLastCopyLost` in `DisksRemove` (173 states); without the mount checks, in `DisksRemoveGone` (110); counting `ok` copies alone, as §25 does, in `DisksRemoveGone` (655). What it costs: a check of each disk's mount, which wakes none whose head is in the cache (§10), and a walk of the disk taken out, which wakes it, once per removal. It changes §18's and §25's words, and no contract: a refusal is exit 1, as now, and `disk-change.schema.json` counts `depending` as before, its number then taking surplus copies in.

### Flaw 30: a disk declared failed with an operation planned on it can never be taken out

Found on 2026-11-26 by `FailedLeaves`, and fixed that day with the remedy the maintainer chose, (b) `failed-remove-drops`, (a) set aside (below, The remedy of flaws 29 and 30).

§18: a disk `failed` is "never read or written again"; §17.2: the reprise resolves what is planned on a disk it can write to; §25: "`disk remove` ... refuses a disk with operations left `planned` on it". A disk lost during an operation of its own, a copy to it before its rename, a move to its trash, leaves that operation planned. Declared failed, nothing reads or writes it again, so nothing ends the operation, and `disk remove` refuses it for good, saying to mount it and let the daemon end what waits on it, which the daemon never does for a failed disk (`trash.Session.writable`, `recoverAll`).

- `FlawDiskFailedPlanned` (251 states): the file on disk 1 replicated to disk 2; disk 2 unplugged between the copy's journal and its rename; the user declares it failed: its operation stays planned, whatever comes back.

In the code: `TestFlawAFailedDiskWithAnOperationPlannedCanBeTakenOut` (build tag `flaws`, then `cmd/atoll/flaws_test.go`, now `TestAFailedDiskWithAnOperationPlannedIsTakenOut`), seen failing on 2026-11-26: every rename on t2 failing, the copy there stays planned; `disk fail t2 --yes`, a pass, then `disk remove t2 --yes` exits 1, "1 operations wait on it: mount it, and let the daemon or atoll daemon --until-idle end them". It passes once the flaw is fixed, whatever the remedy.

Remedies proposed, each an entry of `Fixes` then, checked by TLC on 2026-11-26 against every invariant, in every instance of the module, in copies not kept; the maintainer chose (b) that day, the user's explicit removal being what lets the operations go (invariant 17):

- (a) `failed-ops-undone`: the reprise undoes what is planned on a failed disk in the catalog alone, touching nothing on the disk, as nothing reads or writes it again (§18): a copy pending there is none again, a copy whose move to the trash was planned keeps its row. It holds in every instance (`FlawDiskFailedPlanned` 239 states, `DisksFailLive` 3,288 with it alone, `DisksFail` 107,102 and `DisksMixed` 852,730 with `remove-looks`).
- (b) `failed-remove-drops`: `disk remove` of a failed disk drops what is planned there, undone, and takes it out, as nothing of it counts any more. It holds in every instance (`FlawDiskFailedPlanned` 251, `DisksFailDropLive` 3,555, `DisksFail` 113,938 and `DisksMixed` 888,877 with `remove-looks`).

(a) ends at the next pass what a failed disk holds planned, (b) only once the disk is taken out. Neither changes a contract: the operations are `undone`, a state of the journal.

### The remedy of flaws 29 and 30

`remove-looks` and `failed-remove-drops` (the maintainer, 2026-11-26). In the code (`cmd/atoll/diskstate.go`), `atoll disk remove`, under the pool's lock:

- checks every disk's mount, and with `--yes` records what it found (`watch.CheckDisks`; `watch.CheckOnly` for a run that writes nothing), point `remove-checked`;
- of a disk not failed: refuses it `missing`, saying `disk fail` first; looks at it as `atoll catalog verify` compares a disk, confirmed past the cache, writing nothing (`watch.CompareDisk`), and refuses it when the look fails, or finds a file new to it or a copy changed, moved, absent, unread or past its branch's limit, until a pass takes it in; point `remove-looked`;
- counts what depends on it on the disks its checks found holding a copy, in service and online or readonly, or draining and readonly (`catalog.ReadDiskHoldsAmong`), a `surplus` copy counted as an `ok` one, there and elsewhere: `catalog.ReadDiskHolds`, which `disk show` and the other disk commands read from the catalog's states, counts so too (§25);
- of a failed disk, drops what is planned there before it edits the configuration (`catalog.DropPlannedOn`), in one transaction: each operation undone with an `op-undone` event, a move to the trash losing its trash row, the row of Atoll's own view that names one undone with it, a copy that was to be made there gone with its job, nothing written to the disk; point `remove-dropped`; then the edit of the configuration.

No contract changes: a refusal is exit 1, as `disk remove` gave before; `disk-change.schema.json` keeps its fields, `depending` counting surplus copies in, `planned_operations` the operations a failed disk's removal drops; the three points are new rows of the table of crash points (`../scenarios/README.md`). A disk lost, or an edit made, between the look and the edit of the configuration is not seen: that window is the write of the configuration, a user's own race with the command; the disks' states recorded right after the edit tell of a disk lost there, and what the disk taken out holds stays on it (`TestADiskLostBetweenTheLookAndTheEdit`).

What it costs: a mount check of each disk, which wakes none whose head is in the page cache (§10), and a walk of the disk taken out, which wakes it and reads every name below its root, once per removal. On 200,000 files a disk, in qemu without KVM, a pass that walks two disks where nothing changed took 8.9 s at the median in the soak of 24 hours (DONE.md, 2026-11-21): about 4 s a disk there, the walk with its comparison and the confirmation, most of a pass; a machine runs it faster. Measured with this fix: not.

The tests: `cmd/atoll/diskremove_test.go`, each seen failing before the fix: `TestAnEditOnADrainedDiskStaysInThePool`, `TestASurplusCopyStaysWhileTheKeptOneIsGone`, `TestASurplusCopyCountsForARemoval`, `TestAMissingDiskGoesOnceFailed`, `TestARemovalWhoseLookFails` (`EIO` on the look), `TestARemovalStoppedAtEachStep` (a stop as by kill -9 at `remove-checked` and `remove-looked`), `TestADiskLostBetweenTheLookAndTheEdit`, `TestAFailedDiskWithAnOperationPlannedIsTakenOut`, `TestARemovalOfAFailedDiskStoppedAfterTheDrop` (a stop at `remove-dropped`); `TestDropPlannedOnADisk` (`internal/catalog`), written with the function it tests; the scenarios of the runner, each with the real kill -9 of a conformance build at its point where it stops (`disk-remove-edited.json`, `disk-remove-missing.json`, `disk-remove-surplus-gone.json`, `disk-remove-failed-planned.json`, `disk-remove-crash-checked.json`, `disk-remove-crash-looked.json`, `disk-remove-crash-dropped.json`). In the model, the remedies are what every instance does, `Fixes` is empty, the removal's drop a step of its own, and the flaws' three instances expect `pass`. For a user: a disk goes out of the pool only once Atoll has looked at it and found nothing there the pool does not hold, and every other disk's state checked; a disk missing goes only once declared failed; and a disk declared failed can always be taken out.

### Observations

- The statechart of `states.json` had no transition to `retired` but from `draining` and `failed`, where `disk remove` takes out a disk in service nothing depends on, as the code allows (`changedText`); drawn on 2026-11-26 as the maintainer asked, with flaw 29's fix: `online` and `readonly` to `retired`. A disk `missing` is refused by that fix, and goes `failed` first, whose transitions were drawn already.
- The re-check of the device before each job, taken out alone, changes nothing here (`DisksWorld` 173,250 states, `DisksMixed` 852,997): each write goes through a handle held to the mount the session confirmed, which an unmount, a remount or another filesystem at the path leaves where it was. Taken out with the handle, a write by the path reaches an empty mount point: `Inv3_WritesConfirmed` in `DisksWorld` (17,069 states).
- A device that fails every read past the cache passes the daemon's check every 30 s, which answers from the cache, and the pass that follows finds it `missing` at its confirmation (§10, the price of a check that wakes no disk): a disk `missing` and back at each check, while nothing is decided of it.

### How sensitive it is

Each mechanism taken out, in a copy of the module not kept: question 2 of the mount check, an empty mount point passing, `Inv4_UnknownNeverAbsent` in `DisksWorld` (1,205 states); question 8, another filesystem passing, the same (2,482); question 11, a clone with no marker passing, the same (2,431); the handle held to the mount and the re-check before each job, `Inv3_WritesConfirmed` in `DisksWorld` (17,069); the session's verdict, a disk `readonly` a target and written by its jobs, the same (24,330); a copy only to a disk in service, `NoCopyOntoLeaving` in `DisksMixed` (58,391); what depends on the disk, `NoLastCopyLost` in `DisksRemove` (1,374); with the remedies in, the look at the disk, `NoLastCopyLost` in `FlawDiskRemoveEdited` (44) and `DisksRemove` (172); the mount checks, in `FlawDiskRemoveGone` (55) and `DisksRemoveGone` (110); `ok` copies counted alone, in `DisksRemoveGone` (653); the drop, `FailedLeaves` in `FlawDiskFailedPlanned` (395); a draining disk's copies counting toward the level, `EventuallySettled` in `DisksDrainLive` (64), the drain never ending; the grace, a copy on a disk missing counting for good, the same in `DisksBackLive` (760); the identity `disk add` checks, `OneIdOneDisk` in `DisksClone` (10); its topology, `OnePhysicalDisk` in `DisksShared` (10). Each instance reaches what it is there for, each shown by an invariant that fails, added in a copy: ntfs-3g's dead superblock mounted (`DisksWorld`, `DisksBackLive`), a disk `readonly` for another pool's marker and an edit made while a disk is away, taken in (`DisksWorld`), a disk remounted by the daemon and back `online` (`DisksBackLive`), a copy made on the spare (`DisksReplace`, `DisksDrainLive`, `DisksMixed`), a disk taken out (`DisksRemove`, `DisksFail`, `DisksMixed`), a drain that ends (`DisksRemove`, `DisksMixed`), an operation planned on a failed disk (`DisksFailLive`, `DisksFail`, `DisksMixed`), a copy `surplus` (`DisksRemoveGone`, `DisksMixed`), and a removal `remove-looks` lets through once the edit was taken in (`DisksRemove`).

### Modelling mistakes, found by TLC and fixed

- A removal stopped between its look and its edit of the configuration for good, the passes waiting for the pool's lock behind it: a command that started runs to its end.
- A disk whose device came back while its mount outlived it, with no remount in the instance, was mounted again by nobody, and the grace never ran out, its copy counting for ever: the administrator mounts a disk again by hand, and time passes.
- The plan had no `surplus` copy, so `disk remove` counted every copy in sync as §25 counts an `ok` one, which hid the third way of flaw 29: the plan keeps the file's level, by `compareKeep`'s ranks, and marks the others `surplus`, which §25 counts for nothing.

### What it leaves out

The steps of a copy and of a move, a power cut, the confirmation after a walk and the mount a session's handles hold between its confirmation and its writes (`Replication.tla`'s, which this model takes as they are: its walk and its confirmation are one step); Atoll's own view but for what holds a mount, and the SSD cache (`Replication.tla`'s); renames and folders (`Rename.tla`'s, `Folders.tla`'s, `Nested.tla`'s); the guards (`Guards.tla`'s); the trash's purge and prune of a surplus copy (`Trash.tla`'s); a file's size and the room on each disk, so that a copy always fits; two files; a user's deletion; the physical disks two filesystems of the pool share after `disk add`, which each start of the daemon asks again (`Replication.tla`'s, at a fixed topology); `standby`; the reasons of `readonly` but a read-only mount and another pool's marker, which are the mount check's pure questions (`conformance/vectors/disk_check.json`); a dirty NTFS volume, which is never remounted; `disk mark`; and a clone made after `disk add`, with the disk's marker, which no check can tell from its disk (`docs/threat-model.md`, G8).

## Scrub and repair against modifications: Scrub.tla

A model of its own, `Scrub.tla` with `MCScrub.tla` (2026-11-26), of §16 and §16.1, with what §15.4 says of a pass and §17.2 of a reprise, and invariants 1, 4, 7 and 9: the fourth of the maintainer's models by risk (TODO.md). `Conflicts.tla` holds a corrupt copy that changed since it was found and the conflict it settles into; this one goes into the scrub and the repair themselves. One file of two chunks, a copy at the same path on two or three disks, in sync and verified at the start, but on the disks of `Unverified`. A chunk holds the file's version, rot, or a user's edit; a sector may not be read at all, and a read through the page cache may come back wrong once. Each copy has its modification time and its change time as its filesystem holds them, the change time in whole ticks on a disk of `Coarse`, as an ext4 with 128-byte inodes holds whole seconds (§10, `time_precision_ns` 1e9): a change in the tick of the last moves nothing there. Its size, which an edit in place keeps, is fixed. The catalog holds the file's chunk hashes and state, each copy's row (`ok`, `corrupt`, or none once sent to the trash), its times, whether it was ever verified, the repair left planned on it and the scrub's cursor. Atoll is one process, as a scrub, a repair and a pass each hold the pool's lock (§15.13), and does what flaws 31 and 32's remedies say, chosen by the maintainer on 2026-11-26 (below):

- a scrub of a copy due (`jobs.scrubCopy`): opened after a look that its name holds what its row says; each chunk read and its hash compared; a chunk that does not match, or cannot be read on a disk that then confirms, the copy's times looked at, then the chunk read again past the page cache; a mismatch then makes the copy `corrupt`, or its file a `conflict` for a copy never verified and on a disk of `Coarse`, whose times cannot tell an edit from rot; a disk that does not confirm is lost, and nothing is concluded; the copy verified once every chunk matched and its times are still those it was opened with; the budget may stop it between two chunks, and the next scrub begins at the cursor while the row has not changed;
- a corrupt copy healed (§16, §16.1): a healthy copy, `ok` on a disk that can be read, read whole, its chunks the file's and its times its row's; by chunk (`trash.Session.RepairChunks`): the copy read whole and its bad chunks found, a `conflict` on a disk of `Coarse`; the operation and its trash row journaled; the bad chunks saved to the trash; each bad chunk read from the healthy copy and checked, the copy looked at against what the repair's own last change left of it and the chunk read again, written, read back; the copy looked at again, the modification time set back to the row's; looked at again, the commit, the row taking the change time the copy has then; a look that finds someone else's write stops the repair, the copy left as it is; a repair that stops otherwise resolved by the run, which knows whether it wrote; by file: the copy sent to the trash whole, made again by the next pass; without `auto_repair`, quarantined the same way; with no healthy copy, left as it is; a corrupt copy left in the root taken again by the next scrub;
- the reprise of a repair left planned (§16.1, Reprise), which explains what it finds or stops;
- a pass (§15.4), which takes a copy changed against its row in as a modification, its content the file's version, the other copies sent to the trash and made again from it, or does not run, as a guard holds the change; it decides nothing of a file corrupt or in conflict, and makes again a copy that went to the trash.

A user edits a chunk of a copy in place, directly or through the view, keeping its modification time (`touch -r`) or not; the world rots a chunk, makes a sector unreadable, makes a read through the cache come back wrong once, loses a disk and brings it back, and the clock ticks; Atoll's process may be killed between any two steps.

| Invariant | Checks |
|---|---|
| `NoEditCalledCorrupt` | invariant 9, §16: no copy holding a user's edit is called corrupt |
| `NoEditRepairedAway` | invariant 9, §16.1: no user's edit is written over by a repair, nor sent to the trash with its copy by a repair by file or a quarantine |
| `TrashFirst` | invariant 1, §16.1: whatever a repair writes over is in the trash first, but a sector that could not be read |
| `SourceVerified` | §16.1: what a repair writes is the healthy copy's chunk, checked against the file's hash |
| `NoWrongVerify` | §16.1: a repair, or its reprise, never records a copy `ok` and verified, its change time taken anew, while it holds a user's edit a later scrub could still call corrupt: the row would hide it |
| `LastKept` | invariant 1, §16: a corrupt copy never leaves its path as the file's last copy at its path ("nothing is lost to a scrub") |
| `SoundNeverCorrupt` | §16: a copy holding the file's content is never called corrupt, a read through the cache wrong once included |
| `NoUserTakenForTorn` | §16.1, Reprise: the reprise of a repair stopped by a crash never takes a user's write for its own torn chunk, which it would set the time back over and hide behind the row, for the next scrub to repair away |
| `EventuallyHealed` | liveness: once the world is done, a corrupt copy with a healthy copy elsewhere is no longer corrupt, but one that changed since it was found, which settles into a conflict (`Conflicts.tla`'s); one without stays at its path |

Measured on 2026-11-26 with `test/tla/run.sh`, 2 workers, once flaws 31 to 34's remedies were what every instance does; before them, the flaws' instances found them in 40, 137, 475 and 139 states. A chunk's write may be torn by the crash that stops it, part its old bytes and part the new, and the reprise is the code's (`integrity.RepairReprise`).

| Instance | Expects | What it explores | States | Time |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ScrubRot` | pass | rot, an unreadable sector or a read wrong once on disk 1, twice in all; the scrub, its repair by chunk, and Atoll killed once; disk 2 lost once | 9,308 | 2 s |
| `ScrubEdit` | pass | a user editing a copy in place, its modification time moving or not, while rot comes on disk 1; the scrub, its repair by chunk, a pass; Atoll killed once | 2,740 | 1 s |
| `ScrubSector` | pass | an unreadable sector, rot or a read wrong once on disk 1, disk 1 or 2 lost, two times in all; the repair by chunk stopped by a kill or a disk lost at any step, and its reprise; a user editing disk 1 once | 28,894 | 2 s |
| `ScrubQuarantine` | pass | without auto_repair: rot on disk 1, quarantined, made again by a pass; a user editing either copy, keeping its modification time or not; Atoll killed once | 1,738 | 1 s |

| `ScrubNoHealthy` | pass | three disks: rot on two of them, the third's copy edited and not taken in, so that no copy is healthy: the corrupt copies stay where they are | 2,747 | 1 s |
