| `FlawScrubTorn` | pass | flaw 33, fixed on 2026-11-26 (torn-foreign): rot on disk 1, the scrub's repair by chunk stopped by a crash after it saved the bad chunk, a user writing into that chunk, keeping its modification time or not, before the reprise: someone else's change, nothing written | 824 | 1 s |
| `FlawScrubUnreadStuck` | pass | flaw 34, fixed on 2026-11-26 (unread-found): an unreadable sector on disk 1, the repair by chunk stopped by a crash once journaled, before it saved anything: its reprise resolves it, and the copy is repaired | 109 | 1 s |

`ScrubLive` checks `EventuallyHealed` under `LiveSpec`: each step of a scrub or a repair weakly fair, a scrub that takes a corrupt copy again, the reprise and the pass that makes a copy again strongly fair, as they wait for the pool's lock, and a disk coming back weakly fair.

### Flaw 31: an edit in place in the second of a copy's last change is called corrupt and repaired away

Found on 2026-11-26 by `NoEditCalledCorrupt`, and fixed that day with the remedy the maintainer chose, (a) `coarse-conflict`, (b) noted for format version 2 (below, The remedy of flaws 31 and 32).

§16: "size, modification time and change time unchanged (compared at the coarser precision of the filesystems involved), and the mismatch confirmed by a second read without cache: `corrupt`"; "An edit by a tool that keeps the modification time still moves the change time, so it is never 'repaired' away". On a filesystem whose change time holds whole seconds, as an ext4 made with 128-byte inodes (§10: `time_precision_ns` 1e9), an edit in place made in the second of the copy's last change does not move the change time it holds. If it keeps the size and the modification time too, as `touch -r` or a tool that edits a header in place does, every look found the copy as its row says (`watch.Unchanged`): the scrub read another content with nothing changed, called it corrupt, and repaired it from the healthy copy; the edit's bytes went to the trash as `repaired`, purged at the end of the retention.

- `FlawScrubCoarse` (40 states before the remedy): disk 1 coarse; a user edits its copy in place, keeping its modification time, in the tick of its last change; the scrub reads the edited chunk, finds the copy's times unchanged, reads it again past the cache, and calls it corrupt.

In the code (build tag `flaws`, then `cmd/atoll/flaws_test.go`, now `TestAnEditInTheSecondOfTheLastChangeIsNotRepairedAway` of `cmd/atoll/scrubedit_test.go`), seen failing on 2026-11-26: t2's copy edited in place, its modification time given back, its row given the change time the edit left, as such a filesystem shows it, since no program can set a change time; `atoll scrub --yes` called it corrupt and repaired it, and t2 held the version before.

Remedies proposed, each an entry of `Fixes` then, checked by TLC on 2026-11-26 against every invariant, in every instance of the module; the maintainer chose (a) that day, and noted (b), a schema change, for format version 2 (§29.3):

- (a) `coarse-conflict`: on a disk whose `time_precision_ns` is a second or coarser, a hash mismatch is never rot: the file becomes a `conflict`, every version kept, as for a copy never verified (§16). It held in every instance (`FlawScrubCoarse` 216 states, `ScrubCoarse`, `ScrubMixedCoarse`). What it costs: no rot is repaired on such a disk; each is a conflict for `atoll conflicts resolve`.
- (b) `racy-conflict`: only where a copy's row was recorded in the tick of the copy's last change, as git takes a "racy" entry: a mismatch there is a `conflict`, and so is a corrupt copy taken again while its row is racy; a scrub whose every chunk was read once that tick had passed, and found right, clears the mark, the budget's cursor keeping whether the copy's read began so; a repair's commit records the row racy. It held in every instance then, once TLC had found two holes in its first design, the cursor and the copy taken again, in an instance gone with it, `ScrubMixedRacy` (`NoEditCalledCorrupt` in 298,325 states, `NoEditRepairedAway` in 2,915,213). It needs to know, for each row, whether its times were recorded once the second of the copy's last change had passed: `copies.verified_ns` given a stricter meaning, or a mark of its own in `copies`, and the cursor (`catalog.ScrubCursor`) the time the copy's read began. It is gone from the model, with its instances `ScrubRacyFixed` and `ScrubMixedRacy`.

### Flaw 32: a user's edit made while a repair by chunk writes is written over, or hidden

Found on 2026-11-26 by `NoEditRepairedAway`, and fixed that day with the remedy the maintainer chose, `repair-recheck` (below, The remedy of flaws 31 and 32).

§16.1, step 4: "Right before the first write, the copy is compared with its row as §15.10 compares a known copy: an edit in place is never overwritten"; step 5, "each bad chunk in turn, by index: read from the healthy copy and checked against its hash; written in place"; step 7, "the copy `ok`, verified now, with the change time the writes gave it". After that look, the repair read each bad chunk from the healthy copy, up to 4 MiB from another disk, which may have to spin up first, then wrote it. A user's write in place landing then, through the view or directly:

- into a bad chunk, before the repair writes it, was written over, and was never saved to the trash, whose save came before the look (`TrashFirst`, `NoEditRepairedAway`);
- into another chunk, or after, stayed in the copy, but the modification time was set back to the row's and the commit recorded the change time the copy then had, the user's write's included: the row hid the edit, and the next scrub called it corrupt and repaired it away (`NoWrongVerify`).

The instance, `FlawScrubRepairEdited` (137 states before the remedy): rot on disk 1, the scrub's repair by chunk saves the bad chunk and looks at the copy; a user writes into that chunk; the repair writes it over.

In the code (build tag `flaws`, then `cmd/atoll/flaws_test.go`, now `TestAnEditWhileARepairWritesIsNotLost` of `cmd/atoll/scrubedit_test.go`), seen failing on 2026-11-26: t2's copy rotted, its row left as it was; when the repair, having opened t2's copy to write, read from t1, the user's edit was written into t2's copy; the repair wrote the healthy chunk over it, and the edit was neither at its path nor in the trash.

A remedy proposed, an entry of `Fixes` then, checked by TLC on 2026-11-26 against every invariant, in every instance of the module; the maintainer chose it that day, both its parts:

- `repair-recheck`: the repair keeps the times its own last change gave the copy, and looks at them again right before each write, before the modification time is set back and before the commit; right before each write, it reads the chunk it is about to write again too, and finds it as it found it bad, as on a coarse disk a write in the second of the repair's own last one moves no time. Times that moved, or a chunk that changed, are someone else's write, and the repair stops with `changed`, the modification time not set back, the row left as it was, so that the write stays a change, which the copy's settling (§16.1, A corrupt copy that changed) makes a `conflict`. It held in every instance (`FlawScrubRepairEdited` 187 states). TLC found its second part missing from its first design, which looked at the times alone, in `ScrubMixedRacy`'s pool (`NoEditRepairedAway` in 1,408,386 states).

### The remedy of flaws 31 and 32

`coarse-conflict` and `repair-recheck` (the maintainer, 2026-11-26). In the code:

- **What a disk's times vouch for** (`integrity.TimesVouch`, `integrity.ScrubMismatch`, pure, `conformance/vectors/scrub_mismatch.json`): a disk's `time_precision_ns` is what its mount check read from its head in this session (`disks.ReadIdentity`: an ext4's revision and inode size, a constant for the other filesystems), recorded in `disks.time_precision_ns`, which `trash.Session.TimePrecision` gives: never guessed. A precision of a second or coarser, or none read, vouches for nothing.
- **The scrub** (`jobs.scrubCopy`): a mismatch, read again past the cache, the copy's size and times unchanged, is a `conflict` on such a disk, as for a copy never verified, every version kept, nothing written, an unreadable chunk there included; `corrupt` on any other.
- **A corrupt copy taken again**, and `atoll repair` (`jobs.changedCopy`): on such a disk, a copy whose name holds what its row says is read as it is too, as one that changed, and is `verified` when it holds its file's content, a `conflict` otherwise: one found corrupt there before Atoll knew the disk so is never repaired.
- **The repair by chunk** (`trash.Session.RepairChunks`): it keeps the times its own last change left, its row's to begin with, then what each write left, taken right after the write and before its sync (`ops.Disk.WriteChunk`), then what setting the time back left, taken right after it (`ops.Disk.SetTimeBack`). Right before each write, once the healthy chunk is read and checked, it looks at the copy again (`ops.Disk.SourceStill`) and reads the chunk to write again (`ops.Disk.ChunkStill`), which must hold the bytes `saveChunks` found, or, for one that could not be read, still fail; point `chunk-looked`. After the last write it looks again, point `times-looked`, then sets the time back; and again before the commit, point `commit-looked`. A look that finds someone else's write ends the repair at once (`ops.Disk.LeaveRepair`): nothing more written, the time not set back, the row as it was, the operation `done` when a chunk reached the trash and `undone` when none did, the event `repair-stopped` with `changed`. A look that fails is an I/O error: the disk's when it no longer confirms, which loses it, the operation `planned` for the reprise (invariant 4).

No contract changes: `conflict` and `changed` are outcomes already; `scrub_mismatch.json` is a new vector, `chunk-looked`, `times-looked` and `commit-looked` new rows of the table of crash points (`../scenarios/README.md`). No black-box scenario reaches either: a folder the runner drives moves a copy's change time at each write, so that no scrub finds one corrupt, and no disk it drives holds its change time in whole seconds.

What it costs: on a disk that holds a time to the second, rot is never repaired by itself: each is a conflict for `atoll conflicts resolve`. A repair by chunk reads each bad chunk once more, 4 MiB at most, and looks at the copy once per bad chunk and twice more.

The tests, each seen failing before its fix: `cmd/atoll/scrubedit_test.go`: `TestAnEditInTheSecondOfTheLastChangeIsNotRepairedAway` and `TestAnEditWhileARepairWritesIsNotLost` (the two flaw tests made regular, the second following the write to the conflict the next scrub makes of it), `TestAScrubOverACoarseDiskAndAFineOne` (a conflict on the one, rot repaired on the other, in one run), `TestAnUnreadableChunkOnACoarseDiskIsAConflict`; `internal/jobs/scrubcoarse_test.go`: `TestAMismatchOnADiskWhoseTimesDoNotVouchIsAConflict` (a second, and a precision none read, with and without `auto_repair`, beside a fine disk), `TestACorruptCopyTakenAgainOnACoarseDiskIsAConflict`; `internal/trash/recheck_test.go`: `TestARepairLeavesAWriteBeforeItsFirstWrite`, `TestARepairLeavesAWriteAtEachLook` (between two writes, after the last, after the time is set back), `TestARepairLeavesASectorThatReadsAgain`, `TestARepairWhoseLookFails` (`EIO` on each look and on the read again), `TestARepairStoppedAtEachLook` (a stop as by kill -9 at each new point, the reprise ending it); `TestScrubMismatchVectors` (`internal/integrity`); and on the loop devices, `TestAnEditInTheSecondOfTheLastChangeOnACoarseExt4`, a real edit in the second of a placed copy's last change on an ext4 made with `-I 128`. In the model, the remedies are what every instance does, `FixNames` and every `Fixes` empty, and the flaws' two instances expect `pass`. For a user: an edit is never called rot where the disk's times cannot tell it, and a repair never writes over, nor hides, a write that lands while it works.

### Flaw 33: a user's write into a bad chunk before a repair's reprise is taken for its torn write, and repaired away

Found on 2026-11-26 by `NoUserTakenForTorn`, once the model had torn writes, as the maintainer asked (DONE.md), and fixed that day with the remedy he chose, (a) `torn-foreign` (below, The remedy of flaws 33 and 34).

§16.1, Reprise: the repair "explains what it finds when ... its bad chunks, by index, hold what their hashes say, then at most one chunk that holds neither that nor what was saved of it, then what was saved of them"; then "a write began: the modification time is set back to the row's, the row takes the change time the copy has then, and the repair stops". That one chunk that holds neither is a write the crash tore. A user's write in place into a bad chunk between the crash and the reprise, directly or through the view, whether into the chunk the crash was writing, the next bad one, or the first that holds the healthy bytes again, holds neither too: the reprise takes it for the torn write, sets the modification time back over it and gives the row the change time it left, so that the copy reads as its row says. The next scrub takes it again, finds that chunk bad, and repairs it, the user's bytes saved to the trash first, as `repaired`: nothing is lost, but the user's write is gone from its path. On a disk whose times vouch it goes so; on a coarse one, a write that kept the modification time ends in a conflict instead (flaw 31's remedy), the time set back over it all the same. A user's write into a chunk the repair did not find bad is someone else's change, as before.

- `FlawScrubTorn` (475 states before the remedy): rot on disk 1; the repair saves the bad chunk, then a crash stops it right before its write; a user writes into that chunk; the reprise takes the write for a torn one.

In the code: `TestFlawAUsersWriteIsNotTakenForATornChunk` (build tag `flaws`, then `internal/trash/flaws_test.go`, now `TestAUsersWriteIsNotTakenForATornChunk` of `internal/trash/reprisetorn_test.go`), seen failing on 2026-11-26: a repair of t3's copy stopped as by kill -9 at `chunk-looked`; the user's bytes written into the bad chunk; the reprise ends the operation `done`, the time set back, the row taking the change time; the next repair writes the healthy chunk over the user's bytes. It passes once the flaw is fixed, whatever the remedy: the user's write stays at its path.

Remedies proposed, each an entry of `Fixes` then, checked by TLC on 2026-11-26 against every invariant, in every instance of the module (the flaw's instance in copies not kept); the maintainer chose (a) that day:

- (a) `torn-foreign`: a bad chunk that holds neither the file's content nor what was saved of it is never the repair's own: someone else's change, the operation ended, nothing written, the time not set back and the row as it was, so that the copy settles as a corrupt copy that changed, a conflict, every version kept. It holds in every instance (`FlawScrubTorn` 824 states, `ScrubTorn`, `ScrubTornCoarse`, and the instances that take it). What it costs: a crash that tears a repair's write leaves a conflict for `atoll conflicts resolve` rather than a repair the next scrub takes on.
- `torn-index`, checked and holding not: the journal records the chunk each write is at, and a chunk that holds neither is the repair's own only there. A user's write into the very chunk the crash tore is still taken for it (`NoUserTakenForTorn` in `FlawScrubTorn`, 538 states, and in `ScrubTorn` and `ScrubEdit`): it is no remedy to propose. It is gone from the model, with the journal's index of a write it read (`wi`).

### Flaw 34: a chunk that cannot be read at a repair's reprise leaves the repair planned for good

Found on 2026-11-26 by `EventuallyHealed` in `ScrubLive`, once the model's reprise did what the code does, and fixed that day with the remedy the maintainer chose, `unread-found`, both its parts (below, The remedy of flaws 33 and 34).

§16.1, Reprise: "A bad chunk saved as an empty file, which the repair could not read, holds what was saved of it while it still cannot be read; any other chunk that cannot be read leaves the operation `planned`, for a reprise that can read it." A repair stopped by a crash after its journal and before it saved the empty file of a bad chunk it could not read; a bad chunk it read and saved that cannot be read since; or a chunk it did not find bad that cannot be read since: the reprise leaves the operation planned. A sector that cannot be read stays so until something writes over it, and nothing does: no scrub takes a corrupt copy with an operation planned at its name (§16), so the copy stays corrupt in the root for good, which invariant 14 wants out of it.

- `FlawScrubUnreadStuck` (139 states before the remedy, `EventuallyHealed` under `LiveSpec`): an unreadable sector on disk 1; the repair by chunk stops once journaled, before it saved anything; its reprise leaves it planned for good.

In the code: `TestFlawAnUnreadableChunkLeavesNoRepairPlannedForGood` (build tag `flaws`, then `internal/trash/flaws_test.go`, now `TestAnUnreadableChunkLeavesNoRepairPlannedForGood` of `internal/trash/reprisetorn_test.go`), seen failing on 2026-11-26: every read of t3's chunk failing; a repair stopped as by kill -9 at `journaled`; two reprises leave the operation planned. It passes once the flaw is fixed, whatever the remedy.

A remedy proposed, an entry of `Fixes` then, checked by TLC on 2026-11-26 against every invariant, in every instance of the module; the maintainer chose it that day, both its parts:

- `unread-found`: at the reprise, a bad chunk the repair could not read, with nothing saved of it yet, that still cannot be read, holds what the repair found, as no write begins before every bad chunk is saved; any other chunk that cannot be read, on a disk that confirms, holds neither its hash nor what was saved of it, and the reprise decides from what it holds: someone else's change, or, without flaw 33's remedy, the torn write. The operation ends, and the next scrub takes the copy again. It holds in every instance (`FlawScrubUnreadStuck` 139 states, with `torn-foreign` too 109, `ScrubLive`), and with `torn-foreign` in every instance of the module. It changes no contract.

### The remedy of flaws 33 and 34

`torn-foreign`, and `unread-found`, both its parts (the maintainer, 2026-11-26). In the code:

- **The reprise's decision** (`integrity.RepairReprise`, pure, `conformance/vectors/repair_reprise.json`): once every bad chunk was saved, the bad chunks, by index, must hold what their hashes say, then what was saved of them; a bad chunk that holds neither is someone else's change, `foreign`, wherever it is: nothing written, the time not set back, the row as it was, the operation `done` when a chunk reached the trash and `undone` when none did, the event `repair-stopped` with `changed`. The copy then settles as a corrupt copy that changed, a `conflict`, every version kept (§16.1, A corrupt copy that changed); a true tear, part the healthy bytes and part the old, ends so too. The vector's four cases of a chunk that holds neither, `keep` before, are `foreign`, and so is the oracle of `FuzzRepairStopped`.
- **A chunk that cannot be read** (`ops.Disk.ResolveRepairConfirmed`, `ops.chunksNow`): the reprise of a disk (`ops.Disk.Recover`) gives a repair whose copy has a chunk it cannot read in `Recovered.Unreadable`, still planned, rather than among the operations that stay; the session (`trash.Session.recoverAll`) confirms the disk then (§10), and loses it when it does not, the operation waiting for it (invariant 4); once it confirms, it resolves each such repair again, a chunk that cannot be read being the chunk's fault: a bad chunk the repair found so, nothing saved of it yet, holds what the repair found, and any other holds neither its hash nor what was saved of it, which `RepairReprise` weighs as any such chunk. A repair whose own resolution, as it stops, cannot read a chunk (`trash.Session.stopRepair`) does the same. A bad chunk saved as an empty file holds what was saved of it, as before.
- **A write that does not take** (`trash.Session.stopRepair`, `specs.md` §16.1, step 5): a chunk the repair wrote that cannot be read back, on a disk that confirms, or that reads back otherwise than its hash says, holds neither, as a user's write landing between the write and its read back would: someone else's change, as at the reprise, nothing more written, the row as it was; the scrub's move of the copy to the trash then finds it changed, and leaves it, and the next scrub settles it as a conflict. It was taken for the repair's own, the time set back over it, and the copy quarantined.

No contract changes: `conflict`, `changed` and the reprise's actions are what they were, four cases of the vector expect another of them; no new crash point (`../scenarios/README.md`). No black-box scenario reaches either, as for flaws 31 and 32: no folder the runner drives holds a copy a scrub finds corrupt, for a repair by chunk to take.

What it costs: a crash that tears a repair's write leaves a conflict for `atoll conflicts resolve`, rather than a repair the next scrub takes on; a chunk that cannot be read since the repair began ends it, and the next scrub takes the copy again.

The tests: `internal/trash/reprisetorn_test.go`: `TestAUsersWriteIsNotTakenForATornChunk` (on a disk whose times hold nanoseconds, and on one whose times hold seconds) and `TestAnUnreadableChunkLeavesNoRepairPlannedForGood`, the two flaw tests made regular, seen failing before the fix; `TestATrueTearEndsInAConflict`; `TestAReprisePastAChunkThatCannotBeRead` (unreadable when the repair found it and readable again at the reprise, a bad chunk saved and unreadable since, a disk that does not confirm and then does); `TestAnUnreadableChunksRepriseStoppedAtEachStep` (kill -9 at the disk's confirmation and at each call of the resolution after it, `EIO` at the folder, the look and the open); `TestARepairStoppedAtTheConfirmationOfAnUnreadableChunk`; `TestARepriseWithBothRemedies` (a user's write into the next bad chunk, every read failing since); the tests of `internal/trash` that held the behaviour before, now the remedies' (`TestARepairThatStopsBeforeItWrites`, `TestARepairWhoseChunkCannotBeReadBackLosesNoDiskThatConfirms`, `TestARepriseOfARepairOnADiskThatNoLongerConfirms`); `internal/jobs/repair_test.go`: `TestARepairWhoseWriteDoesNotTakeEndsInAConflict`, and `TestARepairThatFailsQuarantines` on a write refused (`EPERM`), its chunk as the repair found it; `TestRepairRepriseVectors` and `FuzzRepairStopped` (`internal/integrity`); and the loop-device tests of the repair and the scrub, in qemu on both kernels. In the model, the remedies are what every instance does, `FixNames` and every `Fixes` empty, and the flaws' two instances expect `pass`. For a user: a write into a copy while Atoll is down after a crash is never repaired away, and a sector that cannot be read never keeps a corrupt copy from the scrub for good.

### Observations

- The look right before the first write changes nothing taken out alone (`ScrubEdit` 2,692 states, `FlawScrubRepairEdited` 189): the look of `repair-recheck` before each write takes it in.
- The chunk read again right before a write changes nothing taken out in the model (`ScrubMixed`, `ScrubMixedCoarse`), and nor does the conflict of a corrupt copy taken again on a coarse disk (`ScrubCoarse`, `ScrubMixedCoarse`): with `coarse-conflict`, no copy is ever corrupt on a disk of `Coarse`, and on another the times tell every write. In the code, the read again covers what `time_precision_ns` does not say, measured on 2026-11-26 (`docs/research/ctime-granularity.md`): on Linux 6.12, ext4, XFS, btrfs and ntfs3 date a change to the kernel's tick, 4 ms, two changes after a look carrying one change time up to 10.9 ms apart in qemu without KVM; on 7.3, ext4, XFS and btrfs take multigrain timestamps and never do, while ntfs3 and the kernel's `ntfs` date a change to a 1 ms tick; ntfs-3g and lowntfs-3g never do. The same tick leaves flaw 31's remedy a limit, recorded in §16: an edit in place within it of a row's last recorded change, keeping its size and modification time, is called corrupt on any disk, which `racy-conflict`, noted for format version 2, would close.
- The reprise taking a user's write for its own torn chunk, an observation of the fix of flaws 31 and 32: flaw 33 since the model has torn writes, fixed (above).
- `unread-found`'s first part changes nothing taken out alone (`FlawScrubUnreadStuck` 109 states, `ScrubLive` 7,232): before every bad chunk is saved the reprise writes nothing, whatever a bad chunk holds, and the second part has the chunk hold neither, which ends the repair untouched all the same. In the code, `ops.chunksNow` gives both the same answer.
- The healthy copy's times compared with its row change nothing taken out (`ScrubFile`, `ScrubQuarantine`): its chunks checked against the file's hashes already catch an edit there, which is what keeps the repair's source right; the times keep a copy being written from being read as healthy, which the model's one-step edit does not show.
- A copy never verified taken as another version changes nothing taken out in `ScrubUnverified`, whose copy starts with the file's content: it matters for a copy that came with another content, as a disk added or a catalog rebuilt brings one (`Conflicts.tla`).

### How sensitive it is

Each mechanism taken out, in a copy of the module not kept: the second read past the page cache, a read wrong once then called rot, `SoundNeverCorrupt` in `ScrubRot` (221 states); the scrub's look at the copy's times before that read, an edit called corrupt, `NoEditCalledCorrupt` in `ScrubEdit` (82); the bad chunks saved before any is written, `TrashFirst` in `ScrubRot` (3,089); the healthy copy's chunk checked against the file's hash, `SourceVerified` in `ScrubEdit` (1,455); the look right before the move of a quarantine, `NoEditRepairedAway` in `ScrubQuarantine` (837); the reprise's test that it explains what it finds, where a write began, a user's edit made after the stop taken for the repair's own, `NoEditRepairedAway` in `ScrubSector` (24,952); `coarse-conflict`, `NoEditCalledCorrupt` in `FlawScrubCoarse` (40), `ScrubCoarse` (111) and `ScrubMixedCoarse` (288); `repair-recheck` whole, `NoEditRepairedAway` in `FlawScrubRepairEdited` (137) and `ScrubEdit` (1,457); its look at the times alone, `NoWrongVerify` in `FlawScrubRepairEdited` (151) and `ScrubEdit` (1,723); `torn-foreign`, `NoUserTakenForTorn` in `FlawScrubTorn` (487), `ScrubTorn` (1,911) and `ScrubEdit` (1,499); `unread-found` whole, liveness in `FlawScrubUnreadStuck` (109) and `ScrubLive` (7,216), and its second part alone in `ScrubLive` (7,216), `FlawScrubUnreadStuck` passing as the first holds its chunk (Observations, above). Each instance reaches what it is there for, each shown by an invariant that fails, added in a copy: a repair by chunk committed (`ScrubRot`, `ScrubEdit`), a copy sent to the trash whole and made again by a pass (`ScrubQuarantine`, `ScrubFile`), a repair left planned once it wrote, resolved by its reprise (`ScrubSector`), a conflict of a copy never verified (`ScrubUnverified`), the budget's cursor taken by the next scrub, and a corrupt copy with no healthy copy left where it is (`ScrubNoHealthy`), an unreadable sector written over (`ScrubSector`), a conflict on a coarse disk (`ScrubCoarse`, `ScrubMixedCoarse`, `FlawScrubCoarse`), and a repair that wrote left by a look (`ScrubEdit`, `ScrubMixed`, `FlawScrubRepairEdited`).

### Modelling mistakes, found by TLC and fixed

- Rot that came after a chunk was read and matched made the copy "verified wrong": no scrub can see it, and a scrub's verification records none of the copy's times, so the invariant judges a repair that takes the change time anew, while the copy holds a user's edit.
- A corrupt copy sent to the trash while the healthy copy found was edited since by a user was taken for the last copy lost: the rotted old version goes to the trash, the user's newer one is on the other disk; the invariant asks that the file keep a copy at its path.
- `racy-conflict`, gone since, cleared a row's mark at the end of a scrub whose read began before the tick of the copy's last change, and took a repair's commit after a tick for one that knows the content after it: only a scrub opened once the tick had passed cleared it, and a repair, whose content is its own last change's, recorded it racy.
- A chunk that could not be read, holding a user's edit a pass had already taken in as the file's version, was taken for an edit the repair wrote over (`ScrubMixed`): an edit is what is not the file's version, which the healthy copy holds too.

### What it leaves out

A corrupt copy that changed since it was found, and the conflict it settles into (`Conflicts.tla`'s: here it waits); the pipeline of a copy (`Replication.tla`'s: one step here); the trash's purge (`Trash.tla`'s); renames and inodes, so an atomic save, which every look sees as a new inode; the scrub's time windows, `scrub_every` and the order of disks; files of another size, empty or of one chunk; the guards but as a pass that does not run (`Guards.tla`'s); a kernel's clock tick coarser than a filesystem's precision says; a write that lands within the width of a `stat`, a `pread` and a `pwrite`; and a write in place that does not take, or reads back otherwise than written, which the code makes someone else's change (§16.1, step 5), the model's stop of a repair that wrote being the keep it is while every chunk holds what was found or written.

## Balance: Balance.tla

A model of its own, `Balance.tla` with `MCBalance.tla` (2026-11-26), of `atoll balance` and the daemon's (§13.3), with the hard constraints of §12.4, the plan of §13.4, the copy pipeline of §14.2, the move to the trash of §17.1 and the retire as prune would of §17.3, and invariants 1, 9, 13 and 18: the fifth of the maintainer's models by risk (TODO.md). One file, a copy at its path on some of three or four disks, at its level at the start; two of the disks maybe filesystems of one physical disk (`Shared`, R-06). The file's rule says `tags_mode = "strict"`: the disks it lists are `Listed`, and an Atoll copy on another is misplaced, as a change of rule or of a disk's tags leaves it. For each disk: whether its device answers and its mount is read-only; the version the copy at its path holds, the name it sits at, whether Atoll placed it, the part of a copy in its `tmp/`, what its trash holds, and its room past `min_free`, in copies. The catalog: the file's version, name and state (`ok`, `conflict`); each copy's row (none, `ok`, `surplus`, `pending`, `stale`), who made it, the version it records and whether it was verified; what the last check of each disk found; whether it is draining; and the operation planned on it. Atoll is one process, the balance holding the catalog's lock through a run's steps, the passes coming in between (§13.3, §15.13):

- a balance run (`jobs.balance`): its session checks every disk as it opens (`trash.Open`); its plan, made with each misplaced copy counting for nothing, is an oracle that picks any target the hard constraints allow, listed, writable, with no copy of the file there nor on its physical disk in any state, room past `min_free` and nothing planned there, and any source (invariant 18); the copy through the pipeline: the operation journaled and the part written, `min_free` checked at the write (`jobs.room`), the source looked at again, the part placed without replacing anything at the file's name as the catalog has it then, the commit, which with `--move-originals` makes the new copy the user copy and the old one an Atoll copy; the plan made again, which marks the old copy `surplus`; the retire as prune would (`trash.Session.Retire`): the old copy `surplus` and an Atoll copy, the copies that stay `ok`, verified, looked at and found as their rows say, at the level, the file not in conflict; the move journaled, then the copy looked at right before it moves (§17.1, step 4). A run with no copy to make, as one stopped once a copy was made leaves it, goes straight to the plan and the retire;
- the passes, each part a step of its own: the check of each disk; a copy changed against its row taken in as a modification, the others stale, two changed a conflict; a copy gone a deletion, sent to the trash from every other copy unchanged since in sync; a copy at another name a rename, followed on the other disks; a file where the catalog has no copy recorded, another version a conflict; a stale copy refreshed, what it held to the trash first; a pending copy made, or canceled when it no longer fits, counts or has a disk, but one whose job is running; a copy made for a file below its level, its strict setting kept; the marks;
- the reprise of what is planned on a disk that answers (§17.2): a create whose part reached the file's name committed, one whose part is in `tmp/` undone; a move to the trash done once the copy is there, undone otherwise.

A user edits the file in place, deletes it, renames it, or writes a file of their own at its name on a disk with none, directly or through the view, which writes to one disk as a user does; the world loses a disk and brings it back, mounts one read-only, drains one (`disk remove --evacuate`), or fills one past its room with other files; Atoll's process may be killed between any two steps.

| Invariant | Checks |
|---|---|
| `Inv13_OnePhysical` | invariant 13, R-06: never two copies of the file on one physical disk, at any step, a copy at another name included |
| `NoBelowLevel` | §13.3: a balance never takes the file below its level, even for a moment: its move to the trash leaves the level's number of copies of the file's version on disks that answer, but for what a user or the world did since its look |
| `NoUserCopyMoved` | §12.4, §13.3, §6.1 item 9: a balance never sends a user copy to the trash; `--move-originals` hands its part to the copy made first |
| `UserCopiesKept` | the file never has fewer user copies than at the start, a crash included, but once a user deleted it |
| `EditKept` | invariant 9: the version a user wrote last is at a path, in a part, or in a trash, on some disk, but once the user deleted the file |
| `NoOnlyVersionMoved` | §13.3: a balance never sends to the trash the only copy of the version a user wrote last |
| `MinFree` | §12.4: no write takes a disk's room past `min_free` |
| `EventuallySettled` | liveness: once the world is done, nothing is left for Atoll to do: a balance started finished or was undone, its operations resolved, and the pool settled |

Measured on 2026-11-26 with `test/tla/run.sh`, 2 workers. Disk 1 holds the misplaced Atoll copy, and disk 2 the user copy, where an instance says no other.

| Instance | Expects | What it explores | States | Time |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `BalanceMove` | pass | the file on disks 1 and 2, its user copy on 2, its Atoll copy on 1, which its strict setting no longer lists: the balance moves it to disk 3, beside the passes; Atoll killed once | 53 | 1 s |
| `BalanceEdit` | pass | the same, a user editing or deleting the file on any disk once, while the balance moves it | 740 | 1 s |
| `BalanceEdits` | pass | the same, a user editing the file twice, on disks 1 or 2, or editing and deleting it | 1,568 | 1 s |
| `BalanceCreate` | pass | the same, a user's own file written at the file's name on disk 3, the target, while the balance copies there, or an edit, twice in all | 2,031 | 2 s |
| `BalanceRename` | pass | the same, a user renaming the file on disk 1 or 2, or editing it, twice in all | 4,920 | 1 s |
| `BalanceWorld` | pass | the same, disk 1, 2 or 3 lost, mounted read-only, drained or filled past its room, twice in all | 13,436 | 2 s |
| `BalanceShared` | pass | four disks, 3 and 4 filesystems of one physical disk, the user copy on 3: the copy on disk 1 can go to disk 2 alone; disk 2 or 3 filled or lost, and a user editing the file, once each | 4,282 | 2 s |
| `BalanceFull` | pass | disk 3 with no room past `min_free`: no target, the copy on disk 1 stays; a user editing the file once | 7 | 1 s |
| `BalanceEnospc` | pass | disk 3's room taken by other files, before or while the copy is made there, ENOSPC; disk 3 read-only once | 168 | 1 s |
| `BalanceOriginals` | pass | `atoll balance --move-originals`: the file at level 1, its user copy on disk 1, unlisted, moved to disk 2 or 3, the copy made there the user copy; a user editing or deleting it once | 1,214 | 2 s |
| `BalanceOriginalsBoth` | pass | `--move-originals` at level 2: the user copy on disk 1 and an Atoll copy on disk 2, unlisted with disk 1, the listed disks 3 and 4; disk 3 or 4 lost or filled once | 8,803 | 2 s |
| `BalanceMixed` | pass | four disks: the user copy on 2, the Atoll copy on 1, unlisted, the listed disks 2, 3 and 4; a user editing, deleting or renaming the file, or writing a file of their own at its name, once; disk 1, 3 or 4 lost, read-only, drained or filled once | 113,683 | 6 s |
| `BalanceLive` | pass | liveness: the move, a user editing the file once, disk 1 or 3 lost or read-only once, Atoll killed once: the pool settles | 6,060 | 15 s |

`BalanceLive` checks `EventuallySettled` under `LiveSpec`: each step of a run weakly fair; a run starting, each family of a pass's steps and the reprise strongly fair, as they wait for the catalog's lock; and a disk coming back weakly fair.

No flaw found: every invariant holds in every instance, and the pool settles.

### Observations

- The retire's ask that the old copy be `surplus`, and that it be an Atoll copy, change nothing taken out, alone or with the hand-over of `--move-originals` (`BalanceMove`, `BalanceWorld`, `BalanceEdit`, `BalanceOriginals`, `BalanceOriginalsBoth`): the count of the copies that stay already keeps the level, and the plan's order, a user copy before an Atoll one, keeps a user copy `ok`, never `surplus`, so that the retire refuses it; the hand-over is what lets a user copy go with `--move-originals`, its absence leaving the copy where it is.
- The source looked at again once the part is written changes nothing taken out (`BalanceEdit`, `BalanceEdits`, `BalanceMixed`): the commit records the version the part holds, and an edit of the source after its read is a change the next look of that copy finds. It matters for a read torn by a write while it runs, which the model's one-step part does not show (`Replication.tla`'s).
- The session's check of every disk as a run opens changes nothing taken out (`BalanceLive`): a run that plans with an old check, a disk since read-only, has its copy stop at the write, and the pass's check, which comes in time, ends the loop; the check makes a run plan with what it found, as the code does.
- A user's file written directly at the file's name on a filesystem of the physical disk the balance's target is on, between the plan and the placement, would leave two copies on one physical disk until the next plan marks one surplus: the plan reads the catalog, which does not know that file yet. The model's user writes no such file (`Create` keeps off `Shared`); R-06 is about where Atoll places a copy.

### How sensitive it is

Each mechanism taken out, in a copy of the module not kept: the plan's look at the physical disks, `Inv13_OnePhysical` in `BalanceShared` (191 states); the retire's count of the copies that stay, `NoBelowLevel` in `BalanceWorld` (7,461), `BalanceEdit` (472) and `BalanceMixed` (27,269); its look at them, the same in `BalanceEdit` (490), `BalanceWorld` (7,410) and `BalanceMixed` (27,346); the look at the old copy right before its move, `NoOnlyVersionMoved` in `BalanceEdit` (474) and `BalanceEdits` (747); `min_free` checked as the part is written, `MinFree` in `BalanceEnospc` (34); the plan's room past `min_free` with it, the same in `BalanceFull` (7); the part placed without replacing anything, `EditKept` in `BalanceCreate` (155) and `BalanceMixed` (1,519); the marks sparing a pending copy whose job is running (flaw 2), `EventuallySettled` in `BalanceLive` (6,288), the reprise undoing a copy already at its name, which a pass then records as a user's file never verified, and no run can retire the old copy. Each instance reaches what it is there for, each shown by an invariant that fails, added in a copy: an old copy about to go to the trash (`BalanceMove`, `BalanceShared`, `BalanceOriginals`), a run with no copy to make after a stop once a copy was made (`BalanceMove`), a misplaced copy with no target (`BalanceFull`, `BalanceShared`), a part written where the room is gone, ENOSPC (`BalanceEnospc`), a copy stopped on a target mounted read-only (`BalanceEnospc`, `BalanceWorld`), the copy made the user copy (`BalanceOriginals`, `BalanceOriginalsBoth`), an edit taken in and the balance's new copy refreshed (`BalanceEdit`), a rename taken in during a balance (`BalanceRename`), and a user's file at the target's name, a conflict (`BalanceCreate`).

### Modelling mistakes, found by TLC and fixed

- `NoOnlyVersionMoved` asked that no version a balance sends to the trash be the only one: a user's edit of the new copy after the retire's look makes the old copy's version superseded, not lost; the invariant asks it of the version the user wrote last.
- The marks canceled a pending copy beyond the level while its job's operation was planned: the reprise then undid a copy already at its name, and a pass recorded it as a user's file never verified, which no retire takes for a copy that stays. §13.4 never cancels such a copy (flaw 2's remedy).
- The liveness's fairness, one condition for every step of a pass, let the marks stand in for the check: each family of steps has its own now.

### What it leaves out

A second file, so that room is counted in copies, and `colocate` and `balance.equalize`; domains and `spread_mode` (the vectors of `plan.json` and the solver's property tests); the hand-over of a user copy no copy stands in for, with `--move-originals`; `first_seen`, the copies' times and their precision (`Scrub.tla`'s); the trigger, the schedule and the budget, as a run that may start at any time and stop at any step; the guards, as a pass that does not run (`Guards.tla`'s); the pipeline's own faults and a read torn by a write (`Replication.tla`'s); a conflict's resolution (`Conflicts.tla`'s); the trash's purges (`Trash.tla`'s); `missing_grace`, a copy on a disk missing counting for good; and a user's file on a filesystem of the target's physical disk (Observations, above).

## The catalog rebuild: Rebuild.tla

A model of its own, `Rebuild.tla` with `MCRebuild.tla` (2026-11-27), of `atoll catalog rebuild --yes` (§9, §25) and of what the rebuilt catalog decides at its first passes, with §10's checks, the create of §14.2, the moves of §17.1, the reprise of §17.2, the deletions of §15.5, the guards' answers of §15.6, the conflicts of §15.7, the SSD cache's transfer of §19 and Atoll's own view's holds of §21.2: the sixth of the maintainer's models by risk (TODO.md). One file, at its path on two or three disks; disk 1 is maybe the SSD cache, where a new file written through the view lands. For each disk: whether its check confirms it, a disk missing and one whose marker or identity is not its own being alike to every check (§10); whether it is mounted read-only; the version at the file's path and its change time; the entries of its trash, each an operation's folder of one file, and whether its retention has passed; and the parts of its `tmp/`, each named by an operation and made by one catalog. The catalogs: the one at the catalog's path and each one a rebuild set aside, each with the file's version and state, whether its deletion is being sent on, each copy's row, the operation planned, the ids of those over, the entries of the trash it knows and why each went there, what the view holds open (`view_ops` `open`), the modification the guard holds and its reject, and where its ids start. Atoll's daemon is one process under the catalog's lock:

- a pass: a copy it does not know adopted, another version a conflict; a copy changed a modification, the others stale, two changed apart a conflict, the guard maybe holding it, a reject making it a conflict (reject-kept); a copy that held the file's version gone, a deletion, sent to the trash from the copies known to have held it and the stale ones; a stale copy gone, its row alone;
- the operations, journaled before they run, the id the catalog's next: a copy made through a part of `tmp/`, made with no name it would replace, then placed at the file's path, never replacing anything, then committed; a move to the trash, the copy looked at right before it, never replacing an entry; the transfer of the cache's copy once every data disk holds the file, journaled only while the view holds it not open, a move to the trash then a purge at once; the purge of an entry its retention passed; the cleaning of the parts that operations over name;
- the reprise of what is planned, from what the disk holds (§14.2, §17.2).

`atoll catalog rebuild --yes` holds the catalog's lock from its start to its end, so that the daemon waits for it: its new catalog's operations started past the highest id the trash and `tmp/` of each disk its check confirms name; each such disk's trash listed again, each entry of the reason `unknown`; each such disk read whole, a row for each copy; the copies by hash, one version the file `ok`, two a conflict; the swap, the old catalog kept. Atoll's own view (`atoll mount`) opens the catalog once, as it starts, presents from it, and journals in it what it holds open, never taking the lock; since flaw 35's remedy, a rebuild and the view exclude each other, a rebuild starting only while no view runs and a view only while no rebuild does. A user edits a copy in place or deletes it directly on a data disk, or a program opens the file through the view, writes it and closes it; the user may reject a modification the guard holds; the world loses a disk and brings it back, mounts one read-only, and lets an entry's retention pass; Atoll's process may be killed between any two steps, a rebuild's included; the view may be stopped and started again.

| Invariant | Checks |
|---|---|
| `NoUserDataLost` | invariants 1 and 9, §15.7, §21.2: what a user or a program last wrote on a copy, directly or through the view, and every version a reject kept, is at the file's path on some disk: never overwritten, sent to the trash or lost by Atoll, whatever a rebuild lost of what the catalog knew; a disk missing keeps what it holds |
| `RebuiltConflict` | §9, §15.7: a rebuild makes a file whose copies hold two versions a conflict, every version kept |
| `MissingNotEmpty` | invariant 4: a pass decides nothing from a disk its check does not confirm |
| `PartsKept` | §9, §14.2: what an operation of one catalog left in `tmp/` is never cleaned by another, on a disk whose names that one's ids started past |
| `OldKept` | §9: every catalog a rebuild set aside is kept |
| `EventuallySettled` | liveness: once the user and the world are done, the pool settles: nothing planned or under way, the file in conflict, held by the guard, gone, or in sync on every data disk that can be written, the cache's copy transferred |

Measured on 2026-11-27 with `test/tla/run.sh`, 2 workers, once flaw 35's remedy was what every instance does; before it, its instance found it in 1,904 states, and `RebuildViewData` took 78,189.

| Instance | Expects | What it explores | States | Time |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RebuildWorld` | pass | two disks in sync, an entry in disk 2's trash: a user editing or deleting a copy directly, twice in all; a disk lost or mounted read-only, or an entry's retention passing, twice in all; a rebuild among the passes; Atoll killed once, a rebuild's step or an operation's | 1,814,442 | 23 s |
| `RebuildGuard` | pass | a modification the guard holds, the user rejecting it or not, a rebuild before the next pass or after it | 93 | 1 s |
| `RebuildThree` | pass | three disks in sync, an entry in disk 3's trash: a user editing disk 1's or 2's copy directly once; disk 2 or 3 lost, twice in all; a rebuild; Atoll killed once | 619,372 | 10 s |
| `RebuildCache` | pass | a new file on the SSD cache and two data disks: the copies made, the transfer; a disk lost or mounted read-only, twice in all; a rebuild among them; Atoll killed once | 140,645 | 4 s |
| `RebuildViewData` | pass | no cache: a program writing the file through the view, twice, the view maybe stopped and started again; disk 2 lost once; a rebuild; Atoll killed once | 20,604 | 2 s |
| `RebuildLive` | pass | liveness: `RebuildWorld`'s pool, a user editing once, a disk lost once, Atoll killed once: the pool settles | 8,337 | 2 s |
| `RebuildCacheLive` | pass | liveness: `RebuildCache`'s pool, a disk lost once, Atoll killed once: the file reaches the data disks, and the cache's copy goes | 16,873 | 2 s |
| `FlawRebuildViewHeld` | pass | flaw 35, fixed on 2026-11-27 (rebuild-view): a new file on the SSD cache, a program holding it open through the view and writing it, the view maybe stopped and started again; a rebuild, which runs only while the view does not, and the view only while no rebuild does: the transfer never moves the file under the program | 1,832 | 1 s |

`RebuildLive` and `RebuildCacheLive` check `EventuallySettled` under `LiveSpec`: the daemon's steps and a rebuild's once started weakly fair, the view closing what it opened, and a disk coming back.

### Flaw 35: a catalog rebuild while Atoll's own view runs loses what the view holds open, and the transfer purges a file under the program writing it

Found on 2026-11-27 by `NoUserDataLost`, and fixed that day with the remedy the maintainer chose, (a) `rebuild-view` (below, The remedy of flaw 35).

§21.2, Held open: a file the view opens to write keeps its row `open` while the view holds it, "No pass takes in a row `open`, and the SSD cache's transfer never moves a copy one touches (§19)". §9 makes the rebuilt catalog from what the disks hold, and §9 and §25 ask nothing of the view: a rebuild holds the catalog's lock, which the daemon's passes wait for, while Atoll's own view, a process of its own, opens the catalog once as it starts (`cmd/atoll/mount.go`, `readonly.Run`), presents from it, and journals in it what it holds open, without the lock. So the new catalog knows no `view_ops`: what the view held open at the swap is gone from it, and what the view holds open after goes to the old catalog, now `<catalog>.old-<UTC time>`, which its handle still leads to. The daemon's next pass, on the new catalog, sees no hold: the transfer copies the file to the data disks, moves the cache's copy to the trash and purges it at once while the program holds it open, and what the program writes after goes into a file no name holds, lost when it closes it, as flaw 10 was. So goes every file the view opens to write until it is started again. The view also goes on presenting from the old catalog: a copy the new one made since is not presented, and once the cache's copy is purged, the file is not found through the view.

- `FlawRebuildViewHeld` (1,904 states before the remedy): the view opens the cache's copy to write, holding it in the catalog it opened; the copies are made on the data disk; a rebuild makes its catalog from the disks and swaps it in; the transfer, which reads no hold there, moves the cache's copy to the trash under the program, and the program's next write lands there, purged at once.

In the code: `TestFlawARebuildKeepsWhatTheViewHoldsOpen` (build tag `flaws`, then `cmd/atoll/flaws_test.go`, now `TestARebuildKeepsWhatTheViewHoldsOpen` of `cmd/atoll/rebuildview_test.go`), seen failing on 2026-11-27: a pool of three disks, t1 the SSD cache, Atoll's own view as `atoll mount` serves it, over the catalog it opens once; a new file written through it and held open; `atoll catalog rebuild --yes`; `atoll daemon --until-idle`: the file is replicated to t2 and t3, and transferred, the cache's copy gone while the view holds it; the program's later write is in no copy and in no trash. The same without the rebuild keeps the file on the cache while the view holds it, and the later write reaches the library (a control, run once and not kept). It passes once the flaw is fixed, whatever the remedy, the rebuild refused or not. No black-box scenario: the runner has no FUSE to drive the view.

Remedies proposed, each an entry of `Fixes` then, checked by TLC on 2026-11-27 against every invariant, in every instance of the module, in copies not kept; the maintainer chose (a) that day, and set (b) aside:

- (a) `rebuild-view`: a rebuild and Atoll's own view exclude each other: the rebuild refuses while the view runs, and the view does not start while a rebuild runs, as each takes a lock the other holds (the view, say, one of its own for its whole life, which the rebuild takes from its start to its end). It holds in every instance (`FlawRebuildViewHeld` 1,832 states, `RebuildViewData` 20,604, the others as without it). TLC found its first design, the view's check at the rebuild's start alone, missing a view started during the rebuild, which opened the catalog the swap set aside (`NoUserDataLost` in `FlawRebuildViewHeld`, 1,604 states). What it costs: `atoll mount` stopped for a rebuild, then started again, the view gone meanwhile.
- (b) `view-follows`: the view journals in the catalog at the catalog's path, opening it again once a rebuild swapped it, and the rebuild carries what the view holds open into its new catalog with the swap; the view's journal writes and the swap must not interleave, which a lock gives (the view's for each write, the rebuild's around reading the holds and the exchange). It holds in every instance (`FlawRebuildViewHeld` 7,676 states, the others as without it). What it costs: a lock and a reopen in the view, and the holds carried over; the view presents from the new catalog at once.
- `rebuild-holds`, checked and holding not: the rebuild carries what the view holds open into its new catalog, the view staying on the old one: what the view opens after the swap goes there (`NoUserDataLost` in `FlawRebuildViewHeld`, 2,573 states): it is no remedy alone. It is gone from the model, with (b).

### The remedy of flaw 35

`rebuild-view` (the maintainer, 2026-11-27). In the code:

- **The lock** (`cmd/atoll/viewlock.go`, `fsx.LockShared`): the folder of the catalog, `flock`ed, shared by each `atoll mount` for as long as it runs, taken before it opens the catalog (`openView`), and exclusive to `atoll catalog rebuild --yes` from its start to its end (`lockForRebuild`), before it takes the catalog's own lock, the folder made first when it is not there. Neither side waits: a rebuild while a view runs, or another rebuild, exits with 1, changing nothing, and says to stop `atoll mount` for the rebuild; a view while a rebuild runs exits with 1 before it opens the catalog. The kernel lets go of either when its process ends, however it ends. It is the folder's lock, not the catalog's, which every command and pass of the daemon takes and holds for hours at times: a view that waited for it would start only between two passes.
- **How each knows the other runs**: by that lock alone, which exists already as a kind, `flock`, the catalog's own being one; no new file, no new key, no new exit code. A view and a rebuild of two pools whose catalogs share a folder exclude each other too.

No contract changes: the lock is one more of §25's, on a folder that holds the catalog already; the exit code is 1, which both commands give for what they cannot do. No black-box scenario reaches it: the runner holds no lock across its steps, and has no FUSE to keep a view running.

What it costs: `atoll mount` stopped for the time of a rebuild, an administrator's rare command, and started again after it; the library is not presented meanwhile, as when the view is stopped for any other reason.

The tests: `cmd/atoll/rebuildview_test.go`: `TestARebuildKeepsWhatTheViewHoldsOpen`, the flaw test made regular, seen failing before the fix; `TestARebuildIsRefusedWhileTheViewRuns` (exit 1, no catalog made beside the old one, the old one where it was, the daemon's pass going on meanwhile, the rebuild going once the view is over); `TestTheViewDoesNotStartWhileARebuildRuns` (`atoll mount` exit 1, and a second rebuild refused); `TestALockHolderKilledLetsTheOtherGo` (a view, and a rebuild, killed as by kill -9 in a process of their own, leaving no lock behind); and on the loop devices, in qemu on both kernels, `TestARebuildRefusedWhileTheViewRuns`, `atoll mount` serving the view with FUSE, the rebuild refused until SIGTERM stops it. In the model, the remedy is what every instance does, `FixNames` and every `Fixes` empty, and the flaw's instance expects `pass`. For a user: a rebuild never pulls the catalog from under Atoll's own view, which keeps what it holds open.

### Observations

- A disk the rebuild's start finds missing, or whose marker or identity is not its own, and that is back for its listing and its scan, has its trash listed and its copies read, but the names of its trash and `tmp/` do not count where the new catalog's ids start, as the code's warning says ("a new one may meet an old one there, which costs it one io-error"). An operation that meets an old part there is undone at once, and the cleaning of `tmp/` then removes that part, which the undone operation names (`PartsKept` asked of every disk: `RebuildWorld` 478,887 states, `RebuildThree` 337,734, `RebuildViewData` 72,385); a move that meets an old entry is undone, never replacing it. Nothing of a user's is lost (`NoUserDataLost` holds): a part is never a file's last copy. §9's "so that a new one never meets an old one's folder or part" was untrue for such a disk: §9 says so now, a limit, as the maintainer chose on 2026-11-27, with no change to the code.
- §9's paragraph on the rebuild ended "What the disks' trash holds is left on them, and the new catalog does not list it", after saying that the entries of each disk's trash are listed again: a sentence left from before the listing (DONE.md, "A catalog rebuild lists the disks' trash again"), which §9 says otherwise now, as the maintainer chose on 2026-11-27.
- The trash of a disk missing at the rebuild stays unlisted, so never purged, until a rebuild runs again once it is back, as §9 says.
- A reject of the guard is lost with the events, and the copies by hash keep what it kept (`RebuildGuard`), as `daemon-modify-guard-reject-rebuild.json` shows.

### How sensitive it is

Each mechanism taken out, in a copy of the module not kept: `rebuild-view`'s rebuild refused while a view runs, `NoUserDataLost` in `FlawRebuildViewHeld` (1,901 states); its view not started while a rebuild runs, the same (1,599); the copies by hash, the rebuild taking the oldest version for the file's, `NoUserDataLost` in `RebuildWorld` (52,728 states) and `RebuildGuard` (87), `RebuiltConflict` before it (13,231 and 64); the operations started past what the disks name, started at 1, `PartsKept` in `RebuildWorld` (1,512,468); a pass reading a disk its check does not confirm as empty, `MissingNotEmpty` in `RebuildWorld` (89). Each instance reaches what it is there for, each shown by an invariant that fails, added in a copy, in `RebuildWorld`: an operation planned that the old catalog kept (86,670 states), a conflict the rebuild made (17,539), a copy of a disk missing at the rebuild adopted after it (43,340), a part of the old catalog left in `tmp/` (120,389), an entry listed again purged once its retention passed (44,709), a stale copy sent to the trash after the rebuild (81,501), a deletion sent on (79,459), a read-only disk read by the rebuild (23,014).

### Modelling mistakes, found by TLC and fixed

- A deletion of a stale copy was sent on to the copy holding the file's version: §15.5 sends a deletion only to the copies known to have held what went.
- The reprise of a copy's create took a part still in `tmp/` for placed: the scope of a quantifier.
- `PartsKept` was asked of a disk missing at the rebuild's start: the observation above.

### What it leaves out

Other files, folders, renames and names (`Nested.tla`'s, `ViewNames.tla`'s, `Collisions.tla`'s); the link guard's `links/`, whose ids a new catalog starts past as it starts its operations; a trash entry that is not an operation's folder of one file; the purge for room, which never takes an entry a rebuild listed again (`Trash.tla`'s); the guards but as a modification held or rejected (`Guards.tla`'s); the steps of the pipeline (`Replication.tla`'s); `first_seen` and the original's choice; the rebuild's own renames, a stop between them leaving the old catalog or the new one at its path and the other kept beside it (the tests of `catalog.SetAside` and `catalog.KeepLeftover`); the view's other operations, renames, deletions, parts and moves, whose rows a rebuild loses too; and a program writing through the view while a user writes another copy directly.

## Keeping the model and specs.md in step

`fingerprints.json` lists each section of `specs.md` the model formalizes, with the SHA-256 of its text: from its heading to the next heading of the same level or a higher one, so a section holds its subsections; CRLF read as LF, each line without its trailing spaces and tabs, headings inside fenced code blocks not counting, blank lines at the end dropped, each line ended by LF (its `normalisation` says so in full). `go test ./conformance/models` fails when a section no longer has that text, and says to reread against it the modules that cite it, among the `models` listed there, update them or confirm them unchanged, and put the new fingerprint in the same commit (`CLAUDE.md`, "Rust-rewrite readiness"). It also fails when no module cites a section it pins. Each section pinned says so in a sentence that points here.
